| 3.12 | 2026-10-12 | **A test read the developer's keychain; fixed (`9ec986ec`).** Found by the review of the prep commits: four tests in `crates/openalpaca_llm/src/keys/credential_discovery/tests.rs` called the public discovery functions, which read `$HOME/.claude/.credentials.json` and `$HOME/.codex/auth.json` and, on macOS with the first missing, ran `security find-generic-password -s 'Claude Code-credentials' -w`. On this machine that file does not exist (Claude Code keeps its credentials in the keychain), so the fallback ran on **every** macOS run of `cargo test -p openalpaca_llm`, isolated or not — the CI-set gates of rev 3.6, the rehearsal of rev 3.11 and every earlier run in this repository (corrected the same day: the row first said §5.2's scratch `HOME` caused it); a run with the real `HOME` also read `~/.codex/auth.json`, which exists here. The tests asserted nothing about the result (`let _ = result`), so whether `security` returned the item was not observed. Nothing was written or sent; the archives hold no token text (checked). - "Eligible" means at least 7 days old on the day the branch is cut (rev 2.1 §5.1 item 5, one rule for both ecosystems): a crates.io `created_at` or npm `time` date, UTC, at or before cut − 7 days. On 2026-10-10 that is 2026-10-03.

## 0. Summary

**Rev 3.13 — read first.** §0–§2 are dated records of 2026-10-10 with later corrections inline; where they differ from §4–§5, §5.3 and the revision log from rev 3.9 on are authoritative, and a line marked *superseded* states an answer a later revision replaced (SF-19). The 2026-10-17 review (`tasks/deps-upgrade/reports/2026-10-17-plan-review.md`, at `3598a6fb`) found three upstream facts that arrived after rev 3.12 and that nothing in the pipeline noticed: **Tauri's 2026-10-14 patch train** (tauri 2.12.4, tauri-build 2.7.3, tauri-plugin-dialog 2.8.3 and the internals on crates.io; `@tauri-apps/api` 2.12.4, `plugin-dialog` 2.8.3, `cli` 2.12.3 on npm, published 18:47–19:53Z), none of it eligible at a 2026-10-17T20:08Z cut, which P31a's targeted update would nonetheless take (MF-1); **rustls 0.23.46** (2026-10-14T09:54:15Z), which fixes RUSTSEC-2026-0346…-0349 and is eligible from 2026-10-21T09:54:15Z (MF-3); and **Rust 1.99.1** (channel manifest 2026-10-15T17:39:23Z, GitHub release 2026-10-16T04:47:44Z), which fixes an x86_64 miscompilation (SF-8). 1. **Nothing measured loses data, and the measurement is now on this tree.** The formats that move — AES-GCM secrets, SHA-256 names, the SQLite file, TOML and YAML — were differential-tested in September [M-sep]; today the whole wave compiles and passes the current suite on `7295f91b` [M-today]. And the archived E6/E8 tooling (replay driver, comparators, SQLite probe) **cannot be used as gates**: the replay is incomplete and never fails, the comparators write files and differ in source, the probe lacks a module and embeds schema 45 / cache 3 [M-today: reconcile R12–R14]. §6 rebuilds them. *(Rev 3.13, F-15: P05 landed as `afb1da60` and P14 as `05541f1b`, both on 2026-10-11 (rev 3.5), and the E6 comparators were rebuilt as `df477c67`; E8's SQLite probe is still to be built, §6.)*

The Tauri CLI cannot see the JS packages under Bun, CI compiles nothing under `src-tauri`, and the Rust requirements were broad, so P31a — right after P03 — moves both locks to the 2.12 pair, pins `tauri-plugin-dialog` exactly to its JS twin (rev 3.13: the newest version both registries carry that is eligible at the cut instant — whether that is the 2026-10-10 or the 2026-10-14 release, D1 decides; MF-1; rev 3.15: D1 (a) takes the 2026-10-14 family, `=2.8.3` beside tauri 2.12.4) and narrows `tauri`/`tauri-build` to the minor; P04 is then a plain refresh with no Tauri hold. Rev 3.1–3.8's ten-crate hold at P04 (measured: the four-crate form does not compile, the ten-crate form does [M-today, rev 3.1]) existed only so P04 could stay lock-only while the JS half waited for Phase J — a sequencing convenience the owner's rule of 2026-10-12 removes. P31a names what 2.12 brings: windows 0.62 drops Windows 7; wry 0.57 / tao 0.37; the GHSA-w28w-mhc8-qvjv fix (`tauri <= 2.11.5` affected).
4. **Part II is separate, reviewable work with no measured promise.** The shell can leave `openalpaca_storage` (≈1,900 lines moved, 7 crates leave, `cargo tree` says so) and gain a HostBridge, a compatibility contract and honest build provenance; every RAM/startup/compile claim is **[NE]** until the §7.1 baseline exists, and dependency comparisons are always old-vs-new on the same architecture.

## 1. Inventory

The "commit" column names the §5 (P) or §7 (Q) commit that carries the change. "Newest eligible" is dated ≤ 2026-10-03; a later cut widens it (DU-23). Effort/risk carry rev 2.1's scale unless a note changes them. *Rev 3.13 (SF-19):* this section is a dated record of 2026-10-10, not the list of targets — the targets are the newest releases eligible at the cut instant (DU-23), and §5.3 and the revision log from rev 3.9 on are authoritative where a row here differs; a row marked *superseded* names the revision that replaced it. *Rev 3.15 (D1 (a)):* the cut-day versions are the compatibility report's §2 (`reports/2026-10-17-cut-compatibility.md`), which this section does not repeat; every row it supersedes is listed here and stays as written, a record of 2026-10-10 — §1.1 Rust (1.99.0 at P03, 1.99.1 at P03b, D3 (a)); §1.2 sha2 (0.11.1), keyring (keyring-core 1.0.1), zip (9.0.3), toml (1.1.8), opener (0.9.0, P27a), serde_yaml → serde-saphyr (2.0.0 eligible, D4 pending), rustls (0.23.46), serde_json (1.0.154), signal-hook (0.4.5), tauri, Tauri's internals, tauri-build and tauri-plugin-dialog (the 2026-10-14 family), tokio (1.53.3), tokio-util (0.7.20), toml_edit (0.25.17), uuid (1.28.0), the transitive movers (aws-lc-rs 1.18.2 / aws-lc-sys 0.46.0); §1.3 react and react-dom (19.3.1), @tanstack/react-query (5.104.2), dompurify (3.4.17), marked (18.1.1), @testing-library/user-event (14.6.8), the three `@tauri-apps/*` packages (2.12.4 / 2.8.3 / 2.12.3), the root `bun.lock` (v2, 219 entries), vitest (5.0.4), Bun (1.4.3); §1.6 whole — each collision there is resolved at the instant, and what the cut still refuses is §3.1's last row and DU-23's rev 3.15 table.

## 2. Findings refreshed by area

Only what changed or was corrected since rev 2.1. A September finding that still stands is referenced (`plan.md:NNN`), not repeated. *Rev 3.13 (SF-19):* this section is a dated record — 2026-10-10's findings with later corrections inline; §5.3 and the revision log from rev 3.9 on are authoritative where they differ, and a line marked *superseded* names the revision that replaced it. Where a rev 2.1 statement is now simply false, the reconcile ledger's Part 4 (`SP/reports/reconcile.md`) lists it; the ones that matter are folded in below.

### 2.1 Data safety

A claim is load-bearing here when being wrong about it could lose or orphan data or secrets, silently change an on-disk or wire format, or change what people see.

**Stored secrets: AES-GCM (`llm.toml` `secret_encrypted`) and the installed-process-key contract.**
- The construction and byte format are unchanged since September: AES-256-GCM, random 12-byte nonce prefixed, 16-byte tag, no AAD, `aes256:` + base64 STANDARD, the raw 32-byte key from 64 hex [C: `crates/openalpaca_llm/src/keys/key_encryption/mod.rs` — `PREFIX` `:11`, `BASE64` `:7`, `from_hex` `:140`, `ensure_at` `:161`, `encrypt` `:243-260`, `decrypt` `:263-285`]. The two commits since `5e5ac317` (F3: `0047cf90`, `33feb142`) touch only `:6-101`, `:110-118`, `:125-138` [M-today: `git diff` hunk ranges]. So September's cross-decryption evidence (10,000 plaintexts × 20 keys both ways; ring 0.17.14 oracle, 18,030 checks) still describes this code [M-sep], and the archived AES hunks apply unchanged [M-today: `git apply --check --include='…/key_encryption/*' secrets.diff` exits 0; applied in the re-cut].
- **The precedence prose in rev 2.1 (`plan.md:208`, "`OPENALPACA_MASTER_KEY` overrides it") is wrong for the daemon.** At boot the daemon reads or ensures `state/.master_key`, installs it as `openalpaca_llm`'s process-local key and **removes** `OPENALPACA_MASTER_KEY` from its environment, inherited or not [C: `apps/openalpacad/src/main.rs:155-170`, the call at `:160`; `key_encryption/mod.rs:64-75` `install_process_key`, `:86-92` `install_process_key_and_clear_env`]; the library reads the installed key first, then the environment, then the file [C: `:130-138` `from_env_opt`]; the CLI uses the library order through `load_or_generate_at` / `load_at` [C: `:99`, `:116`]. The archived migration does not undo this (its hunks are elsewhere).
- **P11's gate therefore widens** (R22): after the bump run the 13 `keys::key_encryption::tests::*`, `tests/master_key_leaves_the_environment.rs:41` (`a_child_spawned_after_the_install_sees_no_master_key_and_the_installed_key_still_decrypts`), `load_at_never_mints_a_master_key` (`key_encryption/tests.rs:213`) and the CLI state-key tests (`apps/openalpaca/src/commands/ai_config_helpers/tests.rs:36-68` [A08]). In today's re-cut all 13 unit tests and the integration test ran `ok` [M-today: `c03-test-recut.log:3440-3489` (the 13, interleaved with the rest of the crate's tests) and `:3726-3729` (the integration test)].
- **P05's fixture test (`a_secret_written_by_the_aes_gcm_0_10_build_still_decrypts`) does not exist in the tree**, so no test in today's run pinned the on-disk byte format across the bump [M-today: grep]. P05 stays Required and lands before P11. *(Rev 3.13, F-15: landed as `afb1da60` on 2026-10-11 — `crates/openalpaca_llm/src/keys/key_encryption/tests.rs:252` — with the partition-key and `sha256_hex` literals below.)*
- The keychain (keyring 3 → 4): unchanged since September — `services/llm.rs`, `settings_service.rs`, `llm_config/migration.rs`, `credential_discovery/`, `secret_store/` have 0 commits since `5e5ac317` [M-today: `git log`], so the source reading of rev 2.1 §2.1 (same service/account on macOS, same target on Windows, subset matching on Linux; deletes only on explicit replace/remove) still addresses this code [D]. Not measured on a real keychain [NE]; DU-10 unchanged. **keyring never reaches the shell** (0 occurrences in `openalpaca_gui`'s normal graph [M-today: architect]) — G-GUI's P12 trigger is dropped.

**Hashes that name files (sha2 0.11): eleven sites, and the project-UUID partition keys.**
- The spelling is zero-padded lowercase `{b:02x}` and the owner key is the first 32 hex of SHA-256 — unchanged [C: `memory_files/mod.rs:140-148` `sha256_hex`, `:150-155` `owner_key`; `content_io.rs:15-19`]; `u_example_alice` → `32acea7eb739b08afd959db91e46d450` still holds [M-today: Python `hashlib`].
- **The set of names derived from SHA-256 grew since September** [C]: a project **memory partition** is keyed `proj-<owner-key>-<16 hex of sha256(project UUID)>` (`memory_files/mod.rs:163-167`, `:175-177`) and its snapshot binds by id with `root` not compared (`snapshot.rs:274-285`); a project **folder** is `<dashed canonical path>[-<8 hex of sha256(path)>]` (`store/projects.rs`, pinned by literals at `projects/tests.rs:99-107`, `:110-116`, `:126-132`: `df04c200`, `693a1a8d`, `559b2d05`, `9e472a79`, `f7b36aaa` — all five re-derived by `hashlib` today [M-today]); a result file's **pointer row** stores the real file's `sha256` (`file_edit_tests.rs:426`). "A spelling change would orphan every partition" is still true and now also renames every project folder.
- **The one literal that is missing**: `memory_files/tests.rs:74-96` derives the UUID partition key through the same `sha256_hex` helper (`:87-89`). P05 adds `project_key("7d1c2b3a-4e5f-4a6b-8c7d-9e0f1a2b3c4d")` = **`21a5e10e73692d40`** [M-today: `hashlib`] and a `sha256_hex(b"abc")` known answer (`ba7816bf8f01cfea414140de5dae2223b00361a396177a9cb410ff61f20015ad`). It does **not** add folder-suffix pins (they exist; A09 corrects A08).
- **Eleven `format!("{:x}", <digest>)` sites, not ten** [M-today: `rg` over the tree]: `crates/openalpaca_storage/src/content_io.rs:18`; `crates/openalpaca_core/src/tools/builtins/update_persona/common.rs:8`; `crates/openalpaca_core/tests/common/eval.rs:445` (rev 2.1 said `:363`); `apps/openalpacad/src/hot_reload.rs:43, :244, :279, :317`; `apps/openalpacad/src/managers/mcp.rs:2435`; and three new test sites — `crates/openalpaca_core/src/tools/builtins/memory_tools/tests.rs:1548` and `:3228` [A08: E0277 reproduced under sha2 0.11] and `crates/openalpaca_core/src/tools/builtins/file_edit_tests.rs:426` (Stage 2; its pre-fix failure is **inferred** from the identical expression — the re-cut's prefix check failed in storage and llm first and never reached core, and the site was rewritten by shape before the check that passed [M-today]). The two retired storage-test hunks (`database/tests.rs:1097`, `store/tests.rs:440`) are gone. `crates/openalpaca_llm/src/test_support.rs:156` is a `{:x}` of a `usize` (a chunk length), not a digest — excluded. Digest consumers that need no rewrite but must compile under hybrid-array (`--all-targets` stays): `file_ops.rs:637` (`[..4].iter()`), `memory_index/fixture_tests.rs:43, :49, :282`, `memory_index/bench.rs:335, :498, :630` through `hex(impl AsRef<[u8]>)` at `:204`, `session_log/writer.rs:638, :714`, `memory_files/mod.rs:141`; no `GenericArray`/`generic_array`/`hybrid_array` name appears in our code [M-today]. After the rewrite `grep '{:x}'` finds only `test_support.rs:156` [M-today].
- Lock: `sha2 0.11.0` and `digest 0.11.3` join **beside** `0.10.9`/`0.10.7` (twilight, teloxide and rustls keep 0.10), so `cargo tree -d` gains `sha2`, `digest`, `block-buffer`, `crypto-common`, `cpufeatures` [M-today: lockdiff 03→04a, `c05-tree-d-recut.log`]. Rev 2.1's "lock edges only" for P09/P10 is incomplete.
- `format!("{:?}", digest)` still compiles and changes its output (rev 2.1 skeptic) — no current site does it [M-sep; the grep set is unchanged by the three new sites, which all use `{:x}`].

**The SQLite file: schema 4, cache 6.**
- Rev 2.1 said "`schema_version` stays 45", cited a schema-42 refusal test and probed cache schema 3 with two FTS tables. **All three are false today** [C]: the database is at **schema 4** (`migrations/mod.rs:23-49`; `004_project_files.sql:8-14` adds `file_assets.in_project INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` and rebuilds `idx_file_assets_addr` as `(COALESCE(project_root, ''), in_project, rel_path)`), the one refusal is `current_version > newest` ("newer than this build", `database/mod.rs:132-146`, pinned by `database/tests.rs:68`), and the cache is `SCHEMA_VERSION` **6** with `fts_word`, `fts_tri_head`, `fts_tri` (`memory_index/mod.rs:90`, `:516`).
- What still stands: rusqlite 0.40.2 forces 0 source changes [M-today]; the bundled SQLite moves 3.51.1 → 3.53.2 [D: `libsqlite3-sys-{0.36.0,0.38.2}/sqlite3/sqlite3.h` `SQLITE_VERSION`]; SQLite 3.53 renders REAL → TEXT with 17 digits and no SQL of ours converts a REAL to text (`004_project_files.sql` adds no floating-point conversion [A10]); the compile flags are identical [M-sep]. **The portability measurement itself (files and WAL both ways, FTS integrity) was taken on schema 45 / cache 3 with two FTS tables** and must be re-taken on schema 4 / cache 6 with three (E8, with a rebuilt probe). *Rev 3.13 (F-12):* libsqlite3-sys 0.38.2 (2026-08-08) is still the newest release and bundles 3.53.2, while SQLite 3.53.3 (2026-06-26) and 3.53.4 (2026-07-24) fix problems in 3.53.0–3.53.2 and 3.54.1 exists since 2026-10-16 [Docs: read by the review; the release notes not re-read]. No libsqlite3-sys bundles ≥ 3.53.3 — the blocker; its way out is the next libsqlite3-sys release (§3.1). Vendoring an amalgamation instead is owner decision **D11**, pending (recommended: wait, the blocker named in P06). Those fixes are read against our WAL, FTS5 and planner paths before P06.
- The rollback build: a `d484a56a` binary registers only 1–3 and refuses a schema-4 file as "newer than this build" — an application-schema refusal, not a SQLite one [M-today: `git show d484a56a:…/migrations/mod.rs`; `git diff --stat d484a56a HEAD -- database/mod.rs` empty]. So `<pre-wave>` is a **post-Stage-2 commit**, and both SQLite variants are built from that one source (E7). Tests to retain, not duplicate: `database/tests.rs:1384` (3 → 4), `:1299` (migration 3), `:68` (the refusal), `:923` (reset), `:1142` (1 → working state); `artifacts/tests.rs:2953`, `:3069`, `:3321`, `:3362` (pointer row, same-address upload, `put` never adopts a pointer, user edit records no version).
- **What a copied `~/.openalpaca` contains now** (E8 `plan.md:1467`): also `projects/<name>/{project.json, artifacts/, uploads/, memory/}`, `scratch/<conversation id>/`, `artifacts/kept/`, `uploads/kept/`, and `file_assets` rows whose `storage_path` points **outside** the store at real project files (`in_project = 1`); home snapshots bind the old absolute root (`snapshot.rs:283`) [C]. The doctor's local path opens the **migrating** database (`apps/openalpaca/src/commands/memory.rs:697` → `:1737` `store::open_home_database` → `Database::open`) [C], so "doctor scans locally and read-only" (`plan.md:1470`) is false for the database. E8 is rewritten in §6.

**Config and frontmatter parsing (toml 1.1, toml_edit 0.25.x, serde-saphyr 1.3).**
- The parser code is unchanged: `llm_config/`, `config_codec.rs`, `permission_gate.rs`, `manifest.rs`, `tools/config/mod.rs`, `services/agents.rs`, `bootstrap/config.rs`, `middleware/skill/`, `agent/template/`, `persona_frontmatter.rs`, `types.rs` all have 0 commits since `5e5ac317` [M-today: `git log`]. - **zip 9** (new since September): `ZipFile::name()` returns `ZipResult<Cow<'_, str>>` [D: `zip-9.0.1/src/read/zipfile.rs:83`] where 8.6.0 returned `&str` [D: `zip-8.6.0/src/read.rs:801`], so our `let name = entry.name().to_string();` at `apps/openalpacad/src/extraction.rs:198` (rev 3 wrote `:182`, which is the comment `// Try legacy XML index first` [C]) fails with `E0599`; the one-line fix is `let name = match entry.name() { Ok(n) => n.into_owned(), Err(_) => continue };` (judgement: skip an entry whose name does not decode; only the iWork `.iwa` scan reaches it) [M-today: research probe, exit 101 then exit 0]. Everything else we call compiles unchanged. **Held** (§3): calamine 0.36.1 requires `zip ^8.6` [D: `calamine-0.36.1/Cargo.toml:148-151`], a lock with both resolves two zips (fails P16's one-zip gate) [M-today], and 9.0.x is 0–1 days old. zip-rs/zip2 published three GHSAs on 2026-10-09/10 (GHSA-4v9h-p7m9-rh26, GHSA-vv5m-jxxh-4627 patched 9.0.0; GHSA-4684-h2q9-2vcp patched 9.0.1), all in `extract()` paths we never call (`grep '\.extract(\|ZipStreamReader'` → nothing) [Docs; C]; RustSec has not imported them (`crates/zip/` holds only RUSTSEC-2025-0168) [M-today: GitHub contents API] — P34 pre-records a reasoned `ignore` (§5.3). *Superseded (rev 3.9): zip 9 for our reads, not held. Rev 3.13 (SF-7): RustSec imported the three on 2026-10-16 (advisory-db `64f92cd`, 23:27Z) as RUSTSEC-2026-0367, -0368 and -0369 (GHSA-vv5m-jxxh-4627, GHSA-4v9h-p7m9-rh26, GHSA-4684-h2q9-2vcp), each patched in zip ≥ 9.0.0 by RustSec's record (the GHSA gave 9.0.1 for -4684); calamine's zip 8.6.0 stays in the lock and still matches them, and cargo-deny's `workspace` scope narrows only unmaintained and unsound advisories, not vulnerabilities — so P34's ignore is live from P34 on, not dormant; the ids match only zip < 9 and cannot hide our own 9.x.*
- **opener 0.9.0** (never researched): no public signature change (`diff -r src/`: `lib.rs` edits and a new `browser_command.rs`), our one call `opener::open(target).map_err(|e| e.to_string())` at `routes/files_types.rs:157` compiles unchanged [M-today: probe]. Behaviour: on Linux `xdg-open` of a missing path now errors (our route stages the file first, `files_types.rs:140-145` — *corrected, rev 3.13 (A2-15): only an upload is staged, `stage = origin != Produced` (`routes/files_types.rs:183`); a by-product or a result file opens in place (`:152-157`), so a missing one reaches opener; the Linux Open check stays, deferred under DU-33*); `OpenError::Io` displays the inner error, so the `500 OPEN_FAILED` text changes (`:364`); spawned programs are reaped on an `opener-reaper` thread (not used on macOS — rev 3.13, A2-15); WSL uses PowerShell [Docs: CHANGELOG 0.9.0; D]. P27a, DU-24.

**MCP protocol behaviour (rmcp 0.16.0 → 3.5.0).**
- `crates/openalpaca_mcp/` has 0 commits since `5e5ac317` [M-today], so rev 2.1 §2.1/§2.7's reading stands for the *client* (no rmcp type persisted; the tool input schema byte-identical; annotation hints survive by field name; the handshake is still the legacy `initialize`, no `server/discover` probe [M-sep, re-read in 3.4.1 and 3.5.1: `service/client.rs:676-722`]).
- **The protocol version our `initialize` offers changes again at 3.5.0.** 3.4.1 offers `2025-11-25`; **3.5.0 moved `ProtocolVersion::LATEST` to `2026-07-28`** [D: `rmcp-3.5.0/src/model.rs:177` vs `rmcp-3.4.1/src/model.rs:175`] and added `LATEST_WITH_INITIALIZE = 2025-11-25` (`:202`), `NO_INITIALIZE = 2026-07-28` and `has_initialize()`. `ProtocolVersion::default()` is still `LATEST` (`model.rs:157-159`), `ClientConfig::default()` uses it (`:1439-1447`, `:1198`), `ClientHandler::get_info` returns `ClientConfig::default()` (`handler/client.rs:277-279`), our `NotifyingHandler` has **no `get_info` override** [C: `crates/openalpaca_mcp/src/client.rs:68`] and starts with `handler.serve(transport)` [C: `:665-666`] → `legacy_startup` sends the version unclamped (`service/client.rs:693-705`, `890-915`). **Consequence: under 3.5.x our `initialize` offers `2026-07-28`, a revision whose own spec has no `initialize`.** How real servers answer is [NE]. The re-cut's measured state carries **no override** [M-today]; DU-5's sub-question (d) — pin the offered version through a `get_info` override returning `ClientConfig { protocol_version: ProtocolVersion::LATEST_WITH_INITIALIZE, ..Default::default() }` — becomes a **required decision** for any 3.5.x (DU-25). The `MCP-Protocol-Version` header follows the **server's** negotiated `InitializeResult.protocol_version` (`streamable_http_client.rs:112-129`), not the offer, so the pin does not change the header [D] — rev 2.1's Appendix B #24, header half, answered.
- Unchanged 3.4.1 → 3.5.1: `transport/child_process.rs` (`TokioChildProcess`), `transport/streamable_http_client.rs`, `model/content.rs` (`ContentBlock`) [M-today: `diff -rq src/`]. So the descendant-cleanup question (P19's gate) is neither fixed nor worsened by the version choice, and `stdio.rs:79` still hands a raw `tokio::process::Command` to `TokioChildProcess::new` [C]; Stage 2's `tools::platform::run_bounded` (`platform.rs:81`, `process_group(0)` `:88`, `killpg` `:186`) is a different path and proves nothing about MCP (R16).
- The pre-existing, unchanged items of rev 2.1 §3.3 stand: the cross-origin 307 header replay (`transport/http.rs`, no redirect policy), the reconnect counter reset (`client.rs:325`), `client_info` never sent.
- 3.5.0's own changes that reach a client [D: CHANGELOG; diffs]: `"structuredContent": null` kept as `Some(Value::Null)`; `cacheScope: ""` tolerated; floats decoded through `serde_json::Number` (`Annotations.priority`; `ToolAnnotations` itself untouched, so the constructor rewrites stand); duplicate SEP-2243 headers rejected (server); default ports in the server Origin allowlist. 3.5.1 adds client-side handling for a no-initialize server (`client.rs:921-931`) and auth/server fixes. Neither touches the API the archived hunks use [D: `model.rs:1546`, `:2507`, `:2518`, `:1134`, `:1151`, `:1157`; `model/tool.rs:95`, `:100`, `:125-143`; `model/content.rs:259`, `:280`, `:311` at tag `rmcp-v3.5.0`].

### 2.2 Rust toolchain 1.93.0 → 1.99.0 and the MSRV

- **1.99.0 was released 2026-10-01** (GitHub API `published_at 2026-10-01T12:41:12Z`); no 1.99.1 existed on 2026-10-10 (`static.rust-lang.org/dist/channel-rust-1.99.1.toml` → 404 then — it exists now, below; `src/version`: stable 1.99.0, beta 1.100.0, master 1.101.0); 1.100.0 is due 2026-11-12 [M-today: research]. All of 1.93.0, 1.98.1, 1.99.0 are installed locally [M-today: `rustup toolchain list`]. *Rev 3.13 (SF-8): 1.99.1 now exists* — `channel-rust-1.99.1.toml` last-modified 2026-10-15T17:39:23Z (re-read for this revision), the GitHub release 2026-10-16T04:47:44Z; it is installed on this machine. It fixes a miscompilation of FFI `bool` return values on x86_64 (rust-lang/rust#163911; CI's ubuntu runners are x86_64), the rust-lld libLLVM rpath on macOS (#163947) and `wcslen` on UEFI [Docs: the release, read by the review]. Our own code declares no `extern "C"` function (`git grep 'extern "C"' -- crates apps`: 0 hits at `3598a6fb`); whether a dependency compiled for CI returns `bool` across FFI was not checked [NE]. DU-2 says to take an x.y.1 that appears before the cut; DU-23 makes 1.99.1 eligible only from 2026-10-22T17:39Z (by the manifest) or 2026-10-23T04:47Z (by the GitHub release), and which publication time counts for a toolchain is not stated — owner decision **D3**, pending (§4.2). *Rev 3.15: D3 (a), taken 2026-10-17 — DU-23 governs and the later clock counts, so 1.99.1 is eligible from 2026-10-23T04:47:44Z and moves in P03b (DU-2, §4.2).*
- **The unmodified tree on 1.99.0** [M-today: recut §A]: `cargo check --locked --workspace --all-targets --exclude openalpaca_gui` exit 0, `--locked` accepted and the lock unchanged; **1 rustc warning** (`deprecated`: `Atomic::<u64>::fetch_update` → `try_update`, `crates/openalpaca_core/src/session_log/mod.rs:519`); 1 future-incompat report (`proc-macro-error2 2.0.1`, E0365, the known one). Tests 5,080 / 0 / 16 — identical to the 1.93.0 gate.
- **Clippy, same method on the same tree**: 78 unique `(file, line, lint)` on 1.93.0 → 101 on 1.99.0 (rev 3.3: not 44 → 67, see §0's lint row), **+23 / −0**: `useless_borrows_in_formatting` ×6, `result_large_err` ×5, `drain_collect` ×2, `single_element_loop` ×2, `unnecessary_sort_by` ×2, `for_kv_map`, `question_mark`, `collapsible_match`, `unneeded_wildcard_pattern`, `useless_format` ×1 each, plus the rustc `deprecated` ×1 [M-today: `comm` over `a07`/`a02`]. This replaces rev 2.1's "+17" table (`plan.md:343-356`, measured 1.93 → 1.98.1 on `5e5ac317`): `single_element_loop` and three more `result_large_err` sites are new, and the project's own 78 at `3810d9d8` is the 1.93.0 figure by the same method.
- **rustfmt output is byte-identical** between 1.93.0 and 1.99.0: 1585 `Diff in` hunks in 303 files on both (`cmp`) [M-today]. September's 1456 was the older tree.
- Release-note items checked against this tree, each with the grep that establishes "no effect" [M-today: recut §A table]: legacy integral modules (`(std|core)::(i8…f64)::` 0 hits), `no_mangle` (0), `Pin::new_unchecked` (0), extern statics (no `extern "C" {}` blocks), U+FF9E/U+FF9F in `escape_debug` (0 hits; the approval-surface escape uses its own table), `semicolon_in_expressions_from_non_local_macros` (0 warnings), `#[path]`/`cfg_select` (5 `mod` sites, compile clean), Cargo's incremental-off-under-`CI` (`ci.yml:10` already sets `CARGO_INCREMENTAL: "0"`), an edition-2024 member may override an inherited dependency's `default-features` (an option for P23). `RangeInclusive`'s exhausted-iterator change cannot be grepped for [NE]; LLVM 23 is codegen only [NE].

None of the new names appears in the +23 list, so none fires on this tree [M-today: the count file].

### 2.3 The semver-compatible lock refresh (P04)

- At `7295f91b` on 1.99.0, `cargo update` prints "Locking 139 packages to highest Rust 1.99 compatible versions"; by `(name, version)` sets that is **145 moved, 3 added (`core_detect 1.0.0`, `multiversion_no_op 1.0.0`, `ndk-context 0.1.1`), 9 removed** (the five `unic-*` 0.9.0, `ctor-proc-macro`, `dtor`, `dtor-proc-macro`, `tinyvec_macros`); cargo notes 22 dependencies "behind latest" (the held majors) [M-today: `recut-02-update.log`, `recut-02-lockdiff.txt`]. Re-run on a fresh export at 06:00 UTC on 2026-10-11 (P03's two edits applied) the same command produced a lock **byte-identical** to `recut-lock-02-update.lock` [M-today, rev 3.1: `held-01-update.log`, `held-01-vs-recut02.txt`]. **With P04's ten-crate hold applied the refresh is 103 moved / 2 added (`core_detect`, `multiversion_no_op`) / 1 removed (`tinyvec_macros`), 732 → 737 entries** [M-today, rev 3.1: `held-04-held-vs-head-lockdiff.txt`]. The research group's dry run on a `git archive` export at `rust-version 1.93` printed 128 Updating / 11 Adding / 9 Removing ("Locking 138"); with `1.99` the only extra line is `takecell` [M-today: `research-update-dryrun-export-msrv193/199.log`]. The two counts differ in method and in `rust-version`; both replace September's 80.
- **The data-path watch-list is unchanged except**: `serde_json 1.0.151 → 1.0.152`, `encoding_rs 0.8.35 → 0.8.42` (the legacy-encoding upload note stands), the `toml 1.1.4 → 1.1.8` / `toml_edit 0.25.13 → 0.25.17` edges (tauri-utils), `base64 0.23.1` added as a third `base64` (hyper-util). `libsqlite3-sys`, `rusqlite`, `aes-gcm`/`aes`, `sha2`, `blake3`, `keyring`, `serde`, `chrono`, `unicode-normalization`, `regex*` unchanged [M-today]. *Rev 3.13 (G-07):* the prep lock (`refs/prep/deps-wave-2026-10-12`) adds 52 new major versions of transitive crates against HEAD's, some on data paths — cff-parser 0.2 (via pdf-extract), json-patch 4.2 and jsonptr 0.7 (via tauri-build/-codegen/-utils), typed-path (via zip), atoi_simd and fast-float2 (via calamine); all 70 crates new to that lock carry permissive licences [M: the review's comparison of the two locks]. Each version-moving commit names the new majors it pulls in (§5.2), and P04's watch-list gains cff-parser, typed-path and calamine's number parsers (§5.3).
- Security-relevant moves: `rustls 0.23.43 → 0.23.45` (RUSTSEC-2026-0285 gone), `process-wrap 9.1.0 → 9.1.1` (10.0.1 arrives with rmcp), `reqwest 0.13.5`, `tokio 1.53.2`, `clap 4.6.7`, `uuid 1.28.0`, `libc 0.2.190` [M-today].
- **Every Tauri subcrate moves to a 2026-09-30/10-10 release** — `tauri 2.12.3`, `tauri-build 2.7.2`, `tauri-plugin-dialog 2.8.2`, `tauri-plugin-fs 2.7.0`, `tauri-utils 2.10.1`, `tauri-runtime(-wry) 2.12.1`, `tauri-codegen`/`tauri-macros 2.7.2`, `tauri-plugin 2.7.1`, `wry 0.55.1 → 0.57.0`, `tao 0.35.3 → 0.37.1` — three of them zero days old; the GUI crate was excluded from every re-cut build [M-today]. P04 holds them — **all ten, not the four leaf crates** (§5.3 P04; DU-26; rev 3.1): `tauri 2.11.6` requires `tauri-runtime ^2.11.3`, `tauri-runtime-wry ^2.11.4`, `tauri-macros ^2.6.3`, `tauri-utils ^2.9.3` and `tauri-build ^2.6.3` as **carets** [D: `tauri-2.11.6/Cargo.toml`], and tauri-build 2.6.3 requires `tauri-utils ^2.9.3`, `tauri-codegen ^2.6.3` [D: `tauri-build-2.6.3/Cargo.toml:102-107`] — so `--precise` on the four leaf crates alone (dialog 2.7.3 → fs 2.5.2 → tauri 2.11.6 → tauri-build 2.6.3; each step rc 0) leaves every internal crate at its 2.12-line version (`tauri-runtime(-wry) 2.12.1`, `tauri-macros`/`tauri-codegen 2.7.2`, `tauri-plugin 2.7.1`, `tauri-utils 2.10.1`, wry 0.57.0, tao 0.37.1) with `webview2-com` 0.38.2 + 0.39.1 and the `windows` 0.61 family beside 0.62 (725 → 739 entries), **a state in which `tauri 2.11.6` does not compile**: `cargo check -p openalpaca_gui --locked` fails with 19 errors in `tauri-2.11.6/src/{app.rs, menu/plugin.rs, window/mod.rs}` (`E0599` ×9, `E0277` ×6, `E0308` ×4 — `match` arms over `tauri-runtime 2.12.1`'s types) [M-today, rev 3.1: `held-03*`, `held-05-ggui-check-held4.log`]. The 2.12 line pins its internals with `~` (`tauri-runtime ~2.12.1`, `tauri-utils ~2.10.1`, `tauri-codegen ~2.7.2` [D]), which is what 2.12.0's note "Lock unstable tauri crates to minor versions" did and why the mixing cannot recur from 2.12 on. **The ten-crate hold** — the four, then `tauri-runtime-wry 2.11.4`, `tauri-runtime 2.11.3`, `tauri-macros 2.6.3`, `tauri-codegen 2.6.3`, `tauri-plugin 2.6.3`, `tauri-utils 2.9.3` (in that order; `tauri-utils` last, since every 2.7.x/2.12.x crate requires `~2.10.1` and must be gone first; every step rc 0) — brings wry 0.55.1, tao 0.35.3, webview2-com 0.38.2, the 0.61 `windows` family, brotli 8, cfb 0.7.3, `infer 0.19.0` and the five `unic-*` back with it: **HEAD's Tauri sub-graph with only `tauri` moved**, 45 moved / 8 added / 1 removed against the un-held refresh, and the shell compiles clean on it — `cargo check -p openalpaca_gui --locked` 0 errors, 0 warnings, 13 s with the gui group's warm `target-gui`, the lock unchanged [M-today, rev 3.1: `held-04*`, `held-05-ggui-check-held.log`; `cargo check` only — clippy and fmt were not run on it, P04's G-GUI runs them]. The host-target graph of the workspace minus the shell is **identical** under the held and the un-held lock — `cargo tree --workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui -e normal --prefix none --locked | sort -u`: 509 lines each, diff empty; over **normal, build and dev edges** (`-e normal,build,dev`): 530 lines each, diff empty [M-today, rev 3.1: `held-08-*`, `held-10-*`] — so the hold touches only what the shell compiles on macOS and the re-cut's check and test results carry over (the test suite was not re-run on a held lock; `held-09` is a compile check); under `--target all` the two graphs differ by 17 `windows-*` lines — the 0.61 family beside 0.62, for the Windows target only — which no job but a Windows one compiles (Appendix B #39) [M-today, rev 3.1: `held-06-*`, `held-08-*`].
- A targeted `cargo update -p tauri -p tauri-build -p tauri-plugin-dialog` (requirements narrowed to `"2.12"`, `"2.7"`, `"2.8"`) lands on **tauri 2.12.2**, not 2.12.3 — 2.12.3 differs from 2.12.2 only in requiring `tauri-build ^2.7.2`, `tauri-macros ~2.7.2` and `swift-rs ^1.0.9` (an apple-non-macOS target), and the untargeted `swift-rs 1.0.8` holds it back; `cargo update -p tauri --precise 2.12.3` moves exactly swift-rs and tauri, with or without `--ignore-rust-version` (not an MSRV hold) [M-today: gui §4; crates.io `/dependencies` of both]. *Superseded (rev 3.13, MF-1):* swift-rs 1.0.9 (2026-10-10T10:41:32Z) is eligible from 2026-10-17T10:41Z and no longer holds the targeted update back, and since Tauri's 2026-10-14 train the targeted update must unlock tauri's internals from HEAD's 2.11.x: tauri 2.12.3 requires them by tilde (`tauri-utils ~2.10.1`, `tauri-runtime(-wry) ~2.12.1`, `tauri-macros ~2.7.2`), every 10-14 release satisfies those ranges, so cargo resolves to the 10-14 releases. The review reproduced it offline on an export of `3598a6fb` with step 1b's manifest edits: tauri moved to 2.12.4, and `--precise 2.12.3` then moved only tauri back — tauri-build 2.7.3, tauri-codegen/-macros 2.7.3, tauri-runtime(-wry) 2.12.2 and tauri-utils 2.10.2 stayed (the exact set depends on the local index snapshot; in the verifier's run tauri-plugin and tauri-plugin-fs moved too). That mix — a 2.12.3 core over 10-14 internals — was never released together by Tauri and never compiled under G-GUI. Every 10-14 internal requires `tauri-utils ~2.10.2`, so a step-back to the 2.12.3 set is an ordered cascade with tauri-utils last, the lock re-read after each step, each spelled `name@locked` — measured on the day, never copied from a list — and the wrong order fails ("failed to select a version for the requirement `tauri-utils = \"~2.10.2\"`"). Under D1's recommended cut instant (2026-10-21T19:53Z) the whole train is eligible and no step-back is needed. *Rev 3.15 (D1 (a); the compatibility report's C2):* that instant is taken. From HEAD's lock the targeted update takes the whole family in one command; from a lock already on the 2.12.3 family — the prep tip's — it moves only tauri-plugin-dialog 2.8.3 and tauri-plugin-fs 2.7.1 and leaves tauri 2.12.3 over the 10-10 internals, so `cargo update -p tauri --precise 2.12.4` and `cargo update -p tauri-plugin` follow (P31a). - All eight steps combine with no semantic conflict on `7295f91b` [M-today]: 28 files changed outside the lock (+261 / −234: `Cargo.toml`, `rust-toolchain.toml`, two crate manifests, 11 sha2 sites, the AES module and its tests, 14 rmcp files, 3 serde-saphyr files); the lock +1,446 / −1,050, **732 → 764 packages**, 187 moved / 68 added / 30 removed HEAD → final. Patches: `SP/patches/recut-combined.diff` (183,083 B, lock included), `recut-source-only.diff` (45,876 B), per-step locks `recut-lock-{00-head,01-toolchain,02-update,03-storage,04a-secrets-prefix,04-secrets,05-documents,06a-rmcp-prefix,06-rmcp,07-web,08a-config-prefix,08-config,99-final}.lock` — today's reference results, replacing September's `combined-commits/NN-*.diff`; **they were cut without P04's Tauri hold**, so G-base compares with rev 3.1's `recut-lock-02-update-held.lock` at step 2 and `recut-lock-99-final-held.lock` at the end (§5.1 item 6), and the un-held series is the reference for steps 3–8's own moves.
- Duplicates (`cargo tree -d --workspace -e normal`): 101 top-level entries / 46 names / 84 name@version / 33 multi-version names → **109 / 52 / 93 / 37**. Newly duplicated (13): `sha2`, `digest`, `block-buffer`, `crypto-common`, `cpufeatures`, `aes`, `cipher`, `inout` (the 0.11/0.9 line beside 0.10/0.8), `sha1` (tungstenite 0.30), `tower-http` (reqwest), `alloc-no-stdlib`, `alloc-stdlib`, `brotli-decompressor` (lopdf 0.42). No longer duplicated (9): `infer`, `nix`, `foldhash`, `constant_time_eq`, `thiserror`/`thiserror-impl`, `toml`, `toml_datetime`, `winnow` [M-today: `a06`/`c05`]. Duplicates cost compile time, not correctness (rev 2.1).
- The final `cargo update` notes 22 dependencies "behind latest" before the wave and 12 after the rmcp pin — the held majors; a post-wave `cargo update --dry-run` was not run [NE]. E3 therefore gates on matrix conformance, not on "locks 0".
- The order of §5.3 is the order the re-cut took (toolchain → update → storage → secrets → documents → rmcp → web → config), with the format pins (P05, P14) and the CI/hygiene commits placed as rev 2.1 placed them.

- *Rev 3.9: taken by P10* — the eleven forced digest rewrites call one `openalpaca_storage::hash::sha256_hex`, and the duplicated helpers go with them.
- `Atomic::<u64>::fetch_update` at `session_log/mod.rs:519` — the one rustc deprecation 1.99.0 adds — is replaced by the infallible `Atomic::update` (1.95; rev 3.9, not `try_update`); **not** in P03 (so the lint delta stays attributable); P36's (Required since rev 3.9; `modernization-plan.md` M04).
- Pinning Node (runner 22.23.3 and local 24.15.0 satisfy every floor).
- A `.cargo/config.toml` `registry.global-min-publish-age` — once the pin is ≥ 1.100 (§9).

### 3.3 Found in passing: pre-existing problems, separate work

Unchanged from rev 2.1 §3.3 and re-verified as still present [M-today: `git log`, C]: the MCP HTTP redirect leak (`transport/http.rs`, no redirect policy — one line, recommended soon as its own `fix:`; *rev 3.13 (SF-6): scheduled nowhere until now, and ours, not rmcp's — `crates/openalpaca_mcp/src/transport/http.rs:96-98` builds a reqwest client with the default redirect policy and the `HttpAuth::ApiKey` header (under any name) and every `extra_headers` entry as default headers, handed to rmcp by `with_client` (`:112`); reqwest strips only Authorization, Cookie and Proxy-Authorization on a cross-origin hop, so those headers reach a redirect target. rmcp fixed the same leak in its own default client only (GHSA-9g45-5xwm-f3wc; GHSA-c9xm-49cp-xcr9 not verified), so rmcp 3.5 does not fix ours. Refusing cross-origin redirects adds an enforcement point and breaks a server whose configured URL relies on a redirect, http→https included — owner decision **D6**, pending; recommended as its own `fix(mcp):` before the cut (it moves no version), same-origin redirects still followed, with a loopback test redirecting to a second port that asserts the header does not arrive, and the change named in the commit body and `docs/Daemon_Manual.md`*); the unbounded reconnect ladder (`client.rs:325`); `client_info` never sent (no `get_info` override; rev 3.9's `Auto` lifecycle needs none, so nothing forces it — a two-line addition to P19 if the owner wants our identity sent); `openalpaca config set|unset daemon.*` drops comments (`daemon_config_cli/mod.rs:408`, `:438`); the Linux-only unused imports at `notification/imessage.rs:4` (*rev 3.13, SF-28: `apps/openalpacad/src/notification/imessage.rs:3-5` imports unconditionally while every use is under `cfg(target_os = "macos")`, so `modernization-plan.md`'s M65 warnings gate on CI's ubuntu job would fail on them; they are `cfg`-gated before M65, which Part III carries*); README's Linux prerequisites lacking OpenSSL headers until P28; two rustls crypto backends in one binary (rev 3.9: P41 takes ours to one, aws-lc-rs; `ring` stays only on teloxide 0.17's reqwest 0.12 path); credential discovery reading the real macOS keychain from an isolated daemon (§5.2 switches it off — rev 3.13, SF-13: by an exact pre-seed, with the CLI backends, checked in the daemon's log).

New, or sharpened today:
- **`prepare-sidecar.ts` and tauri-build write and read the same path.** tauri-build copies `src-tauri/bin/openalpacad-<triple>` to `<target_dir>/<profile>/openalpacad` [D: `tauri-build-2.6.3/src/lib.rs:56-75` `copy_binaries`, called at `:535-551`; `tauri-utils-2.10.1/src/resources.rs:46-61`], which is exactly the "source binary" `prepare-sidecar.ts:43` compares by mtime (`:46-52`) and copies (`:81-82`); after any shell compile the shortcut compares the staged copy with a copy of itself. In the checkout: `target/release/openalpacad` absent; `target/debug/openalpacad` 153,165,936 B (Oct 10); the staged `openalpacad-aarch64-apple-darwin` 58,041,424 B (Oct 3; release or debug [NE]) [M-today: `ls`]. Rev 2.1 recorded the mtime rule; the loop with tauri-build is new. Q02 fixes it; until then E5's hand sequence applies.

Q03; until then E5/E7 build the daemon alone, once.

Nothing here is adopted by default: the shipped code is the "no change" answer, and a DU whose recommendation changed says why.

- Options:

Everything measured today applies as measured.

**DU-2 — Which Rust?** (recommendation changed)
- Facts: 1.99.0 published 2026-10-01 (eligible since 10-08); no 1.99.1 on 2026-10-10 (rev 3.13: it exists — the last bullet of this DU); 1.100.0 due 2026-11-12; on this tree `--locked` accepted with the lock byte-identical, 0 errors, one rustc `deprecated` warning, clippy +23/−0, rustfmt byte-identical, tests 5,080/0/16 unchanged; no 1.99.0 compatibility note touches the code [M-today: §2.2]. 1.98.1 was never run today [NE].
- (A) 1.99.0 — measured on this tree. (B) 1.98.1 — September's numbers stand, but on `5e5ac317`, not on this tree; a re-run would be needed anyway (R35). (C) 1.100.0 — not before 2026-11-19 (7 days after release), and unmeasured.
- **Recommendation: (A).** Under (B) P03's figures become rev 2.1's and must be re-measured on `7295f91b` on 1.98.1 before P03; under (C) the wave waits. Rev 2.1's "prefer an x.y.1" still holds as a rule: if a 1.99.1 appears before the cut, take it after re-running P03's gate.
- **Rev 3.13 (SF-8) — 1.99.1 exists, and DU-2's rule and DU-23 disagree on it.** The facts are in §2.2: published 2026-10-15T17:39:23Z by the stable channel manifest and 2026-10-16T04:47:44Z by the GitHub release, it fixes an x86_64 FFI-`bool` miscompilation that CI's runners are exposed to (our own code has no `extern "C"`; dependencies unchecked [NE]). The sentence above says to take it; DU-23 makes it eligible only from 2026-10-22T17:39Z (manifest) or 2026-10-23T04:47Z (GitHub release), and neither DU says which publication time counts for a toolchain. **Owner decision D3, pending** (*taken since — the next bullet, rev 3.15*). The review recommends that DU-23 governs: P03 takes 1.99.0 at the cut, and a one-line P03b `build: Rust 1.99.1` follows through P03's gates once 1.99.1 is eligible, the later (GitHub) time counting; a DU-23 (c) waiver is the alternative because of the x86_64 fix. D3 stays live under D1's recommended cut (2026-10-21T19:53Z); only a cut at or after 2026-10-23T04:48Z takes 1.99.1 at P03 by either clock. Until D3 is answered the gates name **the pinned toolchain** (`rust-toolchain.toml`), not `+1.99.0` (§5.2's G-check; `modernization-plan.md`'s gates).
- **Rev 3.15 — D3 (a), taken by the owner on 2026-10-17: DU-23 governs.** P03 takes Rust 1.99.0 at the cut; a one-line **P03b** `build: Rust 1.99.1` follows through P03's gates once 1.99.1 is seven days old (§5.3). **The clock for a toolchain:** a Rust release is published at the later of its stable channel manifest's time (`static.rust-lang.org/dist/channel-rust-<version>.toml`, its last-modified time) and its GitHub release's `published_at`, and is eligible 7 days after that — the conservative reading the review recommended, and the rule for every toolchain move from now on (1.100.0, due 2026-11-12, among them; §9). For 1.99.1 the manifest reads 2026-10-15T17:39:23Z and the GitHub release 2026-10-16T04:47:44Z, so it is eligible from **2026-10-23T04:47:44Z** (21:47:44 PDT on 2026-10-22), about 33 hours after the cut instant; for 1.99.0 both (2026-10-01T12:38:04Z, 12:41:12Z) are weeks before it. The x86_64 fix that made a waiver the alternative reaches CI from P03b on; our own code declares no `extern "C"`, and whether a dependency returns `bool` across FFI stays [NE] (§2.2). DU-2's rule above — take a 1.99.1 that appears before the cut — is superseded by this clock: 1.99.1 appeared before the cut and is not eligible at it. Since rev 3.13 the gates name the pinned toolchain, so P03b rewrites no gate literal (§5.2; §5.3's preamble (4)).

**DU-13 — Bun version and lockfile format.** (recommendation changed)
- Facts [M-today: gui §2.1; Docs]: Bun 1.4.2 keeps an existing v1 lock at v1 on re-save (11 advisories remain) and writes v2 from scratch (0 advisories; four gates green); dependabot-core `main` bundles Bun 1.4.2 and accepts `lockfileVersion` ≤ 3 since 2026-10-06; the hosted updater's rollout is [NE]; the owner's Bun is 1.3.13 and must not be touched by the research; setup-bun reads `packageManager` from the **root** `package.json`; the lock is the root `bun.lock`.
- **(a)** Pin `"packageManager": "bun@1.4.2"` (P01) and commit the **v2** lock Bun 1.4.2 writes from scratch (P31). Every local Bun must then be ≥ 1.4; the owner installs 1.4.2 exactly (§5.1 item 2). **Condition:** the first Dependabot bun run on the v2 lock must succeed; if it fails with an unsupported-lockfile error, fall back to (b).
- **(b)** Pin 1.4.2 but commit a v1 lock written from scratch by a Bun **1.3.x** (in the one measured run 1.4.2 wrote v2 from scratch and kept an existing v1 on re-save [M-today: gui B1/B2]; whether it can be made to write v1 from scratch was not tried and no documentation was read for it [NE]) — the lock that then has 11 advisories unless it is regenerated by 1.3.x, which is what September's locks are. Keeps Dependabot happy on any image. **Prerequisite: a Bun 1.3.x still installed** (§5.1 item 2 keeps the owner's 1.3.13 until P35's first bun PR succeeds).
- **(c)** Pin 1.4.3 — 1.0 d old, unmeasured; its install-path and TLS fixes are a one-line follow-up after 2026-10-17.
- Node: unpinned (every floor met); pin 24 via setup-node only for local/CI parity.
- **Recommendation: (a).** Under (b) P31 is authored with a Bun 1.3.x and the §9 checklist watches the lock version; the `grep lockfileVersion: 1` assertion idea is dropped. Under (c) re-run the four gates under 1.4.3 first.
- **Rev 3.13 (SF-29, SF-2).** (b)'s prerequisite no longer holds: the machine holds only `~/.bun`, at 1.4.3 (the owner's default since rev 3.7); `~/.bun-1.4.2` does not exist and no Bun 1.3.x is installed [M: the review's listing of `~/.bun*`; elsewhere on the machine not checked]. (b) now needs a fresh Bun 1.3.x in its own `BUN_INSTALL` root — installed by the owner before P35 if the fallback is to stay ready, or recorded as needing that install when it triggers: **owner decision D12, pending** (recommended: record that it needs the install when it triggers — dependabot-core reads lockfile versions up to 3, so the fallback is unlikely to be needed). (a)'s condition gains a second failure mode — dependabot-core bundles Bun 1.4.2 while P42 pins 1.4.3 — and can be judged only after the merge, because Dependabot version updates and `schedule:` workflows read the default branch only; P35's gate and this condition move to an item after the merge, naming the follow-up commit they may trigger (§5.3).

### 4.3 New decisions for Part I

**DU-23 — The freshness collisions (the 7-day rule on the day of the cut).**
- Facts: §1.6's table — on 2026-10-10 the absolute latest of 17 packages is younger than 7 days; **today's groups measured the absolute-latest set** (recut: uuid 1.28.0, serde_json 1.0.152, toml 1.1.8, toml_edit 0.25.17, tokio-util 0.7.20, signal-hook 0.4.5 in the lock; gui: vite 8.3.4, marked 18.1.0, prettier 3.9.10, jsdom 30.1.2, @types/node 26.6.5, plugin-react 6.1.2, the 2.12.3/2.8.2/2.12.2 Tauri packages) — and the eligible set is a set nobody ran. Every one of the measured versions is eligible on or after **2026-10-17** (vite 8.3.4 from 10-15; marked 18.1.0, plugin-react 6.1.2 from 10-12; jsdom 30.1.2, signal-hook 0.4.5 from 10-11; toml 1.1.8, toml_edit 0.25.17, tokio-util 0.7.20, @types/node 26.6.5 from 10-16; everything dated 10-10 from 10-17). Two measured versions stay below the latest by choice: rmcp 3.5.0 (not 3.5.1, DU-25) and Bun 1.4.2 (not 1.4.3, DU-13). *(Superseded, rev 3.9: rmcp takes the newest 3.5.x eligible at the cut, DU-25 (v4), and Bun 1.4.3 at P42 — neither stays below by choice.)* P04's refresh at the cut will move whatever appears meanwhile; "measured = taken" is a statement about today's names, checked by P04's gate against `recut-02-lockdiff.txt`. The five open advisories do not wait for this: DU-1 (C) is the counterweight, and the delay under (a) is seven days.
- **(a)** Cut on or after 2026-10-17 and take the measured set as the eligible set. No gate re-run beyond the ordinary ones. *Reworded, rev 3.13 (MF-2): cut on or after 2026-10-17, at the cut instant below, and take the newest release eligible at that instant, checked by the freshness gate. "Take the measured set" was contradicted by rev 3.9's own minute table (serde_json 1.0.154, measured in the rehearsal, is eligible only on 2026-10-18); the reword states the rule the owner took on 2026-10-11 and decides nothing new.* *Rev 3.15: the instant is answered — D1 (a), 2026-10-21T19:53Z (the paragraph after this DU's tables).*
- **(b)** Cut earlier and take §1.6's "newest eligible" column: P04 steps back with `cargo update -p <spec> --precise <version>` — `uuid --precise 1.27.0`, `serde_json --precise 1.0.151`, **`toml@1.1.8 --precise 1.1.6`**, **`toml_edit@0.25.17 --precise 0.25.15`**, `tokio-util --precise 0.7.19`, `signal-hook --precise 0.4.4` (the two `name@locked-version` spellings because the lock holds three `toml` and three `toml_edit`; the bare `-p toml` / `-p toml_edit` are refused as ambiguous, exit 101, and change nothing — all six steps measured, their lock `recut-lock-02-update-stepback-b.lock`, 6 moved [M-today, rev 3.1: `held-02a…h`]; the rule: any crate the day's lock holds at more than one version is spelled `name@locked`); the Tauri set held anyway; P31–P33 pin vite 8.3.2, marked 18.0.14, prettier 3.9.9, jsdom 30.1.1, @types/node 26.6.4, plugin-react 6.1.1; P31a takes the 2.12.1 pair — and **re-runs the four bun gates and G-GUI on that set**, since none of it was measured.

**Rev 3.13 — the cut instant (MF-1, MF-2, G-03).** The cut is an **instant**, written in UTC with the owner's zone (PDT, UTC−7) beside it, never a date: a date alone, read in either zone, admits releases early. Which instant is **owner decision D1** of the 2026-10-17 review — pending when this was written, *taken by the owner on 2026-10-17 as recommended (rev 3.15, the paragraph after the tables)* — recommended **2026-10-21T19:53Z (12:53 PDT)**, when the last release of Tauri's 2026-10-14 train on either registry (the npm cli's `cli-linux-riscv64-gnu` binary, 2026-10-14T19:52:53Z) is 7 days old; until answered, rev 3.9's **2026-10-17T20:08Z (13:08 PDT)**. Under either answer: every version-moving commit takes the newest release **eligible at the cut instant** — crates.io `created_at` or npm `time` at or before the instant minus 7 days — **checked by the freshness gate** (§5.2: `lockdates.py --gate --max-date <the instant minus 7 days, UTC>` on every lock a commit moves, for cargo; `bunfig.toml`'s `minimumReleaseAge = 604800`, for JS) and **measured on the day**. No version literal in this plan is re-anchored before D1 is answered; once it is, P31a, this table, the driver's pair and JS pins (literals at `tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/gbase/gbase.sh:341-344` at `3598a6fb`; parameters once §5.1 item 6b (2) is built) and step 1b's reference lock are re-anchored to the answer, and G-GUI and G-bundle re-measured (*rev 3.15: P31a, this DU's table and §5.1 item 6b's driver values are re-anchored; G-GUI was measured on the 2026-10-14 family in scratch by the compatibility report, and G-bundle is P31a's own gate*). The G-base driver refuses to start before the instant (a `--not-before <UTC>` flag, MF-1 3). What D1 decides besides the instant: under its recommendation D2 (rustls 0.23.46, eligible 2026-10-21T09:54:15Z) is moot and the whole 2026-10-14 Tauri train is taken with no Tauri step-back — G-GUI and G-bundle re-measured on 2.12.4 / tauri-build 2.7.3 / `tauri-plugin-dialog = "=2.8.3"` ↔ `@tauri-apps/api` 2.12.4 / `plugin-dialog` 2.8.3 / `cli` 2.12.3, and the JS releases eligible by then (among them @tanstack/react-query 5.104.2, dompurify 3.4.17, marked 18.1.1, vitest 5.0.4, react 19.3.1 — eligible from 2026-10-21T16:47Z, three hours before the instant) measured — @tanstack/react-query 5.104.3 and vitest 5.0.5 (npm `time` 2026-10-15T07:53:35Z and 14:29:01Z) are eligible only from 2026-10-22T07:53Z and 2026-10-22T14:29Z, after it, so that cut does not take them and §5.1 item 5's before-the-merge sweep does; serde-saphyr 2.0.0 becomes eligible before P30 (D4); D3 stays live. Under rev 3.9's instant P31a, P04 and the driver carry ordered step-backs measured on the day (the Tauri cascade with tauri-utils last, §2.3; about 33 entries of HEAD's lock by the review's approximate sweep), and D2 needs an answer.
