**DU-24 — opener 0.8.5 → 0.9.0 (never researched before).**
- Facts [M-today; D; Docs]: 0.9.0 released 2026-09-29 (eligible), MSRV 1.77; no public signature change; our one call (`routes/files_types.rs:157`) compiles unchanged; behaviour: Linux missing-path error (our route stages the file first — *corrected, rev 3.13 (A2-15): only an upload; a by-product or a result file opens in place, §2.1*), `OpenError::Io` display text changes (the `500 OPEN_FAILED` body), an `opener-reaper` thread on unix (not used on macOS), WSL via PowerShell; `opener = "0.8"` (`Cargo.toml:149`) so P04 never moves it; rev 2.1 listed it under "at their latest" by mistake.
- **(a)** Take it as **P27a** (one manifest line, no source change) beside P25–P27, gated by `cargo test -p openalpacad -- routes::files` and a manual Open on Linux (E5b, or the Linux native-smoke under DU-31) — *the Linux Open deferred under DU-33 (rev 3.13, SF-17)*.
- **(b)** Hold 0.8.5 and say so in §3.
- **Recommendation: (a).** Under (b) P27a is dropped and `opener` joins §3.1 with the trigger "a reason to touch the Open path". *Rev 3.15 (C3):* 0.9.0 is eligible at the instant and P27a lands as written; the prep tip does not carry it (`opener = "0.8"`, `refs/prep/deps-wave-2026-10-12:Cargo.toml:175` — its re-cut `6992a402` never touched it), so a cut-day set measured on the prep tip adds it.

**DU-25 — rmcp: which version, in how many commits, and the protocol offer.** (supersedes DU-5's (A) and (d))
- Facts [M-today; D]: one step 0.16.0 → 3.5.0 compiles and passes the whole suite with the archived source hunks plus the fifth annotation helper; 3.5.0 is eligible (09-28), 3.5.1 (10-05) is not until 10-12; 3.5.0 moved `ProtocolVersion::LATEST` to `2026-07-28` and our client has no `get_info` override, so its `initialize` offers `2026-07-28` — **the measured re-cut state has no pin**; 3.5.1 adds server-side handling for a client that skips `initialize`, an ungated `object!` macro and auth fixes — `service/client.rs` and both streamable-HTTP client files are byte-identical to 3.5.0's, so it is behaviour-neutral for our features [D: `diff -rq rmcp-3.5.0/src rmcp-3.5.1/src`, rev 3.9; rev 3–3.8 said "client-side"]; `TokioChildProcess`, the streamable-HTTP client and `ContentBlock` are unchanged 3.4.1 → 3.5.1; the archived stage patches target 0.17 / 1.8 / 2.2 / 3.4.1 and none contains the fifth helper; September's `rmcp-stage4-3.4.diff:512` wrote a caret `3.4.1`, which at branch cut resolves 3.5.x — a build nobody compiled.
- **Version:** (v1) **3.5.0** — measured; (v2) 3.5.1 — 2-day waiver today, eligible from 10-12, unmeasured on our code (its diffs touch no file we use [D]); (v3) `=3.4.1` — September's research target, offers `2025-11-25` without an override, needs the archived stage-4 patch re-cut for the fifth helper, and takes a `3.4.1 → 3.5.x` follow-up later.
- **Shape:** (s1) **one commit** 0.16.0 → the chosen version, as measured, carrying P20's constructors, P21's `ContentBlock` and the `docs/api` regeneration, P22's `Option<server_info>`, process-wrap 10.0.1, the five annotation helpers and the gates (real-server round trip, descendant `ps` check); (s2) rev 2.1's four commits (P19 0.17, P20 1.8, P21 2.2, P22 3.x) — bisectable, but each intermediate state must be re-cut against today's `builtins/mod.rs` and the last against the chosen version; none of them is measured on this tree.
- **Protocol offer (DU-5 d, now required for 3.5.x):** (p1) override `NotifyingHandler::get_info` to return `ClientConfig { protocol_version: ProtocolVersion::LATEST_WITH_INITIALIZE, ..Default::default() }` (offers `2025-11-25`, the version 3.4.1 offered; `client_info` stays rmcp's default unless the owner separately decides to send ours, rev 2.1 §3.3) — a small source change **not in the measured state**, verified by P19's real-server round trip reading the offered version on the wire; (p2) take the default offer `2026-07-28` and let the round trip decide; (p3) `=3.4.1` (v3) and no override.
- **Requirement spelling:** (r1) `rmcp = { version = "3.5.0", … }` — a caret, so the maintenance bot's lockfile-only refresh moves to 3.5.1 (its job) while this wave's lock pins 3.5.0 (`cargo update -p rmcp --precise 3.5.0`, run twice from a 0.16 lock, and `rmcp-macros` likewise); (r2) `=3.5.0` — the bot cannot move it; a later bump is a manifest commit.
- **Recommendation (rev 3.9): (v4) + (s1) + (p4) + (r1′).** **(v4)** the newest 3.5.x ≥ 7 days old at the cut (3.5.1 today, eligible 2026-10-12T18:35Z) — §9's freshness rule, no `--precise` step-back. **(p4)** `serve_with_lifecycle(transport, ClientLifecycleMode::Auto { preferred_versions: vec![ProtocolVersion::LATEST], legacy_version: Some(ProtocolVersion::LATEST_WITH_INITIALIZE) })` in `serve_with_conn` (`client.rs:665-666`): a modern server gets `server/discover` (`2026-07-28`), a legacy one `initialize` offering `2025-11-25`; rmcp's streamable-HTTP client turns a pre-session 4xx (other than 401/403) to `server/discover` into a correlated JSON-RPC error, so a legacy HTTP server falls back too [D: `rmcp-3.5.1/src/transport/streamable_http_client.rs:375-406`, `transport/common/reqwest/streamable_http_client.rs:284-288`, `service/client.rs:799-835`, `:1044-1057`]; the named costs — a server that ignores unknown methods waits out `DEFAULT_AUTO_DISCOVER_TIMEOUT` (10 s, `:657`) per (re)connect, and 401/403, 5xx, a transport failure or a malformed 2xx fail the connect with no fallback; no `get_info` version pin (`client_info` unchanged, §3.3). **(r1′)** a caret whose floor is the version taken (`rmcp = { version = "3.5.1", … }`). And the stdio child wrapped as a process-group leader, unconditionally (P19). *Rev 3–3.8 recommended (v1) + (s1) + (p1) + (r1) — a patch below the newest eligible and the old lifecycle pinned one revision back: compromises under the owner's rule of 2026-10-12.* Under (s2) P20–P22 come back as separate commits and the count rises by three.
- **Rev 3.13 — P19b is presented as a decision (E-19).** P19b (rev 3.12, "the lead's ruling") has a `2026-07-28` session open a new outbound `subscriptions/listen` to its server; Part III treats a new outbound protocol message as a decision to present (DU-R4 (v), `modernization-plan.md` §5), so P19b is **owner decision D5**, pending. The review recommends taking it — it closes §3.7's silent hole (X-35) for modern servers — together with one refresh on the `listen` acknowledgment (A1-12: a list change announced between `serve` and the acknowledgment is otherwise not heard until an unrelated change; one `ServerChange`, i.e. one `tools/list`, through the existing channel on acknowledgment, and a stub test that announces a change before acknowledging), recorded in api-fix-plan §0 and CLAUDE.md once taken. P19b's in-process stub is its gate of record, its real-server check an E-step (SF-12, §5.3).
- **Rev 3.14 — sub-row (m): a per-server lifecycle mode and a named negotiation failure** (the ZCode audit's A-02, A-03 and A-27; Z5-01, Z5-13). **Pending, not adopted**; recorded in `tasks/pending-decisions.md`. Facts: P19 hard-codes `ClientLifecycleMode::Auto` (`refs/prep/deps-wave-2026-10-12:crates/openalpaca_mcp/src/client.rs:701-706`); rmcp's 10 s discover timeout is a private constant that every public entry point passes (rmcp-3.5.1 `src/service/client.rs:657`, `:755`), so no shorter probe can be set; a server that closes the probe, or answers it with anything but a correlated JSON-RPC error, fails with no fallback (`:1044-1057`); the probe runs on every connect, mid-call respawns (`crates/openalpaca_mcp/src/client.rs:438-491`) and boot included, and boot waits for MCP before it serves (`apps/openalpacad/src/main.rs:381`, `:785`); and every bring-up failure but 401/403 reads `Unreachable`, "not actionable — retry" (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/tools/mcp/classify.rs:28-36`). ZCode's dual-era negotiation has a per-server `auto`, `legacy` or strict `2026-07-28` mode, a probe bounded by 5 s and by half the connect budget, and a typed `protocol_negotiation_failed`, which it added after a real server's non-standard answer to the probe was shown as "network unreachable" (ZCode:docs/mcp-dual-era-version-negotiation.md:21-116; ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/adapters/src/mcp/index.ts:1726-1747, :2316-2370). The options, all or some: **(m-a)** a `protocol = "auto" | "legacy" | "2026-07-28"` key on `[servers.<name>]` in `mcp.toml`, mapped to rmcp's Auto, Initialize and Discover, the pin never downgrading, and written with `skip_serializing_if` so that every existing `config_fingerprint` stays byte-identical; **(m-b)** `NoCompatibleProtocolVersion`, `ExpectedInitResult`, `LegacyFallbackFailed` and a transport close during the probe classified as one new `FailureReason` word in the extension design's total enum (its §4.2), whose text names `protocol = "legacy"`; **(m-c)** an in-session respawn pinned to the era this load negotiated — OpenAlpaca's own choice, since ZCode renegotiates on every reconnect; **(m-d)** a warning, never a refusal, for `connect_timeout_secs <= 10` with `auto`; **(m-e)** an rmcp issue asking for a configurable auto-discover timeout, the named way out of the private 10 s — the owner's to post; **(m-f)** `get_info` overridden with our `client_info` and an explicit capability set, empty until M84 — the two lines §3.3 already offers; **(m-g)** the negotiated protocol version and the connect and list durations on the extension row and in `openalpaca ext info`, `docs/api` regenerated (A-27, in Part II's list) — from which Appendix B #4 and #42 are then read (A-06). **No change** is P19 as written. **Recommendation (the audit's): take (m-a)–(m-g) with P19, before it lands** — a new recommendation for a new sub-row; DU-25 (p4) as taken stands. Each item adds a config key, an enum word, an outbound identity or a row field, so none is adopted here, and P19's body (§5.3) is written to hold under either answer.

**DU-26 — The Tauri pairing: hold at P04, move the pair in one commit.**
- Facts [M-today: §2.10, §2.3]: the break is real today; the Tauri CLI cannot see the JS packages under Bun; CI compiles nothing under `src-tauri`; the lock holds 2.11.5 (GHSA-w28w-mhc8-qvjv affected); 2.11.6 is eligible and the GHSA fix; the 2.12.1 pair is the newest eligible pair today, the 2.12.3 pair from 10-17 and the one measured (check/clippy/fmt 0 warnings; four bun gates); 2.12 drops Windows 7 (windows 0.62) and the plan records no supported-OS floor; **the hold is measured (rev 3.1)**: `tauri 2.11.6`'s requirements on its siblings are carets, so `--precise` on the four leaf crates alone leaves `tauri-runtime(-wry) 2.12.1` and `tauri-utils 2.10.1` in the lock and `tauri 2.11.6` does not compile against them (19 errors); the ten-crate hold — HEAD's Tauri sub-graph with only `tauri` moved — resolves and compiles the shell clean (`cargo check`, 0 warnings), and leaves the host graph of everything else untouched.
- **(a)** P04 holds the Rust side at **HEAD's Tauri sub-graph with only `tauri` moved to 2.11.6** — ten `--precise` steps in the measured order: tauri-plugin-dialog 2.7.3, tauri-plugin-fs 2.5.2, tauri 2.11.6, tauri-build 2.6.3, tauri-runtime-wry 2.11.4, tauri-runtime 2.11.3, tauri-macros 2.6.3, tauri-codegen 2.6.3, tauri-plugin 2.6.3, tauri-utils 2.9.3 — which keeps wry 0.55.1 / tao 0.35.3 / webview2-com 0.38.2 and `infer 0.19`/`unic-*` until P31a (rev 3's four-crate form is **not an option**: it is a mixed 2.11/2.12 state that does not compile [M-today]; G-GUI at P04 re-runs the measured check with clippy and fmt), then **P31a** moves both locks together to the 2.12 pair — 2.12.3 / tauri-build 2.7.2 / dialog 2.8.2 ↔ `@tauri-apps/api` 2.12.3 / `plugin-dialog` 2.8.2 / `cli` 2.12.2 under DU-23 (a), the 2.12.1 / 2.7.1 / 2.8.1 ↔ 2.12.1 / 2.8.1 / 2.12.1 pair under DU-23 (b) — and narrows the requirements to `tauri = "2.12"`, `tauri-build = "2.7"`, `tauri-plugin-dialog = "2.8"` so that neither a lockfile-only Dependabot PR nor a plain `cargo update` can move a Tauri minor alone.
- **(b)** Take 2.12.x on the Rust side at P04 and the JS side at P31: the tree is mismatched in between. Not recommended.
- **(c)** Hold 2.11.6 for the whole wave (no P31a): the GHSA fix lands, the JS stays 2.11.1 (same minor), the `unic-*` warnings and the GUI-only `infer 0.19` stay (the ten-crate hold keeps tauri-utils 2.9.3 [M-today]), wry/tao do not move, Windows 7 stays supported.
- Sub-row — **the Windows 7 floor**: the owner either accepts the drop (recorded in P31a's body and the release notes — rev 3.13, G-06: no release-notes file exists, so the *Other Platforms* section of `docs/Installation_Manual.md` records it, P31a) or takes (c). No supported-OS floor is written anywhere today [C: none found].
- Sub-row — **(p-ii)**, the ten-line CI comparison of `tauri*` in `Cargo.lock` with `@tauri-apps/*` in `bun.lock` (DU-15): the narrowed requirements make it a belt over braces; still a new enforcement point.
- **Recommendation (rev 3.9): (a′) — the pair first.** P31a moves to Phase B, right after P03, and moves both locks together (the Rust side by `cargo update -p tauri -p tauri-build -p tauri-plugin-dialog`, then `--precise` to the newest eligible 2.12.x; the JS side by an incremental `bun install`, the v1 lock rewritten from scratch only at P31); P04 is then a plain refresh with **no** Tauri `--precise` step. (a)'s invariant — the pair moves in one commit; Rust never moves a Tauri minor alone — is kept and tightened: `tauri-plugin-dialog` is pinned `=` to the newest version both registries carry **that is eligible at the cut instant** (rev 3.13, MF-1 — rev 3.9–3.12 left out "eligible"; which version that is waits on D1: `=2.8.2` at 2026-10-17T20:08Z, `=2.8.3` at D1's recommendation; crates.io also carries crate-only 2.0.3–2.0.5, so plain equality is not always available — a crate-only patch is a hand-made Rust-only commit that says so), `tauri = "~2.12"` and `tauri-build = "~2.7"` (tilde: the minor held — a caret `"2.12"` admits 2.13, rev 3.10) join it in `[workspace.dependencies]`, P35 ignores every update type for the plugin packages, and (p-ii) is withdrawn. Why: (a)'s ten-crate hold existed only so P04 could stay lock-only while the JS half waited for Phase J — measured to compile, but a sequencing convenience; the gui group's stage D **is** the pair-first commit's Rust half (56 lock changes from HEAD's lock; check 0 / clippy 0 / fmt 0 [M-today: gui D]). The pair-alone JS state (the three `@tauri-apps/*` pins on HEAD's other pins, an incremental v1 lock) was never measured as a stage, so P31a's gate runs the four bun gates itself, and `bun audit` stays at HEAD's 24 until P31. The Windows 7 drop stays accepted and recorded (the sub-row). Gone with the hold: the ten-crate list, `tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/gbase/hold-footprint.txt` as a reference, the held reference locks as G-base references (§5.1 item 6b), P18's and P29's interim notes, P31a's ten-step back-out, Appendix B #29, #39, #40. *Rev 3.1–3.8 recommended (a); the owner took it on 2026-10-11, and the owner's rule of 2026-10-12 is what revises it.* Under (c) P31a is dropped and §3.1 gains "tauri 2.12: the Windows 7 decision". *Rev 3.13 (MF-1):* "the Rust side by `cargo update -p tauri -p tauri-build -p tauri-plugin-dialog`, then `--precise` to the newest eligible 2.12.x" no longer lands on one coherent set: since the 2026-10-14 train the targeted update unlocks the internals and resolves to the 10-14 releases, and `--precise` on tauri alone leaves them (§2.3). So P31a's Rust half is: the targeted update, then — only for releases younger than 7 days at the cut instant — an ordered step-back cascade, tauri-utils last, the lock re-read after each step, each spelled `name@locked`, measured on the day (none under D1's recommendation); then G-base's step 1b checks every Tauri crate exactly and each one's `created_at` against the instant (§5.1 item 6b). The swift-rs rationale for the `--precise` step is gone (§2.3). P04's "no Tauri step" holds only when the instant admits the whole set P31a took: otherwise P04's refresh retakes tauri's newest patch under `~2.12` and P04 steps it back the same way (§5.3). *Rev 3.15 (D1 (a); C2):* at the instant every crate of the family is eligible (DU-23's rev 3.15 table), so the cascade is empty, P04 steps back no Tauri crate, and `tauri-plugin-dialog = "=2.8.3"`. The starting lock decides the command: from HEAD's lock — the branch's real base — the targeted update takes the whole family in one command; from a lock already on the 2.12.3 family (the prep tip, or a rehearsal from it) it moves only the dialog and fs plugins, and `cargo update -p tauri --precise 2.12.4` then `cargo update -p tauri-plugin` (or every internal named with `-p`) complete it; step 1b's exact check refuses the mixed state either way (P31a).

### 4.4 New decisions for Part II (the flexible apps; §7)

Each of these widens a component's job, adds a boundary or an enforcement point, or costs money, and is therefore a decision. None lands under a dependency-bump commit.

**DU-27 — Extract `openalpaca_app_support` (G1).** [facts: architect §1–§2, measured crate counts; sizes from `fn_ranges.py`]
- The shell uses 17 storage items, none SQLite; a database-free crate of ≈1,910 moved lines (paths ≈450, discovery ≈210, daemon_lifecycle ≈180, the project reader incl. `resolve` ≈820, three `fsx` primitives + `sha256_hex` ≈80, reveal ≈170) and 637 lines of test files moving whole, with storage re-exporting every moved item under its old path so the 184 references in 65 files outside storage do not change (*superseded, rev 3.9 — C-07: no re-exports; the references are re-pointed, the recommendation below*); the shell's graph 245 → 238 distinct crates (236 under W2); after it no edit under `crates/openalpaca_storage/` rebuilds the shell. Reveal moves **with the one canonical project reader** (A10's correction of A09).
- **(a)** Two commits — Q05 (paths, discovery, lifecycle) and Q06 (project reader, fsx, reveal; the shell leaves storage) — with W1 (the whole discovery module moves; `rand`/`chacha20` stay in the shell's graph), the 46 writer-using tests staying in storage, and the two `DURING_READ` hook tests rewritten onto a 10-line `#[cfg(test)]` fixture writer and moved. **(b)** The same with W2 (feature-gate the writer; saves two crates; adds a feature to reason about). **(c)** The same but the two hook tests kept unchanged through a `test-hooks` feature on support (the `test-home-guard` pattern). **(d)** Do not extract: the shell keeps linking storage; G-GUI keeps its P06/P08/P12-shaped triggers (P12's is dropped either way).
- Name: `openalpaca_app_support` (alternative `openalpaca_host`).
- **Recommendation: (a).** Under (d) §7.3 is dropped and the shell's compile isolation stays [NE]. Under any of (a)–(c) the `test-home-guard` audit extends to the new crate (its tests carry a copy of `HomeStoreGuard`), Q01's discovery byte pin lands first, and the shell's dependency baseline is recaptured after Q06 (G-GUI, §7.7). **Rev 3.9 (the owner's rule):** (a) **without** re-exports — Q05 and Q06 re-point the 184 references in 65 files outside storage to `openalpaca_app_support::…` in the same commits (the daemon and the CLI gain the dependency; storage imports support internally; *rev 3.13: so do core, plugins and connectors' tests, which name the items Q05 moves — §7.2's direction sentence*); no `pub use` keeps the old paths alive beside the new ones, and the shell switches once, in Q06.
- **Rev 3.13 (SF-20):** P10 creates `openalpaca_storage::hash::{sha256_hex, lower_hex}` and re-points every caller to it (prep `b899046c`), among them `store/projects.rs`'s `path_hash`, the reader Q06 moves into `openalpaca_app_support`, which may not depend on storage. So Q06 moves `hash` whole, with P10's known-answer test, to `openalpaca_app_support::hash`, storage importing it from support; P10 stays as written (support does not exist yet). P10's callers in core (among them `memory/service`, `session_log/writer.rs`, `tools/builtins/file_ops.rs`, `update_persona`, and core's tests) and the daemon (`hot_reload.rs`, `managers/mcp.rs`) then import from support (the prep tree's list: `git grep -l 'storage::hash\|crate::hash' refs/prep/deps-wave-2026-10-12 -- crates apps`). Core already depends on support from Q05, which moves items core names (`config_io.rs:207`, `:261`, `tools/working_folder.rs:186`, `tools/builtins/pre_edit.rs:135` at `3598a6fb`; §7.2), so P10's `hash` callers in core add to that dependency rather than create it. Q05's body names the widening — the daemon, the CLI, core, plugins and connectors' tests — and Q06's body names the `hash` callers it re-points; the only alternative is a re-export, which this DU's answer forbids. §7.2's table, Q05 and Q06 carry it.
- **(e)** (rev 3.13, C-13) **Reveal as a daemon route by file id**, instead of moving the project reader and reveal into the shell's support crate: it would remove Q06's ≈1,070-line move and the webview-supplied paths while Q05 stays in the shell, but it changes §7.8's settled rule — no daemon API change beyond the additive protocol field — and widens the daemon. **Owner decision D8**, pending, with **no recommendation to adopt it by default**; it wants one answer before Q06.

**DU-28 — HostBridge and a browser dev mode (G3-a).** [facts: architect §3]
- Four `invoke` sites (`lib/connection.ts:79`, `:106`, `:241`; `lib/reveal.ts:43`), one `getCurrentWindow` (`TrafficLights.tsx:18`), one dynamic plugin-dialog import (`AddExtensionForm.tsx:44`), `canReveal` on `globalThis.isTauri` (`reveal.ts:25`); 23 test files `vi.mock("@tauri-apps/api/core")`. Plain Vite is not a daemon-connected app today because `connection.ts:26` imports `invoke` and `:298` bootstraps through Tauri.
- **(a)** A `HostBridge` interface (`ensureDaemonRunning`, `getConnectionInfo`, `readDaemonLogTail`, `awaitDaemonStopped`, `reveal`, `dialog`, `window`) with three hosts: `TauriHost`; `DevServerHost`, a serve-only Vite middleware reading `$OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE/state/discovery.json` **server-side at request time** (expiry + TCP probe; answers `127.0.0.1`/`localhost` origins only; spawns nothing; never emitted by `vite build`, so no token reaches `dist/`) — **and a widening the owner should see** (rev 3.1): the middleware hands the daemon's token to **any local HTTP client that reaches `127.0.0.1:1420`** while the dev server runs — the `Origin` check binds browsers only, a `curl` sets any `Origin` — so the token's reach grows from "a same-user process that can read the 0600 file" to "any local process of any user"; Vite binds localhost (`vite.config.ts:22-27` sets `port: 1420` and `strictPort`, no `host` [C]), so never a remote one; dev-only and serve-only, never in a bundle (*corrected, rev 3.13 (SF-21): the `Origin` check as written does not bind browsers either — Vite's default `server.cors` is `{ origin: defaultAllowedOrigins }`, which admits localhost, `*.localhost`, 127.0.0.1 and `[::1]` on **any** port, and its CORS middleware is installed before the `configureServer` hooks run [D: Vite 7.3.6 `dist/node/chunks/config.js`, read by the review], while the rule above pins no port and the daemon's CORS is `CorsLayer::permissive()` (`apps/openalpacad/src/router.rs:487`), so a page on any other local dev server could read the connection endpoint cross-origin. DevServerHost is therefore registered only when `VITE_OPENALPACA_HOST=dev-server`; in that mode `server.cors: false` and `Origin` must be exactly `http://localhost:1420` or `http://127.0.0.1:1420`; it refuses unless `OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE` is set and absolute, with no fallback to `home_root()`, which would serve the owner's real token; the log-tail endpoint follows the same rules; and Q07's gate adds a cross-origin fetch from another localhost port. The local-`curl` widening disclosed above stands; `allowedHosts` blocks DNS rebinding; nothing is built yet (Q07). The CSP's loopback wildcard and the daemon's permissive CORS are pre-existing, and whether to record them as accepted residue or narrow them once Q04 lands is owner decision D9, pending — C-24*); `FixtureHost` (replaces the 23 mock blocks; a `?host=fixture` browser mode for layout work). ≈ +350 / −250 LOC in `src/`, ≈ −230 lines of mocks [NE: estimates]. **(b)** (a) plus a fixture daemon (MSW-style canned `/v1/*`, ≈200+ LOC) for a fully offline browser mode. **(c)** Nothing: `bun run dev` stays a layout-only server.
- **Recommendation: (a) now, (b) later** if layout work wants it. Under (c) P31–P33's tests keep their 23 mocks.
- **Rev 3.14 (the ZCode audit, A-21; Z6-09) — an alternative recorded as pending, not adopted** (`tasks/pending-decisions.md` §4.3, *DevServerHost proxy*). SF-21's corrected (a) stands, and Q07 is written under it. ZCode's dev server proxies its API and WebSocket paths to a local server on the same origin (ZCode:packages/web/vite.config.ts:56-70), a server its own audit found unauthenticated by default. The alternative for DevServerHost is a serve-only reverse proxy that adds the token server-side, so its value never leaves the dev-server process. Its cost is stated with it: the daemon becomes ambiently authenticated at `127.0.0.1:1420`; a WebSocket upgrade bypasses CORS, so any web page could open the proxied events socket; and a simple cross-origin request would carry the token. It is therefore weaker than (a) unless every proxied request, the WebSocket and SSE upgrades included, passes an exact `Origin` check, a request with no `Origin` is refused, and SF-21's `server.cors: false` holds. The audit recommends no change: SF-21 stands, and the proxy is offered only with this trade-off stated. It wants an answer before Q07.

**DU-29 — The daemon compatibility contract (G3-b): a new refusal state.** [facts: architect §4]
- `discovery.json` carries `build.version`/`build.protocol` (literals `1` at `discovery/mod.rs:226`, `:241`) that `ConnectionInfo` drops (`:253-267`); `/v1/health` returns `status`, `version`, `pid`, `instance_id` and no protocol (`router.rs:485-492`; `:492-505` at `3598a6fb`); the shell reuses any daemon that accepts a TCP connect within 300 ms (`lib.rs:257-268`), past the token's expiry (`:40-49`, T21); a spawn over a live daemon meets the singleton lock and exits (`discovery/mod.rs:180-210`, `main.rs:118`).
- **(a)** `support::protocol::{PROTOCOL, OLDEST_SUPPORTED}` as the one source for `make_discovery`, a new `/v1/health` field `protocol`, additive `version`/`protocol` on `ConnectionInfo` (Rust + TS; `null` from an older shell/daemon); a pure `decide_reuse` with the five-step rule — spawn (no/unparseable file); spawn (TCP refused, health fails, or `instance_id` mismatch — the listener is not this daemon); reuse (`protocol == PROTOCOL`); reuse with a note (`OLDEST_SUPPORTED <= p < PROTOCOL`); **mismatch** (`p > PROTOCOL` or `p < OLDEST_SUPPORTED`): never kill, never spawn, name the versions and the remedy (`openalpaca daemon stop`, then Start daemon, or update the app); the TS reconnect ladder parks on a mismatch like `stopped_elsewhere`. *Rev 3.13 (C-09): a sixth case — the discovery pid is a live `openalpacad` (`running_daemon_pid()`, which has no side effect) but health does not answer — is **unresponsive**: park and retry, never spawn, no log rotation (today a spawn there rotates the log under the live daemon's open fd and is then refused by the lock); `singleton_lock_is_free()` is never a first step, since it takes the lock and can make a booting daemon exit; an unparseable discovery file over a live pid is a mismatch candidate, not a spawn (§7.2 carries the row).* The health probe: (a-i) a ≈40-line hand-written HTTP GET over `std::net::TcpStream` (no new dependency; the shell has no HTTP client in its graph today [M-today]) or (a-ii) a small client crate. T21 is **not** decided here: today's reuse-past-expiry behaviour is pinned as a test and named.
- **(b)** Only the additive fields and the health field, no mismatch state (information only). **(c)** Nothing.
- **Recommendation: (a) with (a-i).** Under (b) the GUI shows versions but still reuses an incompatible daemon; under (c) the question returns the first time a daemon and an app are built from different commits (Part I's E7 already does that deliberately).
- **Rev 3.14 (the ZCode audit, A-18, A-19, A-20; Z6-01, Z6-06).** **(1) Applied, merged into C-09's sixth case (A-18).** Why the unresponsive window exists: the daemon takes its lock (`apps/openalpacad/src/main.rs:118`), binds (`:210`) and writes `discovery.json` (`:222`), and only then opens the database (`:228`; a refusal there exits with the file written), reconciles every MCP server (`:381` — concurrently, `apps/openalpacad/src/managers/mcp.rs:1702`, each bounded by `connect_timeout_secs`, 30 s by default, `config/mcp.toml:10`), starts plugins (`:439`) and connectors (`:649`), and serves (`:785`). Today the shell's 300 ms TCP test (`apps/openalpaca-gui/src-tauri/src/lib.rs:257-268`) reads a daemon in that window as alive, and the webview's first requests hang; under (a) the health probe fails there, so without C-09's case `decide_reuse` would spawn a second daemon, which meets the lock while the first is still booting. The case is shown as **starting** ("daemon is starting"): the shell parks and retries within a bounded budget, never spawns, never rotates `daemon.log` and never probes the singleton lock, and on timeout it reports with the log tail. The budget is derived from a measurement — a real store's boot with its MCP servers configured, which nobody has measured (Q00's 73 ms came from a fixture with none; Appendix B #51) — and stated in Q04's body; this plan writes no number (§7.5's rule). The audit corrects one claim of its own source: the GUI's reconnect ladder spawns only through bootstrap or an `instance_id` change (`apps/openalpaca-gui/src/lib/events.ts:439-462`; `lib/connection.ts:319-331`), not at every step. **(2) Applied (A-20's apply half).** A seventh row, the same protocol with a different `build.version`: reuse, with a note naming both versions and the Stop then Start that replaces the daemon — never a restart, which the daemon-lifecycle rule forbids (nothing restarts the daemon on its own, `CLAUDE.md:76`, `:80`). `version` is already in `discovery.json` and becomes a `ConnectionInfo` field under (a), so the row adds nothing to the wire and Q01's pin is untouched. Every build today carries the workspace version `0.1.0` (`Cargo.toml:38`), so the row fires only once releases move the version; G-ident's hash is not on the wire. **(3) Pending, not adopted** (`tasks/pending-decisions.md` §4.3). *Discovery timing* (A-19, Z6-01 (ii)): write `discovery.json` just before `axum::serve`, add a phase field to it, or add an early health route; C-09's row is the no-change answer. A phase field changes the bytes Q01 pins (`790bee6c`), so that test's literal would move in the same commit, and it would widen §7.8's one additive exception; it wants an answer before Q04. *Installer prompt* (A-20's other half, Z6-06): `install.sh` already stops the running daemon before it replaces the app (`scripts/release/install.sh:159-184`, called at `:309`); whether it first prints the daemon's `busy` counts and asks is the owner's, and no Q commit carries it.

**DU-30 — `prepare-sidecar.ts` and the three release scripts (G2); and when.** [facts: §3.3; architect §5]
- **(a)** Rewrite `prepare-sidecar.ts`: always run `cargo build -p openalpacad --locked [--release] [--target <T>] --message-format=json-render-diagnostics` and stage the `executable` of the `compiler-artifact` message for `openalpacad`'s bin target (correct for `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, `[build] target-dir`, `--target`; never tauri-build's copy); remove `shouldRebuild`; an explicit `--reuse <path>` for development only (refused with `--release`; built without a path — the *Built* line below, A1-15); in release mode write `src-tauri/bin/openalpacad-<triple>.sha256` and a `.provenance.json` (HEAD, lock sha256, profile, target, features, rustc) **before** `tauri build`; a GUI-only `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` (`target/gui`) by default. And the three package scripts: call `prepare-sidecar.ts --release` first, copy the staged sidecar into `libexec/` (one artifact, one hash), build the CLI alone, `bun install --frozen-lockfile`, `--locked` everywhere; verify the bundled copy against the `.sha256` after `tauri build`. **(b)** Only the script's freshness fix (cargo decides; cargo's artifact path), leaving the joint build. **(c)** Nothing: E5/E7 keep rev 2.1's hand sequence.
- **When:** (w-i) before P04, so every identity check in the wave (G-ident, E5, E7) is mechanical; (w-ii) after the wave.
- *Built (rev 3.5), (a) with (w-i):* `bc0e6963`, `71c6e243`, `c5922704` — with rev 3.5's ruling 1 (no-path `--reuse`, the record for every profile, the variable as the dev switch) and ruling 2 (Linux's bundled copy reported, `libexec/` held).
- **Recommendation: (a) with (w-i)** — Q01–Q03 are script and test work that touch no dependency. P37's `--locked`/`--frozen-lockfile` edits fold into Q03 if Q03 lands first. Under (c) E5 and E7 run the hand sequence of §6 (hash the daemon-only release build before `tauri build`, compare bundled and `libexec/`).

**DU-31 — Native CI: a shell-check matrix per PR, a native-smoke nightly (G8); cost.** [facts: architect §7.2; `ci.yml:27-31`, `:37-58`, `:64-78` — `:42-46`, `:48-74`, `:82-98` at `3598a6fb`]
- **(a)** `shell-check` ×3 (`macos-latest`, `windows-latest`, `ubuntu-latest` + webkit2gtk/appindicator/rsvg dev packages) on every PR: `cargo check -p openalpaca_gui --locked` and `cargo clippy -p openalpaca_gui --all-targets --locked` with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target/gui`, an **empty placeholder** at `src-tauri/bin/openalpacad-<triple>` created only in the fresh checkout and deleted after (tauri-build errors when the file is absent [D: `tauri-build-2.6.3/src/lib.rs:41-45`]); after Q06 also `cargo test -p openalpaca_app_support`. This makes DU-18 (b) permanent and widens it to the shell; P01a/P40 are dropped. *(The Windows and Linux legs, of shell-check and native-smoke alike, are deferred under DU-33, rev 3.7.)* Plus `native-smoke` ×3 **nightly and on release tags**: a real daemon build, `prepare-sidecar.ts --release`, `tauri build`, then launch → `/v1/health` → `instance_id` stable across close/reopen (quit/relaunch: closing the last window quits the app — rev 3.13, C-25) → explicit stop + lifecycle wait → `daemon.log` tail after a deliberately failing start → Reveal on a store file and a project file, refusals on a missing project and a symlink escape (WebDriver where it works; macOS may only assert launch/reuse/stop) — gaps labelled per OS. **(b)** `shell-check` per PR only. **(c)** Nothing beyond Part I's P01a/P40.
- Cost [NE, reasoned]: `shell-check` cold 10–20 min, warm 2–5 min per OS; `native-smoke` cold 25–45 min per OS (the daemon's 364-crate release build). macOS minutes bill 10×, Windows 2× on a private repository, free on a public one; this repository's visibility is [NE] — it decides the cadence.
- Sub-row — **the supported OS and webview floors, all three platforms** (rev 3.1; the 10-10 audit's G8 asks to "define supported OS/webview versions" and rev 3 carried only DU-26's Windows 7 sub-row): no floor is written anywhere today for macOS (WKWebView), Windows (WebView2) or Linux (webkit2gtk) [C: none found]. (f-a) Adopt the floors tauri 2.12's own documentation states for its three webviews, quoted into the release notes and the README when the decision is taken — those floors were **not read today** [NE], so this plan does not state them; (f-b) stricter floors of the owner's choosing (the machines the app is meant for); (f-c) no floor, today's state, with the Windows 7 question answered by DU-26 alone. Recommendation (f-a) (rev 3.1–3.8). **Rev 3.9: an owner question.** (f-a) read literally declares macOS 10.13 (the bundle default [D: `tauri-utils-2.10.1/src/config.rs:694-696`; Docs: v2.tauri.app/distribute/macos-application-bundle/]), but Tailwind CSS v4 is "designed for Safari 16.4+" and Vite 8's default target is Safari 16.4 [Docs: tailwindcss.com upgrade guide; vite.dev migration guide], and WKWebView is the system WebKit — the app cannot run on the floor (f-a) declares. **(f-b1)** `minimumSystemVersion` 13.3 — Tauri's webview-versions table gives macOS 13.3 → WebKit 615.1.26.11.22 → Safari 16.4 [Docs: tauri-docs `reference/webview-versions.md`] — with Vite 8's default `build.target` and `tsconfig` at its ES year; **(f-b2)** 11.0 plus a stated "Safari 16.4 or later" requirement in the README and the release notes (macOS 11 and 12 reach 16.4 only through a Safari update, which `LSMinimumSystemVersion` cannot express [Docs: Apple's Safari 16.4 release notes]); a lower build target is not available (Tailwind v4 needs 16.4). **Recommendation (f-b1)**; **Q18** applies the answer (rev 3.13, G-06: "the release notes" in (f-a) and (f-b2) means the README and the *Scope* and *Build Machine Requirements* sections of `docs/Installation_Manual.md`, since no release-notes file exists; Q18a writes them). The Windows half stays DU-26's sub-row; Linux's is deferred (DU-33). *Rev 3.13 (C-18, G-06):* two facts the question omitted — each package is built for its build host's architecture only (`scripts/release/package-macos.sh:103-107` takes `rustc -vV`'s host triple, `aarch64-apple-darwin` or `x86_64-apple-darwin`, and `apps/openalpaca-gui/scripts/prepare-sidecar.ts:39-40` refuses `universal-apple-darwin`), so a release built on Apple silicon excludes Intel Macs, which `docs/Installation_Manual.md` promises; and Q18's Info.plist half does not depend on P32 (Tailwind 4.3.3 already requires Safari 16.4), so Q18 splits — `minimumSystemVersion` once the number is given, the Vite/tsconfig half after P32 (§7.3).
- **Recommendation: (a)**, nightly for native-smoke. Under (b) WKWebView/WebView2/webkit2gtk rendering stays DU-14 (w-a)/(w-b); under (c) DU-18 (a) stands.
- **Rev 3.14 (the ZCode audit, A-22, A-23, A-29; Z6-07, Z7-01, Z7-11).** **(1) Applied (A-22).** G1's acceptance becomes a standing guard rather than a one-time gate: from Q06 on, Q08's macOS job and a script a person runs locally assert Q06's two `cargo tree` conditions on every run (§7.3 Q08, §7.6, §9). **(2) Pending, not adopted** — *macOS CI job* (A-23, Z7-01; `tasks/pending-decisions.md` §4.3). CI builds, tests and lints only on ubuntu (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:17-46`), so the macOS-only code is compiled by no CI job — the `getattrlistbulk` scan (`crates/openalpaca_storage/src/memory_files/dirstat.rs:23`), iMessage (`crates/openalpaca_connectors/src/lib.rs:16`) and keychain discovery (`crates/openalpaca_llm/src/keys/credential_discovery/mod.rs:448`) — and Q08's macOS leg covers the shell crate only. The option: a `rust-macos` job on a pinned label running the CI set's build, test and clippy with `--locked`, as a pull-request check or on `schedule` and `workflow_dispatch`, with `modernization-plan.md` M65's warnings gate on both operating systems (SF-28); its cost is the 10× macOS rate above. ZCode runs its primary platforms automatically and its deferred ones by hand, non-blocking — doc-level only, since ZCode's CI configuration is not in its export (audit §7). *DU-33 (a) reopened* (A-23's separate question): whether Q08's Windows and Linux legs are written now as non-blocking, dispatch-only jobs — a reopening of DU-33 (a), presented apart and never folded into this row. **(3) Pending, not adopted** — *Minos gate* (A-29, Z7-11): with (c)-2's floor, read the `LC_BUILD_VERSION` minos of every Mach-O in the `.app` and in `libexec/` and require it at or below the floor, and set `MACOSX_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET` explicitly for the sidecar and CLI builds, which are cargo invocations apart from `tauri build`. Rust's default minos for `aarch64-apple-darwin` is probably already below 13.3 — unverified (audit §7; Appendix B #55) — so the gate's value is as a guard (§7.3 Q18).

**DU-33 — Platform priority: macOS first (owner, 2026-10-12).** [rev 3.7]

What the sentence covers is the open question: **(a)** every Linux and Windows verification is deferred, local and CI-only alike — rev 3.6's open local runs, P01a/P40 (the wave's temporary Windows `cargo check`), Q08's Windows and Linux legs and DU-31's native-smoke on those systems — while CI's existing jobs (ubuntu as CI's host; the windows-latest Pester job on every pull request and every push to `main` — `ci.yml:3-6`; rev 3.7 wrote "on every push", corrected in rev 3.13, SF-2), P01b/P39 and the release scripts' Linux and Windows halves stay as they are; **(b)** only the local runs are deferred, and P01a/P40 and Q08's three legs stay scheduled; **(c)** the existing windows-latest Pester job is removed too.

Under (b) the wave's Windows compile is watched from P01 on; under (c) the Windows installers lose their only test.

**Rev 3.14 — the ZCode audit's Part II decisions, pending** (`tasks/research/zcode-audit-2026-10-17.md` §4.2). Each is recorded in `tasks/pending-decisions.md` §4.3, *Raised by the ZCode audit*, under the name given here, with ZCode's evidence and the audit's recommendation; none is adopted, and the no-change answer is this plan as written. Each wants its answer by the point named:
- *Discovery timing* (A-19) — before Q04 (DU-29's rev 3.14 bullet).
- *Installer prompt* (A-20's present half) — no deadline in this plan; `install.sh` is no Q commit's (DU-29).
- *DevServerHost proxy* (A-21) — before Q07 (DU-28).
- *macOS CI job* and, apart, *DU-33 (a) reopened* (A-23) — before Q08 (DU-31, DU-33).
- *App PATH* (A-24, Z6-02) — no deadline; a shell commit beside Q12 and Q14 would carry it if taken. The shell spawns the sidecar with its own inherited environment plus two variables (`apps/openalpaca-gui/src-tauri/src/lib.rs:344-350`), and the daemon resolves every external program through `PATH`: MCP stdio servers (`crates/openalpaca_mcp/src/transport/stdio.rs:64`), the CLI backends (`crates/openalpaca_llm/src/cli_backend/mod.rs:54`, `:154`), `sh -c` (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/tools/platform.rs:20`) and skill interpreters (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/tools/builtins/mod.rs:576`). An app opened from Finder inherits launchd's minimal environment — reasoned, not measured (audit §7; Appendix B #52) — so `npx`, `uvx`, Homebrew's `node` and `claude` may resolve for `openalpaca daemon start` and not for the app's daemon. Options: **(a)** the shell captures the login environment once, asynchronously, with start and end markers, a deadline, a process-group kill and an allowlist of keys, and patches `spawn_daemon` (ZCode's form, ZCode:packages/services/src/runtime-tools/runtimeLoginShellEnvCapture.ts:6-11, :54-101, :173-216); **(b)** the daemon captures it when its `PATH` is launchd's default; **(c)** a fixed bootstrap list — ZCode's darwin default is `/usr/local/bin:/opt/homebrew/bin:/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin` (`runtimeLoginShellEnvCapture.ts:8-10`; the audit's list adds `~/.cargo/bin`, which ZCode's does not carry); **(d)** document only, which P38 does now (A-41). The audit recommends (a) with (c) as its fallback; the source of `PATH` announced in `daemon.log` and in Settings → Connection — where it came from and how long the capture took, never its value, as ZCode logs it (ZCode:docs/desktop/startup-critical-path.md:96); and a decision on the credential-pointer keys (`AWS_PROFILE`, `GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS`, `KUBECONFIG`), which ZCode inherits. API keys are never inherited this way, so `secret_env` stays a terminal-launch feature. (a) runs the person's login shell at every app start, which is why it is the owner's.
- *P19 protocol key* (A-27) — this plan's DU-25 (m), item (m-g): the negotiated protocol version and the connect and list durations on the extension row and in `openalpaca ext info`; before P19 (§4.3). Appendix B #4 and #42 are read from that row once it lands (A-06).
- *Minos gate* (A-29) — with (c)-2's floor, before Q18a (DU-31).
- *B-22* (A-30, with A-08) — THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES generated from the two lockfiles, their hashes recorded and checked, bundled as a Tauri resource and in the archive; before any beta distribution, near Q03 and Q09 (§5.4's B table). Which tool generates the Rust notices is not chosen (audit §7). Deterministic archives wait until the bundle is reproducible: `package-macos.sh` embeds `built_at_utc` in the archive's manifest (`scripts/release/package-macos.sh:202-210`), and bsdtar's flags for a deterministic tar are unverified (Z7-12; Appendix B #56).

## 5. Part I — the dependency wave: commit plan

### 5.1 Before the first commit: owner actions

1. **Take the decisions**: DU-1, DU-2, DU-13, DU-23, DU-25, DU-26 at least (they fix the base, the toolchain, the Bun pin, the versions, rmcp's shape and the Tauri sequence); DU-24 (opener); DU-4, DU-6, DU-10, DU-21 as rev 2.1 asked; Part II's DU-27…DU-32 can wait, except DU-30 (w-i) if the provenance work is to precede P04. *Done: the owner took DU-1…DU-32 on 2026-10-11 (rev 3.4), DU-33 on 2026-10-12 (rev 3.7).* *Rev 3.15: D1 (a), D2 (a) and D3 (a) taken on 2026-10-17 (§4.3's DU-23, §4.2's DU-2); the rest of the list below stays pending.* **Rev 3.13 — the 2026-10-17 review's owner decisions D1–D13** (`tasks/deps-upgrade/reports/2026-10-17-plan-review.md` §6; each recorded in `tasks/pending-decisions.md`), none adopted here, each needed by the point named: **before the cut** — D1 the cut instant and the Tauri set (DU-23 (a), DU-26 (a′)), D2 rustls 0.23.46, D3 Rust 1.99.1 (DU-2 against DU-23) — *all three taken on 2026-10-17 (rev 3.15): D1 (a) 2026-10-21T19:53Z, D2 (a) a waiver that never triggers, D3 (a) 1.99.0 at P03 and 1.99.1 at P03b*; **D13** Q00's owed GUI rows (before P03, item 11); **D6** refusing cross-origin MCP redirects (before its own fix commit, which may land before the cut, §3.3); **D11** SQLite vendoring (before P06, §3.1); **D10** test-bot tokens (before P28 and P41, item 12); **D5** P19b (before P19b, DU-25); **D4** serde-saphyr 2.0 (before P30, DU-4; rev 3.15: the compatibility report §5 is the research its recommendation asked for — evidence, not the answer); **D12** DU-13 (b)'s Bun (before P35, DU-13); **D7** Q04's refusal for a symlinked ancestor directory (before Q04, §7.3); **D8** Reveal as a daemon route (before Q06, DU-27 (e)); **D9** the CSP's loopback wildcard and the daemon's permissive CORS (no deadline; DU-28). **Rev 3.14 — the ZCode audit's owner decisions** (`tasks/research/zcode-audit-2026-10-17.md` §4–§5; each recorded in `tasks/pending-decisions.md`), none adopted here; those with a Part I deadline: **DU-25 (m)**, the per-server lifecycle mode and the named negotiation failure (A-02, A-03, A-27) — before P19 (§4.3); ***P19 descendant walk***, whether teardown also signals a `setsid` escapee found by a parent-pid walk (raised by this revision from Z5-14's conditional walk, beside A-04, not by the audit; its row is `tasks/pending-decisions.md` rev 5 §4.3's *P19 descendant walk*) — before P19 (P19's gate, item (4)); **D6**'s third form, refusing every redirect when credentials are configured (A-13) — with D6, whose commit is now N-MCP-2's (§5.4; the paragraph after P23); **B-22**, the licence policy in `deny.toml` (A-08) — before P34; **A-09**'s stub gates — with D10, before P28 and P41; **A-15**, pinned runner labels, which drops P39 — before P39. B-23 (the tooling job, A-12) and the rest of B-01…B-27 have no Part I deadline (§5.4's table). Part II's, each with its deadline, are listed at §4.4's end (A-19, A-20's installer half, A-21, A-23, A-24, A-29, A-30).
2. **Local Bun at exactly the pinned version, beside the one the owner has** (DU-13; rev 3.1): `BUN_INSTALL="$HOME/.bun-1.4.2" bash -c 'curl -fsSL https://bun.com/install | bash -s "bun-v1.4.2"'` — the install script honours `BUN_INSTALL` as the install root [Docs: bun.com/docs/installation] — and `~/.bun-1.4.2/bin` first on `PATH` in the wave's shells; `bun upgrade` installs the newest release, not the pin [Docs]. **Keep the 1.3.13 install until P35's first bun PR has succeeded**: DU-13 (a)'s condition falls back to (b), and (b) needs a Bun 1.3.x to write a v1 lock from scratch (rev 3 had the owner install 1.4.2 over `~/.bun` and keep a 1.3.x "under (b)" only — the fallback would then have had no Bun to run on). The research never touched the owner's Bun (1.3.13); today's locks were made with a scratch 1.4.2 under `SP/bun142/`. *Rev 3.13 (SF-29): the state this item describes no longer exists — the machine holds only `~/.bun`, at 1.4.3 (the owner's default since rev 3.7); `~/.bun-1.4.2` does not exist and no Bun 1.3.x is installed. From P42 on the pin is 1.4.3, which is the owner's default, so no side install is needed and the 1.4.2 side install is dropped. DU-13 (b)'s fallback needs a fresh Bun 1.3.x in its own `BUN_INSTALL` root — before P35 or when it triggers, owner decision D12, pending (DU-13, §4.2).*
3. **The DU-10 count and the E6 no-print checks** of every real config file and skill directory, with the **rebuilt** comparators (§6 E6) — before P04 (toml_edit 0.25.x arrives there), P12, P29 and P30. *Rev 3.13:* **(SF-14)** the DU-10 count covers every file that can hold a keychain reference, not `llm.toml` alone — the daemon hands the plugin manager its secret store (`apps/openalpacad/src/main.rs:433`), which writes keychain items named `openalpaca-plugin-<name>-<key>` (`crates/openalpaca_plugins/src/manager.rs:2292`) and records each as an inline table, `key = { secret_ref = … }`, in `plugins/.config/<name>.toml` (`permission_gate.rs:446`), a spelling the line-anchored grep of `tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/e6/README.md`'s loop also misses. The count is of `secret_ref` keys at any depth, read with a TOML parser, over `llm.toml` (both copies), `plugins/.config/*.toml` and `plugins/.permissions.toml`, counts only; until the loop does that, P12's waiver of its keychain round trip needs a 0 on both the `llm.toml` count and a plugin-config count. *Rev 3.14:* `keycount`, a fifth E6 tool, makes that count with toml 0.9.12, the parser the daemon reads these files with until P29 (`tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/e6/README.md`); it is built in the tree and lands with this revision's commit. **(A2-16)** E6 runs here, at the consent point — not in §6's after-P38 list — with its toolchain taken from `rust-toolchain.toml` (typedprobe's `cargo +1.93.0` fails on a tree after P03). **(F-08)** At the same point typedprobe is built twice, from the branch point and from the prep tree (the wave's manifests, toml 1.1), and reports index plus ok/err per build, so the real files are loaded under toml 1.1 before any live start.
4. **Backups before the first start of a bumped build on the real store** — rev 2.1 §5.1 item 4 unchanged: stop the daemon; copy `state/openalpaca.db` with `-wal`/`-shm`, every real `config/*.toml` (both copies), `plugins/.permissions.toml`, `plugins/.config/`. *Rev 3.13 (SF-1): that set is rev 2.1's and the moment too early.* The set leaves out exactly what P10 (the `hash` module: revision hashes, partition keys), P11 (aes-gcm) and P12 (the keychain store) touch — `state/.master_key` (it decrypts `llm.toml`'s `secret_encrypted`), `state/memory-control/` (the notes' authority; a missing or invalid snapshot quarantines its partition), `memory/` and `projects/*/{project.json,memory/}`, `config/orchestrator/*.md` (USER.md among them), and the keychain items when `use_keychain` is on; and Chunk 25's backup item stands before P42, days before the first bumped start, so a restore would lose that interval's rows and notes. So, all of it the owner's (no agent reads `~/.openalpaca`): **immediately before the first start of a bumped build on the real store** — the daemon stopped, its exit and the lock's release awaited (`openalpaca_storage::daemon_lifecycle`) — copy the whole store, `cp -Rp ~/.openalpaca <backup>` (leaving out `state/cache/` and `logs/` if wanted), or keep E8's full copy instead of deleting it at E8's step 9; keep the backup at mode 0700, outside any repository, and delete it at the latest when the wave closes; record a checksum list (counts and hashes, never contents) and whether `use_keychain` is on — keychain items are not in the copy, and pointer rows (`in_project = 1`) refer to real project files the backup does not cover; write a restore procedure (stop, move the live store aside, copy back, open with the pre-wave build) and rehearse it once on a scratch copy; Chunk 25's backup item moves after E8. The wave adds **no** migration (the database is at schema 4 before and after); the SQLite files are portable both ways by September's measurement on the old schemas [M-sep] and are re-measured on the current ones by E8 before that first start.
5. **The freshness rule, applied on the day of the cut** (DU-23): every target is ≥ 7 days old, or the newest release that is; §1.6 is the list for 2026-10-10 and goes stale daily. Re-run `cargo update --dry-run --verbose` and `bun outdated` at the cut; a newer *patch* of a researched target goes through its commit's gate; a new *major* is outside this plan's evidence (pin the researched major, or research first). **A cut on or after 2026-10-17 takes the measured set.** **Rev 3.9:** the cut **takes** `bun@1.4.3` (P42, its own commit; eligible 2026-10-17T05:24:54Z) and every newer release DU-23's minute table makes eligible, and is recommended at or after 2026-10-17T20:08Z, when the whole Tauri pair is eligible. **Rev 3.13 (MF-1, MF-2, SF-9).** The cut is **the cut instant** (DU-23: pending D1, recommended 2026-10-21T19:53Z (12:53 PDT); until answered, rev 3.9's 2026-10-17T20:08Z (13:08 PDT)) — "a cut on or after 2026-10-17 takes the measured set" above is superseded, and so is "the whole Tauri pair": Tauri's 2026-10-14 train is eligible only at D1's recommended instant. **At the cut:** DU-23's minute table is re-derived from the registries; every commit that moves a lock passes the freshness gate (§5.2); the prep locks are re-pinned to eligible versions — as integrated, the prep lock holds cc 1.8.0, serde_json 1.0.154, syn 3.0.8, zopfli 0.8.4, unicode-ident 1.0.27 and zip 9.0.2 and is eligible only from 2026-10-19T04:20:27Z; a caret floor in a manifest is the version taken — P16's `zip = { version = "9.0.2", … }` (`refs/prep/deps-wave-2026-10-12:Cargo.toml:164`) excludes 9.0.1, the only eligible 9.0.x at 2026-10-17T20:08Z, so its floor becomes the version taken and the lock follows with `cargo update -p zip@<locked> --precise <it>`, and likewise for P10's `sha2 = "0.11"` (which resolves to 0.11.1, 2026-10-12T18:58Z) and P12's `keyring-core = "1.0"` (1.0.1, 2026-10-13T00:02Z) when the lock is regenerated; each step-back is measured on the day, because they cascade, and no list from the review or from this plan is copied. A new *major* found this way is recorded in DU-23's table and in its commit and researched before it lands, or its blocker named with its way out — serde-saphyr 2.0.0 (D4, P30), jsonschema 0.59.0 (`modernization-plan.md` M82, an API and feature check: default features off, no remote `$ref`) and oxlint 1.88.0 (M66) are the three found so far. **Before the merge** (MF-2 4): a freshness sweep — each release that became eligible after the cut instant gets its own commit with its gate, or a named blocker (§6). **Rev 3.15 (D1 (a); the compatibility report §2, §3.2, C3, §6.1).** The cut instant is **2026-10-21T19:53Z (12:53 PDT)** and every freshness gate's max date **2026-10-14T19:53:00Z**; the rev 3.13 sentences above that read "pending D1" or name 2026-10-17T20:08Z are records. No release published after the max date can become eligible, so the set the cut takes already exists, and the report measured it on 2026-10-17 (DU-23's rev 3.15 table); only a yank before the cut changes it, so the yank scans and the freshness gate re-run on the cut day (the report's §7). **The prep re-pin** (C3), each floor the version the lock takes: P41 `rustls = { version = "0.23.46", default-features = false, features = ["aws_lc_rs"] }` (prep `96a1dc30` writes `"0.23"`, `refs/prep/deps-wave-2026-10-12:Cargo.toml:148`, which admits every rustls release RUSTSEC-2026-0349 affects); P10 `sha2 = "0.11.1"` (prep `"0.11"`, `:156`); P12 `keyring-core = "1.0.1"` (prep `"1.0"`, `:170`); P16 `zip = { version = "9.0.3", default-features = false, features = ["deflate"] }` with `cargo update -p zip@<locked> --precise 9.0.3`, never below 9.0.1 (prep `"9.0.2"`, `:164`; C7); and P27a `opener = "0.9"`, which the prep tip lacks — it still writes `opener = "0.8"` (`:175`), its re-cut `6992a402` never having touched it, so "the prep carries every planned Rust move" is wrong for this one item. The prep lock (eligible from 2026-10-19T04:20:27Z) is eligible at the instant; the report's cut-day `cargo update` from it moved 25 entries and added `static_vcruntime 3.0.0`, and its step-backs — 11 `--precise` steps over 13 releases, none a Tauri crate (the report's §3.2) — are the wave's lower bound from the prep tip, each met by the first commit whose lock change offers it and printed by that commit's gate on the day (P04's refresh eight of the thirteen; P11, P12 and P16, which bring the other five's crates in, the rest — §5.3's P04). **New majors at the instant:** serde-saphyr 2.0.0, eligible and researched (the report's §5), D4 pending; jsonschema 0.59.0, too young (published 2026-10-16T20:30:14Z), so M82 takes 0.58.6, the version it names; oxlint 1.88.0, eligible (M66 names 1.87.0 — `modernization-plan.md`'s entry). The releases the cut refuses — react-query 5.104.3, jsdom 30.1.3, vitest 5.0.5, Rust 1.99.1 (P03b) and the transitive rest of DU-23's rev 3.15 table — are this sweep's.
