Before P42/P03: push, open a draft PR against `main`, and record the CI run URLs for P01, P01b and P02 in Chunk 25. Read CI a second time after P12, P13 and P08, before P15, and record each run — its clippy log also shows Linux-only warnings before M39 converts any `allow` (SF-28). P35's gate and DU-13 (a)'s condition are judged after the merge (DU-13, §4.2). *Rev 3.14 (A-17, optional; Z7-09):* the owner may also land a `workflow_dispatch` trigger on `main` through its own small PR (the owner's push; GitHub offers a manual trigger only for a workflow whose file is on the default branch, which the audit did not re-check against GitHub's docs) and then dispatch with `gh workflow run ci.yml --ref chore/toolchain-and-deps`; the draft PR is opened either way.
10. **Land the prep commits by rewriting them, not by rebasing them** (rev 3.13, SF-3). `refs/prep/deps-wave-2026-10-12` (base `6992a402`, tip `7e702582`; never pushed) is not a series that rebases into the P commits: its base is a scratch tree ("not for landing") that already carries every version move (`git diff d7c6bbc6 6992a402`: 30 files, +1,713/−1,288), and the prep commits are rev 3.9's deltas over it — `b899046c` ("sha2 0.11") touches no sha2 manifest line, `77d106ec` (P15) and `154b679d` (P18) touch no manifest, and `5c265232`, `44ec0b03` and `88394660` move only zip 8.6 → 9.0.2, keyring 4.2 `v1` → keyring-core and rmcp 3.5.0 → 3.5.1. Each P commit is therefore its G-base step's anchored edits and patch hunks plus the prep delta, rewritten onto the real tip, passed through the freshness gate (§5.2) and re-gated. The pure deltas: P10 `b899046c`, P12 `44ec0b03`, P15 `77d106ec`, P16 `5c265232`, P18 `154b679d`, P19 `88394660`, P19b `e87c038d`, P41 `96a1dc30`. `c7e03960` is `9ec986ec` (identical by range-diff) and is dropped; P17 `75358018`, P17a `ea8f0886`, P36 `ac6db26c` and M08 `7e702582` move no version and are rewritten onto the tip and re-gated the same way. The prep anchors — 5,126/0/17, clippy 0, the fmt counts — were measured on a newer lock than the cut admits (item 5), so they are expectations to re-measure, not results; each entry's own divergences from its prep body are folded into §5.3 (A1-05), and P19b's entry's dangling `73df38f5`/`88d9de68` become `e87c038d`/`88394660`. Failure here is loud — conflicts or failing gates — not silent.
11. **Q00's owed GUI rows** (rev 3.13, SF-5): the baseline README's *What a person still owes the baseline* — first paint and first useful content, hidden/minimized memory, the streamed answer with dropped frames, the 100-message scroll, three previews — is comparable only on `a91f4b23` and due before P03; G6's budget and G4's profile depend on it. An owner checkbox before P42/P03, one of: take the rows under §5.2's confined in-app Start on `a91f4b23`; record that they will be taken later on a rebuilt `a91f4b23` bundle; or record them given up, Q10 and G4–G6 then comparing only what Q00 measured. Owner decision **D13**, pending (recommended: do not hold the cut; take them later on a rebuilt bundle under the corrected launcher). Scenario (7), window close/reopen, is quit/relaunch, which Q00 measured (C-25, §7.5). *Rev 3.14 (the ZCode audit, A-25, A-26):* Q14's startup marks could take the first-paint and first-useful-content rows by instrument, but only through a measurement build of `a91f4b23` carrying those marks (§7.5's rule (2)), since `a91f4b23`'s shell has none; which of the three answers holds stays D13's.
12. **Test-bot tokens** (rev 3.13, SF-12): P28's gate is a live Telegram bot round trip, its token the named blocker, and P41's a live Discord connect, which Chunk 25 had reduced to "if a token exists". The owner creates a Telegram and a Discord test bot, or records a waiver per gate — owner decision **D10**, pending (recommended: wait). §6's "what CI cannot cover" table gains a Discord row.
13. **Pinned tools in a scratch root** (rev 3.13, G-05): cargo-audit, cargo-deny, zizmor and shellcheck are not installed (`~/.cargo/bin` holds only the rustup proxies) and no step provisions them, though P01, P04, P34 and E2 run them. Before the cut, install pinned versions with `cargo install --locked --root <scratch>/tools` (or use P34's CI form), record each version in the log of every run that uses it, and leave `~/.cargo` untouched.
14. **The pdf-extract issue** (rev 3.13, A1-16): P15's `#[ignore = "… upstream <issue>"]` points at an issue nobody has filed. The owner files it with the two synthetic fixtures (fixtures only; the owner posts), and its URL goes into the ignore reason before P15, or the reason records why not.

### 5.2 Conventions for every commit

Rev 2.1 §5.2's conventions stand (conventional prefixes; a `Verified:` line; the attribution lines; docs that cite a version change in the commit that changes it; the lock produced by cargo in sequence; test hygiene with a sandboxed `HOME`/`OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE`/`OPENALPACA_CONFIG_DIR`, `RUSTUP_HOME`/`CARGO_HOME` at their real values, `CARGO_TERM_COLOR=never`, output to files, orphaned `sleep 3600` children killed; the seven-item isolation rule for every manual daemon, CLI or app run). Rev 3 adds and corrects:

- **The scratch root is canonical from the moment it is made** (rev 3.1; lessons 2026-10 — rev 2.1 §5.2 item 1, `plan.md:929`, wrote `T=$(mktemp -d)`): `T=$(realpath "$(mktemp -d)")`, once, and every expected path is built from `$T` — the store root Settings → Connection and `GET /v1/status` show (E5 step 4), `project.json`'s `root`, the rows' `project_root` and `storage_path`, E8's snapshot roots. On macOS `/tmp` and `/var/folders/…` both lead into `/private/`, and the daemon records canonical paths, so a literal comparison against `mktemp`'s spelling fails on every correct answer.
- **Onboarding first on a fresh store** (rev 3.1; lessons 2026-10): a fresh store seeds `BOOTSTRAP.md`, and while it exists every turn is onboarding (`Orchestrator::is_bootstrapping`, `crates/openalpaca_core/src/orchestrator/mod.rs:698` [C]) — `start_workflow` and the per-request tools are withheld. E4, E5 and §7.5 therefore begin with one attended turn asking for `update_persona` on both documents and a check that `BOOTSTRAP.md` is gone, before their first check; a refusal made during onboarding is not an attempt.
- **A sandboxed `HOME` does not keep a test off the machine's secrets** (rev 3.12): a fallback for a missing file still reaches the machine — here the keychain item — and an empty `HOME` guarantees the file is missing. A test reaches credential discovery only through `DiscoveryHost` (`crates/openalpaca_llm/src/keys/credential_discovery/mod.rs`, `9ec986ec`), whose `system()` panics in a unit-test build; a new code path that shells out to `security`, the keyring or a credential file takes its host the same way — no log records a `security` call, so the guard is the check. **Residue, recorded:** the guard holds in `openalpaca_llm`'s own unit tests only — a crate that links it builds it without `cfg(test)`, so a daemon test that booted `initialize_services` on a fresh store would run discovery against the machine (`apps/openalpacad/src/services/mod.rs:150`); none does today (`daemon_args` stops at the command line), and the prep agents booted none for this reason. The seeded `llm.toml` has no `[credential_discovery]`, so both sources default on in a real daemon — whose refresh loop repeats the discovery every 30 s for its lifetime (`credential_discovery/mod.rs:119`), on macOS a `security` query for Claude Code's keychain item each time the file is missing; whether they should is T24's ground — presented, not changed (`tasks/pending-decisions.md` rev 3).
- **Every live daemon runs sandboxed from its first launch**: `scripts/eval/memory/loopback-only.sb` (`tasks/lessons.md`), with `OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE` and `OPENALPACA_CONFIG_DIR` under a temp root and credential discovery off in the seeded `llm.toml` (rev 3.13: by the exact pre-seed and log check of the bullet *Credential discovery and the CLI backends are off* below, SF-13). **The mechanism, by launcher** (rev 3.1 — rev 3 named `OPENALPACA_DAEMON_BIN` alone, a variable only the CLI reads [M-today: `grep` over the tree; C: `apps/openalpaca/src/manager.rs:10`]): (i) a daemon started by hand — `sandbox-exec -f scripts/eval/memory/loopback-only.sb <binary>`, its stderr to a file (it writes no `daemon.log`); (ii) a daemon the CLI starts (`openalpaca daemon start`; E7 step 3) — `OPENALPACA_DAEMON_BIN` names a **wrapper script** that does `exec sandbox-exec -f <profile> <real openalpacad> "$@"` (the CLI takes the variable's path as the binary after an is-executable check and no basename check, `manager.rs:241-248`, `:384-392` [C]; `pid_is_daemon` still matches, the exec'd process being `openalpacad`); (iii) the app — the Tauri shell spawns its own staged sidecar and sets only `OPENALPACA_CONFIG_DIR` and `MANAGED_LOG_ENV` on it (`apps/openalpaca-gui/src-tauri/src/lib.rs:349-350` [C]), so the **app itself** is launched under the profile — `sandbox-exec -f scripts/eval/memory/loopback-only.sb <app>/Contents/MacOS/openalpaca_gui` — and the sidecar it spawns inherits it (the profile's header names the app's webview reaching its daemon as one of its two uses [C: `loopback-only.sb:1-3`]; that a `setsid`-detached child keeps the sandbox is [Docs: `sandbox-exec(1)`, the profile binds the process and its descendants] and is **proved, never assumed**, by the check below). The app's executable is started directly, never through `open`/Finder (no environment). **The proof, before the first model turn** (lessons 2026-10): from a shell under the same `sandbox-exec` invocation, a request to `127.0.0.1:<port>/v1/health` succeeds and a request to a public host fails; for launcher (iii) also from a `setsid`-detached child of that shell (`python3 -c 'import os,subprocess; os.setsid(); subprocess.run([...])'`), which is the inheritance the sidecar relies on; and the first chat turn, if one is made, shows a `web_fetch` to a public host refused in the daemon's log. Whether WebKit's XPC helpers inherit the profile is [NE] (Appendix B #41) and does not matter to the daemon's confinement. *Corrected, rev 3.13 (C-03; found by Q00, rev 3.8): launcher (iii) cannot carry a GUI check — under `sandbox-exec` on the app, WebKit's Networking process sets its own block-network flag and the webview's loopback connects fail with `EPERM` (8 of 8 launches), so the window never reaches its daemon. Launcher (iii) is kept for the spawn and the cold daemon-ready figure only; a check that the webview connected uses rev 3.8's launcher — the daemon first under launcher (i), the app unsandboxed with the same store variables — and, for any Start from inside the app, the confined in-app Start below. The profile's header comment (`loopback-only.sb:1-3`) still names the webview reaching its daemon as a use and is corrected outside this plan; whether `sandbox-exec` runs on `macos-latest` is [NE].*
- **Every in-app Start is confined** (rev 3.13, MF-4). Under rev 3.8's launcher the daemon is started under launcher (i) and the app runs unsandboxed. A person's Stop then Start in the app — G-bundle leg A's "stop and start", E5 step 5, `modernization-plan.md` M74's gate — goes `startDaemon` (`apps/openalpaca-gui/src/lib/daemon-control.ts:146-151`) → `ensure_daemon_running` (`src/lib/connection.ts:298`) → with no live discovery file, `spawn_daemon()` (`src-tauri/src/lib.rs:39`, `:275-305`), which execs `<exe dir>/openalpacad` under `setsid` setting only `OPENALPACA_CONFIG_DIR` and the managed-log variable (`:349-350`, `:362`) and applies no profile. Every check after the first in-app Start — model turns, the memory flows, Stage 2's file writes — would then run on a daemon with full outbound network, the breach `tasks/lessons.md:87-89` records, and the shell-side two-request proof cannot see it (it proves the shell's sandbox, not a process the shell spawned). So: **(1)** a debug bundle (G-bundle leg A, before Q04): remove the staged sidecar from a copy of the bundle and put a wrapper named `openalpacad` first on `PATH` — `exec sandbox-exec -f scripts/eval/memory/loopback-only.sb <real daemon> "$@"` — which the debug fallback resolves (`lib.rs:295-299`); **(2)** from Q04 on, and for E5's release bundle: run G-ident on the untouched bundle first, then, only in a copy used for the GUI legs, replace `Contents/MacOS/openalpacad` with the wrapper beside `openalpacad.real` and hash `.real` — or do the Start by hand under launcher (i), so that no run that carries a model ever tests an in-app Start; **(3)** after every in-app Start, prove confinement by Q00's method — `sandbox_check(network-outbound)` on the new daemon's pid, a refused public request in its log, and a `ps` check that the running pid is the confined one. The key-persists check stays on this launcher (under leg B the webview cannot reach its daemon, Q00). Before M77 the unsandboxed window can follow external links, so the Markdown-link checks point at loopback URLs. The baseline README's sentence that "the only process with outbound reach beyond loopback stays confined" holds only until the first unconfined in-app Start and is corrected outside this plan.
- **Credential discovery and the CLI backends are off, and the log shows it** (rev 3.13, SF-13, F-06). Before the first launch of every live daemon — every launcher, G-bundle's leg B (whose sidecar would seed its own `llm.toml`, so the file is put in the store before the launch), `tauri dev` and E5 — the store's `config/llm.toml` carries:

  ```toml
  [credential_discovery]
  claude_code = false
  codex = false

  [cli_backends.claude_code]
  enabled = false

  [cli_backends.codex]
  enabled = false
  ```

  The seed template has neither section (`scripts/release/templates/config/llm.toml`). Without them `build_token_manager` runs discovery unless both sources are explicitly `false` (`apps/openalpacad/src/services/llm.rs:178`), at boot and every 30 s (`crates/openalpaca_llm/src/keys/credential_discovery/mod.rs:119`), on macOS falling back to `security find-generic-password` (`:512`); and with no `[cli_backends]` the router registers whatever `ClaudeCodeCliProvider::detect()` / `CodexCliProvider::detect()` find on `PATH` (`crates/openalpaca_llm/src/config/llm_config/router_builder.rs:327-360`), after which a failed Anthropic or OpenAI call falls back to running those CLIs with the daemon's `HOME` (`routing/router/fallback.rs:43`, `:65`); an entry with `enabled = false` registers nothing (`cli_backend/mod.rs:59`, `:159`). `loopback-only.sb` denies only outbound network: it keeps a token from leaving the machine, not from being copied into the test store and its logs. **Pass condition:** the run's `daemon.log` (or the captured stderr of launcher (i)) has no `TokenManager initialized` line (`services/llm.rs:187`) and no `Auto-detected … CLI` or `Registered … CLI backend` line (`router_builder.rs:333`, `:339`, `:350`, `:356`). Live daemons also get a scratch `HOME`. Q00 did both — pre-seeded, log checked — and the plan now says so. A stricter profile that also denies reads under `~/.claude`, `~/.codex` and `~/.openalpaca` would be stronger, and is checked against the app/webview launcher before it is relied on. **Residue (F-06), recorded, not built:** every test router also registers the real CLIs when they are on `PATH`, though no test reaches the fallback today (the `llm_config` tests are Ollama-only); CLI-backend detection gets a host, as credential discovery got `DiscoveryHost`.
- **Never start a daemon against a copy of the owner's store** (E8). The current reason is Stage 2: `file_assets.in_project = 1` rows point at **real project files** (`artifacts/mod.rs:464`); a read refreshes their hash/size from the real bytes (`refresh_project_file`, `:1494-1522`); content resolves under the real `project_root` (`resolve_version_path`, `:2050`, `:2084-2116`); `tools::working_folder` returns the project's own root (`working_folder.rs:157-178`, `:377`) [C]. A copied home store neither copies nor isolates those targets; `HOME`/`OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE` redirect no project root.
- **The owner's own development runs use a temp store until E8 has passed** (rev 3.13, SF-1). `cargo run -p openalpacad` and `bun run tauri dev` default to `~/.openalpaca`; until E8 has passed on the current schemas, a bumped build is started only with `OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE` and `OPENALPACA_CONFIG_DIR` under a temp root, the pre-seed above and the sandbox — the same rule as every other live run, stated for the owner's runs because nothing else stops them reaching the real store before the backup of §5.1 item 4.
- **Synthetic registered projects for every runtime check** that touches files, Open, Reveal, `send`, a missing or re-pointed project, or a delete: a scratch git repository registered through the test daemon's own API (a chat turn or `POST /v1/workspaces`), never a real project. The CLI runs from a scratch directory outside any repository, or with `--global` — the rule stands, with its reason corrected (R31): the CLI resolves its directory through `store::projects::resolve` (`memory.rs:140-143`), nothing is written inside a project's own folder (ST-1), and since Stage 2 the file tools and the shell write the **real** project folder, which makes the rule matter more.
- **One canonical daemon build, hashed before Tauri copies it.** For any packaged check: build `openalpacad` alone (`cargo build --release --locked -p openalpacad`), record its `shasum -a 256` **before** `tauri build` (tauri-build copies the staged sidecar into `<target>/<profile>/openalpacad`), and compare the bundled copy and `libexec/openalpacad` to that hash (G-ident). Q02/Q03 make this mechanical; until then it is the E5 sequence of §6.
- **Cleanup waits for the detached daemon**: the shell detaches it (`src-tauri/src/lib.rs:362` `libc::setsid()`; Windows `DETACHED_PROCESS` `:372-378`; "the daemon still outlives the window" `:396`) [C], so stop the recorded temporary daemon, wait for process exit **and** lock release (`daemon_lifecycle`), then delete `$T` and the throwaway app data.
- **Mind the `managers::mcp` tests' orphans**: list `ps -o pid,ppid,lstart,command -A | grep 'sleep 3600'` after a daemon run and kill only those with ppid 1 started inside the run (today: two per run [M-today]).
- **The freshness gate, on every lock a commit moves** (rev 3.13, MF-2). For JS `bunfig.toml`'s `minimumReleaseAge = 604800` binds every install. For cargo nothing did at `3598a6fb`: `tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/gbase/lockdates.py` printed `(date unavailable: …)` for a failed lookup and exited 0 regardless (`lockdates.py:80-85` there). It gains a gate mode, `--gate --max-date <the cut instant minus 7 days, UTC>`, that exits non-zero for any `(name, version)` the commit adds or moves relative to its parent's lock whose crates.io `created_at` is later than the max date — unless the entry is a recorded DU-23 (c) waiver — fails on a failed lookup, and prints the `--precise` sequence, dependents first. It runs in G-base step 2 and in every commit that moves the lock: P31a, P04, P06–P19b, P25–P30, P41 and later. Until it is built — `tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/gbase/README.md` records whether it is, as §5.1 item 6b's requirements are recorded — the same check is made by hand from `cargo update --dry-run --verbose` and the registries, and recorded in the commit body. *Rev 3.14 (the ZCode audit, A-11; Z7-03, Z7-04):* gate mode is built — `dd677009`, recorded in the gbase README's 2026-10-17 row — so A-11 amends it rather than adding it. Its lookup-failure path already has a test with no network: the self-test's `x-nolookup` case, in which a version the stub date source cannot date ends the run as no verdict (`tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/gbase/gbase.sh:1476-1480`); what is new is a test of the partial-output path (read here as gate output that ends before its verdict) on a recorded registry fixture, never the network, beside the driver's `--self-test`. The DU-23 (c) waivers become one machine-read record by widening the built `--waivers FILE`, today one `name@version  reason` a line (`lockdates.py:52-54`; `read_waivers`, `:207-225`), to four fields: an id, a `package@version` or RUSTSEC id, its reason and the commit that recorded it. The amendment changes `read_waivers` and the docstring, the driver's usage text (`gbase.sh:79-80`), the self-test's `x-waived` case and its waivers file (`:1441`, `:1470-1474`) and the gbase README's `--waivers` lines, where the file's name and format are recorded. Whether P34's advisory ignores are mirrored into the same record is part of A-08's pending decision (B-22, P34). *Rev 3.15 (D1 (a), D3 (a); the compatibility report's C5):* the max date is **2026-10-14T19:53:00Z** for every gate of the wave — the cut instant, 2026-10-21T19:53Z, minus 7 days. **A toolchain** is dated by the later of its stable channel manifest and its GitHub release (DU-2, §4.2), which this gate does not read, so P03 and P03b record that check by hand in their `Verified:` lines. **For JS, `bunfig.toml` alone does not enforce DU-23**: `minimumReleaseAge = 604800` admits the newest version published at least seven days before the *install*, not before the cut instant, and while the direct pins are exact every transitive package floats in its range — so an install after the cut takes transitive releases published after the max date (undici 8.12.2 and @asamuzakjp/css-color 7.1.6 on 2026-10-17; the report's §3.4). So after every JS lock write — P31a's JS half, P31, P32, P33, and `modernization-plan.md` M66, M67 and M69 — `lockdates.py --bun-lock <the new bun.lock> --max-date 2026-10-14T19:53:00Z` runs over every `name@version` of that `bun.lock`, transitive and platform packages included, and fails on any entry published after the max date. At `6d3e47fc` `lockdates.py` took NAME@VERSION arguments only (its usage, `tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/gbase/lockdates.py:8` there; the report's verifier extracted the 218 pairs by hand); this step's driver amendment adds the `--bun-lock` mode — each entry of the lock's `packages` block read by its value's `name@version` and checked as a `--pins npm` pin, with the same verdict and exit status — which the gbase README records; §8's driver row cites its landed lines. An install may also run under a `minimumReleaseAge` of (install time − 2026-10-14T19:53:00Z) in seconds, as the report's JS leg did, so that it takes what the check will pass; the check runs either way, and its result goes into the commit's `Verified:` line. Whether bunfig's install-time rule should govern transitive JS packages instead is a reading of DU-23 for the owner, not adopted; this gate applies DU-23 as written.
- **A version-moving commit names the new majors it pulls in** (rev 3.13, G-07): its body lists each new major version of a transitive crate and the dependency it comes through (§2.3: the prep lock adds 52).
- **`CARGO_BUILD_BUILD_DIR` beside every `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, from M63 on** (rev 3.13, MF-5). `modernization-plan.md` M63 adds `[build] build-dir = "{cargo-cache-home}/build/{workspace-path-hash}"` to the tracked root `.cargo/config.toml`, early in the wave. With a configured build-dir, `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` moves only the final artifacts and the intermediates still go to the build-dir; `cargo clean` removes both [M: the review, cargo 1.99.0]. `git archive` exports carry the root config, so from M63 on every export built with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` alone — the G-base driver, this plan's export recipes, E6 builds, agent scratch builds under the session rule — writes a `<cargo home>/build/<path-hash>` that deleting the scratch tree never removes, inside the owner's `~/.cargo`. From M63 on each such build sets `CARGO_BUILD_BUILD_DIR` under the same scratch root beside `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, or strips `build-dir` from the export's `.cargo/config.toml`; for the shell the two are set together too (tauri-build's sidecar copy walks `OUT_DIR` up to `build`, so with the two split it lands in the build-dir tree). M63's gate checks that such an export adds nothing under `$CARGO_HOME/build`, plus a G-base `--self-test` run; CLAUDE.md's *Build hygiene* lines and the agent session rules carry the same pairing (`modernization-plan.md` M63).
- **Gate texts name the pinned toolchain** (rev 3.14; the ZCode audit's A-14, which is the review's SF-8 amendment; Z7-07, Z7-13). A gate written from rev 3.14 on, §5.4's among them, names "the pinned toolchain (`rust-toolchain.toml`)", and every gate **run** invokes a plain `cargo`, which the toolchain file resolves, never a `cargo +1.9x.y` literal; a script that runs a gate does the same, or reads the channel from the tree's own `rust-toolchain.toml`, as §5.1 item 3 requires of E6's loop (A2-16) (B-23's policy guard, pending, would check scripts for it). **The one exception is the G-base driver,** which runs `cargo "+$TOOLCHAIN"` (`tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/gbase/gbase.sh:256`, `:908`, `:1105`), the toolchain a parameter (`TOOLCHAIN="1.99.0"` at `:26`, `--toolchain` at `:117`): its export starts from the branch point's `rust-toolchain.toml`, and the driver writes the channel into the export's file itself at step 1 (`:676`), so the toolchain it names is the one it pins. The ten `cargo +1.99.0` that rev 3–3.13 wrote are not rewritten one by one, and each is read as the pinned toolchain for its own reason (this file's lines at rev 3.14): P03's gate (`plan-rev3.md:778`) and P04's contents (`:791`) by §5.3's preamble (4), which covers an entry or §6 and names the literal itself (`:747`); G-check's (`:733`) by its own rev 3.13 note (SF-8); §0's (`:87`) and §7.2's (`:1384`) as dated records of a measurement; and the four in §5.1 items 6, 6a and 6b (`:682-684`, item 6 superseded) as the driver's procedure, which the exception above covers. *Rev 3.15 (D3 (a)):* the pinned toolchain is 1.99.0 from P03 and 1.99.1 from P03b, which rewrites none of these literals; the driver's `TOOLCHAIN="1.99.0"` (`gbase.sh:26` at `6d3e47fc`) belongs to its run of record, which precedes P03b, and a later run passes `--toolchain 1.99.1`.

**Named gates** (rev 2.1's five, re-anchored; one new):

| Gate | What it is |
|---|---|
| **G-check** | `cargo +1.99.0 check --locked --workspace --all-targets --exclude openalpaca_gui` (`+1.93.0` before P03; *rev 3.13, SF-8: the pinned toolchain, `rust-toolchain.toml` — 1.99.0 from P03, 1.99.1 from P03b (D3 (a), rev 3.15)*). `--locked` is accepted by 1.99.0 on the unmodified lock [M-today]: keep it for P03's gate, drop it for the steps that regenerate the lock, restore it afterwards. **Backing out, in general** (rev 2.1): revert in reverse order; to revert a middle commit restore its manifest line and `cargo update -p <crate> --precise <old>`; the formats marked **[rollback M]** are safe to roll back (the old build reads what the new one wrote — measured in September on the format, and today the whole suite passes on the bumped crates).

### 5.3 The commits

Status in each heading: **Required** (lands under every DU answer except one that removes it), **Recommended (DU-x)**, **Conditional** (P01b, P39 — expected to apply, §2.11), **Optional (DU-x)**. Each entry is marked **unchanged** (rev 2.1's text stands at `7295f91b`), **changed: …** or **new**. "Recut step N" names the re-cut step that measured it (`SP/logs/recut-0N-*`, `SP/patches/recut-lock-0N-*.lock`).

**Rev 3.13 — what every entry below now means** (the 2026-10-17 review; MF-1, MF-2, MF-3, SF-2, SF-3, SF-8, G-05, G-07). **(1)** A version named in an entry is a dated record of what was measured or eligible when it was written, never a target: each commit that moves a lock takes the newest release eligible at **the cut instant (DU-23)** — a release that turns eligible later is taken by §5.1 item 5's before-the-merge freshness sweep, in its own commit — and no literal is re-anchored before owner decision D1 is answered (*rev 3.15: answered — (8)*). **(2)** Every commit that moves `Cargo.lock` — P31a, P04, P06–P19b, P25–P30, P41 and any later one — runs §5.2's freshness gate against its parent's lock, records the result in its `Verified:` line, and its body lists each new major version of a transitive crate it pulls in and the dependency it comes through (§5.2, G-07); a JS install is bound by `bunfig.toml`'s `minimumReleaseAge` and, since rev 3.15, each JS lock write is followed by §5.2's `bun.lock` freshness check (C5). **(3)** A caret floor written into a manifest is the version the lock takes, never a newer one the prep commits wrote (§5.1 item 5); a crate the lock holds at more than one version is spelled `name@locked` in every `cargo update -p` and `cargo tree -i`. **(4)** A `cargo +1.99.0` in an entry or in §6 means the pinned toolchain, `rust-toolchain.toml` — 1.99.0 from P03 and 1.99.1 from P03b (owner decision D3 (a), rev 3.15); the same reading covers `modernization-plan.md`'s gate literals (Part III), so P03b rewrites none of them. **(5)** A gate that reads CI — "on GitHub", "the PR's ubuntu job", "CI's gui job" — needs §5.1 item 9's draft PR, and its run URL goes into Chunk 25; cargo-audit, cargo-deny, zizmor and shellcheck are §5.1 item 13's pinned versions in a scratch root. **(6)** Where an entry has a pre-written prep commit (`refs/prep/deps-wave-2026-10-12`), the commit lands by §5.1 item 10's rewrite, not a rebase, and the entry records where the prep body and the entry disagreed (A1-05); the prep's figures were measured on a newer lock than the cut admits and are expectations to re-measure. **(7)** A commit's G-CI anchor is its parent's count with 0 failed (§5.2, G-CI). **(8) Rev 3.15 (D1 (a)).** The versions rev 3.15 writes into P03, P04, P10, P12, P16, P27a, P30, P31a, P31, P32, P33, P41 and P42 are the newest releases eligible at 2026-10-21T19:53Z as `tasks/deps-upgrade/reports/2026-10-17-cut-compatibility.md` measured them on 2026-10-17 (its §2 and §6) — targets, because no release published after the max date (2026-10-14T19:53:00Z) can become eligible at that instant; a yank before the cut can still change one, which the cut-day yank scan and the freshness gate catch, and the step-backs a commit takes are those the gate prints on the day. P03b's 1.99.1 is not one of them: published after the max date, it is eligible from 2026-10-23T04:47:44Z by the toolchain's clock (the later of its channel manifest and its GitHub release, DU-2) and is the toolchain's case of §5.1 item 5's before-the-merge rule, its own commit then. Every other literal stays (1)'s dated record. The report's figures were measured on the prep tip with the prep's code commits, in two trees that never carried both halves together and, in its rev 2, in one that did, through every gate but the workspace suite, check and clippy, so they are each commit's expectation, not its result (its §1, §7, §8).

Deleting the table leaves cargo's default, `auto`; with both tables gone the file is empty and is deleted (`modernization-plan.md` M63 creates it again for `[build] build-dir`). The body says so; `modernization-plan.md` R-17's record of the file is that plan's to update. **(SF-8, D3)** Rust 1.99.1 exists (§2.2; DU-2's last bullet): under the review's recommendation for D3 (DU-23 governs) this commit takes 1.99.0 and a one-line **P03b** `build: Rust 1.99.1` follows through this entry's gates once 1.99.1 is eligible (2026-10-22T17:39:23Z by the channel manifest, 2026-10-23T04:47:44Z by the GitHub release; which counts is part of D3); under a DU-23 (c) waiver, or a cut instant at or after 2026-10-23T04:48Z, this commit takes 1.99.1 itself and there is no P03b. **P12 — `build(deps): keyring-core 1.0 with the native stores (Secret Service over zbus on Linux)`** · Required, gated by DU-10 · changed: lock details; the shell is unaffected; **rev 3.9: keyring-core in place of keyring 4's `v1` facade**
- **Contents.** `Cargo.toml:155` at `865a2376` (`:148` at `7295f91b`): `keyring` → `keyring-core = "1.0"`; in `crates/openalpaca_llm/Cargo.toml` the target-specific stores keyring 4's `v1` feature selects — `apple-native-keyring-store = { version = "1.0.2", features = ["keychain"] }` (macOS), `windows-native-keyring-store = "1.1"` (Windows), `zbus-secret-service-keyring-store = "1.0.1"` (Linux) [D: `keyring-4.2.0/src/v1.rs`, its `Cargo.toml` `v1 = […]`; all four eligible [M: crates.io, re-plan]] — so item addressing is unchanged; `keyring_core::Entry` and `Error::NoEntry` at `crates/openalpaca_llm/src/keys/secret_store/mod.rs:46`, `:56`, `:64` (`:50`, `:68`), with v1's method names. The default store is set **inside `openalpaca_llm`** — a `LazyLock<Result<…>>` that `KeyringSecretStore` checks before every call (or entries built against an owned `Arc` store) — never by a call at daemon and CLI start: `KeyringSecretStore` is constructed at `apps/openalpacad/src/services/mod.rs:122`, `services/llm.rs:68` and `apps/openalpaca/src/commands/ai_config_helpers.rs:38` plus tests, and a missed call answers `NoDefaultStore` [C]. Why (rev 3.9): keyring 4's `v1` is its maintained default, but by its own README "exactly the same API as the original v1" — the older form on the new major [D: `keyring-4.2.0/README.md:8`]. Lock: `keyring` leaves; `keyring-core 1.0.0`, the three store crates, `secret-service 5.2.0`, the `zbus 5.19.0` stack (Linux-only compile) as measured; `dbus-secret-service`, `security-framework 2.11.1`, `core-foundation 0.9.4` leave [M-today]. The wave's one clippy change (−1 `clone_on_copy`) is the AES hunk's, measured at this step.
- **Rev 3.13 — the prep delta and where it disagrees with this entry** (A1-05; prep `44ec0b03`, a pure delta, §5.1 item 10). **(1)** The zbus store carries `crypto-rust` — keyring 4's `v1` sets it, and secret-service 5.2.0 refuses to compile without a crypto feature — so the bare `zbus-secret-service-keyring-store = "1.0.1"` above would fail the first Linux compile; the prep puts each store's version, with `features = ["crypto-rust"]` on this one, in `[workspace.dependencies]` and `openalpaca_llm` takes each under its own target table. **(2)** Its cfg is v1's — every unix but macOS, iOS and Android — not Linux alone. **(3)** `KeyringSecretStore` is constructed at `apps/openalpacad/src/services/llm.rs:25` and `:68` and `apps/openalpaca/src/commands/ai_config_helpers.rs:38`, not at `services/mod.rs:122` [C at `3598a6fb`]. **(4)** The store is a `static NATIVE_STORE: LazyLock<keyring_core::Result<Arc<CredentialStore>>>` inside `keys/secret_store/mod.rs`, made on the process's first get/set/delete and reached through `KeyringSecretStore::native()`; keyring-core's process-wide default store is never set or read, so no lookup can answer `NoDefaultStore`, and a store that could not be made (headless Linux, no Secret Service) fails each call with its own reason while `CachingSecretStore::prefetch` still answers false and the daemon falls back to the in-memory store. Its four tests point a `KeyringSecretStore` at keyring-core's in-memory mock store, so none reads the developer's login keychain. **(5)** The caret `keyring-core = "1.0"` resolves to 1.0.1 (2026-10-13T00:02Z, eligible 2026-10-20T00:02Z), so the floor is the version the lock takes (MF-2). *Rev 3.15 (C3):* at D1 (a)'s instant `keyring-core = "1.0.1"` (1.0.1 changes tests and CI only), where the prep writes `"1.0"` (`refs/prep/deps-wave-2026-10-12:Cargo.toml:170`) and is re-pinned when it lands; the three store crates stay at 1.0.2, 1.1.0 and 1.0.1, each the newest. This is also the commit that first brings zbus into the lock (the Linux store, through secret-service; HEAD's lock holds none), and the lock resolves every target, so on macOS too its freshness gate, not P04's, refuses zbus_names 4.3.5 (2026-10-16T14:49:32Z), zvariant 5.15.1 (14:49:21Z) and zvariant_derive 5.15.1 (14:49:09Z) and steps them back, dependents first, to 4.3.4, 5.15.0 and 5.15.0 (the report's §3.2 order); a release published before the cut is stepped back the same way.
- **Gate.** G-check (macOS); the PR's ubuntu job (the first Linux compile of zbus); DU-10 (count 0, or a disposable-keychain round trip) — unchanged in substance, the store type being v1's; a unit test that the first lookup in a fresh process never answers `NoDefaultStore`; `cargo tree --locked -i keyring` empty. **No G-GUI**: keyring never reaches the shell [M-today]. *Rev 3.13:* **(SF-10)** `cargo tree -i keyring` exits 101 ("did not match any packages") once the package is gone, so the absence check is an inventory that succeeds — `cargo tree --locked --workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui --target all -e normal,build --prefix none | grep -c '^keyring v'` prints 0, and `grep -c '^name = "keyring"$' Cargo.lock` prints 0; **(SF-2)** the ubuntu job runs on §5.1 item 9's draft PR, read a second time after P12, P13 and P08 and before P15; **(SF-14)** a count of 0 waives the round trip only when it is 0 on both the `llm.toml` count and a count over `plugins/.config/*.toml` and `plugins/.permissions.toml` — the plugin manager writes keychain items named `openalpaca-plugin-<name>-<key>` and records each as an inline `key = { secret_ref = … }` (§5.1 item 3).
- **Back-out.** Revert (keyring 3 reads v4-created items [D]). *Rev 3.13 (F-15):* restated for keyring-core — revert; keyring-core writes through the same three stores keyring 4's `v1` selects, with the same item addressing (service `OpenAlpaca`, account the ref, no modifiers; the prep's tests pin it), so keyring 3 reading those items follows from the same source reading as for v4 and is not measured [NE]; DU-10's round trip, when it runs, measures it.

A child then leaves the daemon's process group (a terminal Ctrl+C no longer reaches it directly; the daemon's shutdown kills it by group), and the watchdog's `process::exit(1)` (`apps/openalpacad/src/main.rs:812`) leaves group leaders alive — the same window as R45's, recorded.
  - **Rev 3.13 (A1-04) — the wrapper `ProcessGroup::leader()` alone fails this entry's own pass condition** (the descendant check below), as the prep found (`88394660`): rmcp closes a stdio connection by closing stdin, waiting 3 s, and killing by `killpg` only a server still running [D: `rmcp-3.5.1/src/transport/child_process.rs:81-107`, read by the prep], so a server that exits on the EOF is reaped and nothing is signalled, and what it left running survives, reparented to init — measured with the forking fixture through the daemon's disable (both `sleep 3600` descendants alive with PPID 1). So **`group::EndGroupWithLeader` is stacked over the leader wrapper**: once the leader has been waited for (rmcp's graceful close, its kill after 3 s, its drop guard) it sends SIGKILL to the group once through the `ProcessGroupChild`'s own `start_kill`; EPERM is a warn naming the server, ESRCH is silent; a job object on Windows, compiled and tested by nothing here (DU-33). **Residue, recorded:** the signal follows the reap, so in the window between them only a full wrap of the pid space could hit another group; the zero-window form (signal before the reap) needs `waitid(WNOWAIT)`, which nix does not offer on macOS. Side effect: the MCP hang tests leave no `sleep 3600` behind. The prep also folds `serve_with_conn` into `do_handshake`, keeps a credential rejection `Unauthorized` in either phase (`handshake_error` reads the fallback's error inside `LegacyFallbackFailed`), and adds the dev-dependency `axum` (tokio `net` for its listener) for the HTTP stubs.

- **Gate.**
  - `cargo check --all-targets` on `-p openalpaca_mcp --features test-utils`, `-p openalpaca_core`, `-p openalpacad` (0 errors [M-today]); `cargo test -p openalpaca_mcp --features test-utils`; `cargo test -p openalpacad -- managers::mcp routes::tools` (*rev 3.13, SF-10: rev 3.9 wrote it without the `--`, which cargo refuses as "unexpected argument"*); `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib` (permission-tier, sandbox, annotation tests; `annotations_for_builtin`'s rows unchanged); G-CI with `gen_api_docs.py --check` clean after the regeneration; G-lint (the hunk count back at its parent's; `result_large_err` at `openalpaca_mcp/src/client.rs:663` remains).
  - **A manual real-server round trip**, under §5.2: `cargo test -p openalpaca_mcp -- --ignored` (the npx `@modelcontextprotocol/server-filesystem` e2e; network; *rev 3.13, F-14: the test runs `npx -y @modelcontextprotocol/server-filesystem` unpinned (`crates/openalpaca_mcp/tests/filesystem_e2e.rs:17-27`), outside the 7-day rule, so this commit pins it to a version at least 7 days old at the cut instant and the run sets a scratch `HOME` and `npm_config_cache`*); one MCP server the owner uses, over stdio and over HTTP if any, through the daemon: tools list, one call, disable, enable — **reading the lifecycle on the wire** (`server/discover`, or `initialize` with `2025-11-25`), the server's answer and the connect time. **Stub servers** in `cargo test -p openalpaca_mcp` (rev 3.9): a modern one (expects `server/discover`); a legacy one rejecting it with a JSON-RPC error (falls back to `initialize` at `2025-11-25`); a legacy streamable-HTTP one answering a plain-text 4xx (falls back — rmcp's client turns a pre-session 4xx other than 401/403 into a correlated JSON-RPC error [D: `rmcp-3.5.1/src/transport/streamable_http_client.rs:375-406`]); a silent one (the 10 s recorded).
  - **Disabled means the child is gone, descendants included** (ADR-030) — **a pass condition since rev 3.9**: the existing direct-child test (`managers/mcp/tests.rs:407`) plus, by hand, a forking stdio server's tree (`npx` → `node`) recorded with `ps -o pid,ppid,pgid,command` before the disable, none of those pids after; repeat after re-enable and a second disable; **plus a deterministic forking fixture through the daemon's disable/re-enable path** on macOS (Linux in CI's ubuntu job; Windows' job-object leg deferred, DU-33, the gap labelled in the test's `#[cfg]` and the body). A08's probe measured `descendant_alive=true` without the wrapper [A08]; with it the fixture shows no descendant, or P19 does not land.
  - *Rev 3.14 (A-04; Z5-03, Z5-14).* **(1)** The HTTP round trip — a streamable-HTTP stub in `cargo test -p openalpaca_mcp`, and the owner's server if it speaks HTTP — includes an SSE answer that streams for 45 s and completes, which needs N-MCP-2 (§5.4) landed first; under D5, P19b's listen stream outliving 30 s is P19b's check. **(2)** The deterministic forking fixture gains a grandchild that calls `setsid`. The fixture writes each pid it starts to a file, and the test holds a `KillOnFail` on the grandchild's (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/test_util.rs:314`, or a copy in the daemon's test module if the fixture lives there), so a grandchild that escapes is killed by the test and the gate leaves no orphan (CLAUDE.md *Gotchas*). **(3)** The fixture's teardown check warns, naming the pids, if any pid of the recorded tree survives; every pid that did not leave the group must be gone (the pass condition above, whose "no descendant" does not count the `setsid` grandchild unless (4) is answered with a walk). **(4)** **The parent-pid walk is pending owner decision *P19 descendant walk*** (§5.1 item 1; before P19). The audit adds the walk "only if the fixture shows an escape" (Z5-14), and the `setsid` grandchild leaves the group by its own act, so a group kill cannot reach it and the condition is met on every run: the walk is a question, not a conditional step. A walk bounded to pids the daemon holds unreaped reaches nothing the group kill does not, because the daemon holds only its direct child, and the grandchild is alive under that child or reparented once it dies; reaching the grandchild means signalling a pid the daemon does not hold. R45's settled rule for the shell excludes that: the group id `run_bounded`'s guard signals stays the child's own, alive or an unreaped zombie, until the run ends, so it can never be one the system reused (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/tools/platform.rs:70-77`). P19's prep wrapper comes close without matching it: its group `SIGKILL` follows the leader's reap, and its comment bounds the reuse window to a full wrap of the pid space between the reap and the signal, the new process also a group leader (prep `88394660`, `crates/openalpaca_mcp/src/transport/stdio.rs:150-158`). A walk signals single pids the daemon never held, across the walk's own interval, and any process given a listed pid is hit. B-07's pending recommendation, no descendant signals (the audit's Z1-07: they would break R45's pid-reuse guarantee, `tasks/research/zcode-audit-2026-10-17.md:211`), agrees with the rule without deciding it. ZCode's evidence: it lists the child's descendants from `ps` by parent pid, signals the group and each listed pid with SIGINT, then SIGTERM, then SIGKILL, a grace between each, and lists them again before the SIGKILL (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/adapters/src/mcp/process-tree.ts:128-140, :196-216), so a pid that exits during a grace can be reused before its next signal. Options: **(i)** no walk — the escapee is named by (3)'s warning and is one of R45's recorded edges (storage design §18), for MCP children as for the shell; **(ii)** ZCode's walk, accepting that window; **(iii)** one listing just before the group kill, while the direct child lives, followed at once by SIGKILL to each listed pid, which narrows the window and does not close it. **Recommendation: (i)**, the no-change answer this entry is written to; the run's escaped pid goes into P19's body as the owner's evidence. The spawn-time group and job object stay: they are stronger than ZCode's attach-after-spawn (audit Z5-14).
- **Back-out.** Revert (one commit; the 3.x code does not compile against 0.16).

**P19b — `feat(mcp): a 2026-07-28 session subscribes to its server's list changes`** · **Pending owner decision D5** (rev 3.13, E-19 — rev 3.12 marked it Required as the lead's ruling under the no-compromise rule; a new outbound protocol message is a decision to present, DU-25's last paragraph; recommended: take it) · new: pre-written in scratch beside P19 (`e87c038d` over P19's `88394660`, both on `refs/prep/deps-wave-2026-10-12` over the prep base `6992a402`; rev 3.12 named `73df38f5` and `88d9de68`, which no ref contains — SF-3) and reviewed with it
- **Why.** A server that speaks `2026-07-28` sends `notifications/tools/list_changed` (and the prompt and resource siblings) only on a `subscriptions/listen` stream [D: `rmcp-3.5.1/src/service/server.rs:268-311`; the peer-wide `notify_tool_list_changed`, `:973`, is the legacy path], and rmcp's client opens none by itself. With P19's `Auto` alone a modern server is reached and never heard to add or drop a tool — the silent hole the extension design's §3.7 closed (X-35), reopened by this wave for exactly the servers `Auto` exists to reach. Leaving it out would land a regression with the migration; the rule of 2026-10-12 (`CLAUDE.md:52`) takes the new protocol's way of hearing a change with the new protocol. The review recommended taking it.
- **Contents.** `crates/openalpaca_mcp/src/client.rs`: `serve` opens the stream when the negotiated version has no `initialize` — a `ChangeListener` asks for the list changes the server declares (`listChanged: true` on tools, prompts, resources), and a spawned task forwards each onto the client's change channel as `ServerChange`, where the supervisor's §3.7 refresh already reads them (rmcp invalidates the touched caches first [D: `rmcp-3.5.1/src/service.rs:1626`]). The connect does not wait on the acknowledgment. A refusal, a partial acknowledgment, a failed stream or one the server ends while the connection lives is a `warn` naming the server; the stream ending with its connection is a `debug`. The task holds a weak sender, so the supervisor's receiver still ends at `disconnect` (§3.7, §3.3 E5). A legacy session is unchanged. `docs/Daemon_Manual.md`'s MCP paragraph and CLAUDE.md's MCP bullet say so.
- **Gate.** P19's checks and test filters on the commit; an in-process `2026-07-28` stub: the change reaches the channel and the channel ends with the session; the filter asks for exactly what is declared; the mutation without the `listen` call fails; the legacy stubs see no stray `listen`. Measured in prep (`scratchpad/prep/logs/p19/c2-*`): mcp 39 + 6 passed; daemon `managers::mcp routes::tools routes::extensions` 105 passed; core `--lib` 2,539 passed, 2 failed (P14's pins, as at the parent); clippy and fmt at the parent's counts; `gen_api_docs --check` clean. At the cut: re-gated on the real branch after P19, and **a real `2026-07-28` server** in P19's manual round trip, if one exists by then (none was at hand) — a list change made on the server and seen in `GET /v1/tools` without a reload. *Rev 3.13 (SF-12): the in-process `2026-07-28` stub is this commit's **gate of record**; the real-server check is an end-to-end step (§6, E5 additions), run if such a server exists by then and recorded as not run otherwise.*
- **Residue, recorded.** A change the server announces between `serve` returning and the `listen` acknowledgment is not heard (the connect does not wait); the next list change or a reload brings the refresh. Appendix B #42's per-server discover cost is P19's, unchanged. *Rev 3.13 (A1-12): that window reopens, for one interval, the X-35 hole this commit exists to close. The form the review recommends with D5 closes it: on the acknowledgment the task sends one `ServerChange` — one `tools/list` refresh — through the existing channel, and a stub test announces a change before acknowledging and sees it arrive. It is part of D5's answer, not adopted here; under D5 taken as recommended it lands in this commit and this residue goes.*
- **Rev 3.14 — conditions under D5 taken** (the ZCode audit's A-05; Z5-03, and Z5's and Z8's D5 answers). **(1)** N-MCP-2 (§5.4) has landed: over HTTP a listen stream is otherwise cut at 30 s by reqwest's total deadline and resumed only where the server supports Last-Event-ID (rmcp-3.5.1 `src/transport/streamable_http_client.rs:787-806`), so the stub gate adds a listen stream that outlives 30 s. **(2)** The listener reopens the subscription **once** after an unexpected end while the connection lives, where the prep logs a warning and stops (`e87c038d:crates/openalpaca_mcp/src/client.rs:800-822`); a second end in the same connection keeps the warning; a stub test ends the stream once and then sees a later change arrive. **(3)** A test pins that a list change arriving mid-turn does not alter the running turn's tool surface — the change reaches the next turn — the invariant ZCode keeps by marking the server stale and fetching the list again at the next turn boundary (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/adapters/src/mcp/index.ts:503-531, :733-741); if it does not hold, this commit makes it hold. ZCode's own code opens no `subscriptions/listen` (its SDK is not vendored, audit §7), so its evidence on D5 is that shape, not the stream. Whether P19b lands at all stays D5; the audit's answer is to take it with these three conditions.
- **Back-out.** Revert (one commit); P19 stands without it, and a modern server's list changes go unheard again — record that residue in design §18 if it is ever reverted.

**P23 — `chore(deps): take rmcp without default features`** · Optional (DU-5) · unchanged, plus: Cargo 1.99 lets an edition-2024 member override an inherited dependency's `default-features` [Docs], an option if the trim is ever taken.

*Rev 3.13 (SF-6) — the MCP redirect fix, outside this numbering and pending owner decision D6.* Our streamable-HTTP transport builds its own reqwest client with the default redirect policy (`crates/openalpaca_mcp/src/transport/http.rs:96-98`) and hands it to rmcp (`with_client`, `:112`); the `HttpAuth::ApiKey` header, under any name, and every `extra_headers` entry ride as default headers (`:57-89`), and reqwest strips only Authorization, Cookie and Proxy-Authorization on a cross-origin hop, so a redirect to another origin receives them. rmcp 3.5's fix (GHSA-9g45-5xwm-f3wc) covers its own default client only, so P19 does not fix ours (§3.3). The fix moves no version and can land before the cut, as its own `fix(mcp):` commit; it adds an enforcement point and stops a server whose configured URL relies on a redirect (http→https included) from connecting, so whether it lands is **D6**, pending. Under the review's recommendation: cross-origin redirects refused, same-origin ones still followed (`Policy::none()`, as rmcp's own client does, is the stricter form D6 can choose instead); a loopback test redirecting to a second port asserts that the header does not arrive; the change named in the commit body and in `docs/Daemon_Manual.md`'s MCP paragraph; GHSA-9g45-5xwm-f3wc cited, GHSA-c9xm-49cp-xcr9 not (unverified). It moves no lock, so the freshness gate does not apply; G-CI does. *Rev 3.14 (the ZCode audit, A-13; Z5-03, Z5-08):* the fix's commit is now **N-MCP-2** (§5.4). Whatever D6's answer, it carries the stream fix — the MCP client bounded by a connect timeout and an idle read timeout, never a total deadline — and a refused redirect as a typed, non-retriable failure naming the target origin (`ConfigInvalid` at bring-up, never the reconnect ladder). D6's policy rides in it if D6 is answered first, or follows as its own commit. D6 gains a third form the audit presents: refuse **every** redirect when `HttpAuth` or `extra_headers` are configured, and follow same-origin redirects only for an unauthenticated server — ZCode's credentialed fetch sets `redirect: "manual"` and treats any 3xx as a typed failure that is never retried (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/adapters/src/mcp/official-auth.ts:84-93, :222-228, :325-331). The audit's answer to D6: take it, with N-MCP-2's typed error, and present the credentialed form. Still pending.

**P41 — `fix(connectors): one rustls crypto provider — aws-lc-rs installed at daemon start, ring off our manifest`** · Required · **new (rev 3.9)** · after P28
- **Contents.** *Superseded (rev 3.13, SF-11): rev 3.9's contents — connectors' optional `rustls` given `["aws_lc_rs"]` in place of `["ring"]` (`crates/openalpaca_connectors/Cargo.toml:67`), one install in `apps/openalpacad/src/main.rs`, the two ring installs at `src/discord/connector.rs:285`, `:882` removed, and "ring … in use for Telegram's own connections" after P28 — describe a commit the prep did not build, and the Telegram sentence is wrong: reqwest 0.12 takes the process-wide default provider first [D: `reqwest-0.12.28/src/async_impl/client.rs:761-771`, read by the review], so after P28 ring is compiled on teloxide's path while Telegram's connections run on the aws-lc-rs provider this commit installs.* **The contents, as prep `96a1dc30` built them** (a pure delta, §5.1 item 10): `[workspace.dependencies]` gains `rustls = { version = "0.23", default-features = false, features = ["aws_lc_rs"] }`, and no manifest of ours names `ring`; openalpacad gains `rustls.workspace = true` and a new `apps/openalpacad/src/tls.rs` whose `install_crypto_provider()` installs `rustls::crypto::aws_lc_rs::default_provider()` as the process default — `main` calls it right after the logger, after the command line (W1) and before the store, the runtime and anything that builds a TLS client; an `Err` from `install_default()` means a provider is already installed, which rustls keeps, so the daemon logs a warning naming it (read from `CryptoProvider::get_default()`) and carries on. The two ring installs in `crates/openalpaca_connectors/src/discord/connector.rs` (`:285-286`, `:882-883` at `3598a6fb`) go — `run_loop`'s with a doc line saying the host installs the provider, the test's replaced by an aws-lc-rs install because a test binary has no `main` — and the connectors' `rustls` moves from an optional dependency under the `discord` feature (`Cargo.toml:28`, `:67`) to a dev-dependency on the workspace spec. **What it fixes besides the second provider:** `connector_bridge::discord_http()` builds a twilight client for a stored `discord.token` whether or not the connector runs (`discord.enabled` defaults to false), and `sendable_channels()` is asked on the social fast path and by `send`, so on a daemon whose Discord connector had not started that call panicked with rustls's "Could not automatically determine the process-level CryptoProvider"; and once the connector had started, every reqwest client built afterwards ran on ring. Tests: `tls::tests::the_process_default_is_aws_lc_rs` and `connector_bridge::tests::a_stored_discord_token_offers_discord_before_the_connector_runs`; `main`'s ordering is shown only by a daemon boot or the live Discord gate. The body names the blocker that keeps `ring` in the lock after P28 — teloxide 0.17's rustls path through reqwest 0.12's `__rustls-ring` — and its way out: teloxide 0.18 (its master on reqwest 0.13, §3.1) or an upstream teloxide feature on reqwest's `*-no-provider` (the owner's to post).
- **Rev 3.15 (C3; D2 (a)).** At D1 (a)'s instant the requirement is `rustls = { version = "0.23.46", default-features = false, features = ["aws_lc_rs"] }`, the floor the version P04's refresh takes — the prep's `"0.23"` (`refs/prep/deps-wave-2026-10-12:Cargo.toml:148`) admits every rustls release RUSTSEC-2026-0349 affects, which has no unaffected range below 0.23.46 — re-pinned when it lands. The provider it installs is aws-lc-rs 1.18.2 over aws-lc-sys 0.46.0 (P04, C9), which compiled on macOS in the report's build; no test aimed at TLS or key handling was named as covering it (the report's §7), so P28's Telegram round trip and the live Discord connect (D10, pending) are its check, and whether ring is built on macOS stays `cargo tree --locked -e features -i ring`'s answer after P28 (the gate below).
- **Gate.** G-check; `cargo test -p openalpaca_connectors --features telegram,discord,imessage`; `cargo tree --locked -e features -i ring` shows only the teloxide-core → reqwest 0.12 path; a live Discord connect and one message under §5.2; P28's Telegram round trip re-run. *Rev 3.13 (SF-11, SF-12):* the prep's graph result — `cargo tree --locked -e features -i ring` printing nothing, ring built by nothing — was measured **before P28**, on a teloxide still on native-tls; after P28 reqwest 0.12's `__rustls-ring` enables `rustls/ring` again, the ambiguous-provider case this commit exists for, which the prep never exercised. So on the real branch, after P28: `cargo tree --locked -e features -i ring` (expected: only the teloxide-core → reqwest 0.12 path), the connectors suite above, and the daemon tests that build a twilight client, `cargo test -p openalpacad --bin openalpacad -- tls connector_bridge graceful_shutdown`. The live Discord connect needs the Discord test token of §5.1 item 12 — wait for it or record a waiver, owner decision **D10**, pending; the prep ran no live gate (no token). *Rev 3.14 (A-09, with D10 — pending):* the same supplement — twilight against a local stub of Discord's HTTP and gateway endpoints — beside the live connect, which stays the gate (P28).
- **Back-out.** Revert.

**P30 — `chore(deps): replace the archived serde_yaml with serde-saphyr (serde_yaml-compatible options)`** · Recommended (DU-4 C) · changed: measured today — the archived hunks apply unchanged, the pin test `yaml_1_1_spellings_read_the_way_serde_yaml_0_9_read_them` passes (the suite's +1), serde-saphyr 1.3.0 is still the newest 1.x [M-today]; rustfmt the one line the pin test adds (`middleware/skill/tests.rs`). Contents, gate (incl. the E6 skill-directory check before it) and back-out as `plan.md:1274-1287` — its gate counts (20 `middleware::skill` tests, 2024 in the core suite) are September's at `5e5ac317`, references to re-count; today's core count is in `c03-test-recut.log`.
- **Rev 3.13 (SF-9) — which serde-saphyr is owner decision D4, pending.** "1.3.0 is still the newest" no longer holds: serde-saphyr **2.0.0** (2026-10-13T15:11:53Z, MSRV 1.89, eligible 2026-10-20T15:11:53Z) is a new major whose notes list formally breaking changes — a default-on comments feature, a deprecated `AliasError` text field, new `from_multiple*` signatures, granit_parser 2.0.0 — and §5.1 item 5's rule puts a new major outside this plan's evidence until it is researched. DU-4 is re-answered before this commit (D4); the review recommends researching 2.0 first — the option names and `from_str_with_options` the archived hunks use, comments on or off, the pin test `yaml_1_1_spellings_read_the_way_serde_yaml_0_9_read_them`, E6's yamlcmp (which pins `=1.3.0`, `tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/e6/yamlcmp/Cargo.toml:17`) rebuilt against 2.0 — and then taking the newest eligible 2.x, or naming the blocker with its way out, never pinning 1.3 silently. This entry's version is re-anchored once D4 is answered; until then its measured contents are 1.3.0's. Under either answer the body names RUSTSEC-2024-0449 (unsafe-libyaml unmaintained, imported 2026-10-12), which this commit clears with serde_yaml, and the advisory lines are re-read from the cut-day run (MF-3, §2.11); the E6 skill-directory check runs at §5.1 item 3's consent point against the version chosen; the freshness gate on the lock (§5.2).
- **Rev 3.14 (the ZCode audit, A-07; Z3-04, Z3-05, Z3's D4 answer).** **(1)** The warnings: `SkillFrontmatter` is `#[serde(default)]` and captures no unknown key (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/middleware/skill/types.rs:303-334`), so a one-character typo such as `require_capabilities` leaves a skill with no tools (`orchestrator/skill/invocation.rs:253-255`, `:438-441`) whose body still tells the model to use them. N-SKILL-2 (§5.4), which lands first, owns the `unknown_key` diagnostic and its surfaces; this commit keeps the unknown-key capture working across the parser switch and adds a warning when a skill declares no tools at all. No `deny_unknown_fields`: the parse stays lenient (`middleware/skill/mod.rs:8-9`). **(2)** Fixtures written fresh for a BOM, CRLF, `>` / `>-` / `|` / `|+`, a quoted value containing `: `, a nested map, an unknown key and an empty body, each asserted equal under serde_yaml 0.9 and the serde-saphyr D4 chooses, in the pin test and in E6's corpus. **(3)** One leading BOM is stripped — `extract_frontmatter_str` uses `trim_start()` (`middleware/skill/parser.rs:6`), and U+FEFF is not White_Space — and the closing `---` is matched as a whole line (`:17` closes at the first newline followed by `---`, so `----` or `---x` closes it too). **(4)** One serde parser: `config/skills/create-skill/scripts/list_skills.sh` reads frontmatter with `grep -m1 'slash:'`, which matches anywhere in the file (`:39-42`), and is fixed or retired here; ZCode's own bug when two readers of one format disagreed is the argument (ZCode:docs/external-agent-skills-import.md:63). **(5)** The gate adds "no new skill diagnostic over the E6 corpus" once N-SKILL-2 has landed, as a complement to `yamlcmp`. **(6)** D4's research adds `flatten` under serde-saphyr (the unknown-key capture), its default-on comments feature, and the fixtures of (2).
- **Rev 3.15 — the compatibility report's C1 and C10, under either answer of D4; D4's new evidence** (`tasks/deps-upgrade/reports/2026-10-17-cut-compatibility.md` §4, §5). **(1) C1 — `emit_comments: false`.** The prep tip's `yaml_options()` (`refs/prep/deps-wave-2026-10-12:crates/openalpaca_core/src/middleware/skill/mod.rs:54-63`) sets no `emit_comments`, and under serde-saphyr's default comment handling — 1.3 always, 2.0 with its default-on `comments` feature — a frontmatter with **33 or more consecutive comment lines between a mapping key and its block value** (typically a commented-out block under `routing:`) is refused, "too many consecutive comments before resolving collection entry"; 32 pass, and serde_yaml 0.9 reads all of them. This commit sets `emit_comments: false,` in `yaml_options()`, with a doc-comment line saying that a run of comments is read the way serde_yaml read it, and adds a pin test (the report's scratch `comment_runs_and_non_finite_floats_saphyr_leg_pin`, rustfmt-formatted — as drafted its `.map(...).collect()` chain adds one hunk) that fails without the option (measured: exit 101): `middleware::skill` 20 → 21 tests, the core lib 2,545 → 2,546, the workspace +1 (expected, not measured). With it the four refused probes read equal to serde_yaml on either version and nothing else changes. The option, not `default-features = false` (equal on all 42 probes but 2.0-only), because it is explicit in code, immune to Cargo feature unification and valid on 1.3 too. It restores this entry's own aim — as close to serde_yaml's reading as the options go — so it is a fix inside P30's accepted design, adding no enforcement point and reversing no row, which the report recommends under either D4 answer and this entry writes in; E6's `yamlcmp` takes the same option (its typed parse errors on the probes go 4 → 0), and P38's *Gotchas* line on the saphyr options names it. None of the four repository skills hits it; whether a real skill does is E6's answer at §5.1 item 3's consent point. **(2) C10 — the doc comment on non-finite floats.** The prep's doc comment (`mod.rs:52` there) says a `.inf` value "stays the text it was written as"; in `serde_json::Value` positions (`tools.defaults`, `scripts[].parameters`, the buffered `context.sources` enum) 1.3, and 2.0 with `AsString`, give the canonical spelling instead — `.NaN` → `".nan"`, `+.Inf` → `".inf"`, `1e999` → `".inf"`, where serde_yaml 0.9 gave null (and `"1e999"`) — while string-typed fields keep the text as written. One sentence of the doc comment says so. 2.0's `NonFiniteFloatPolicy::PassThrough` would restore serde_yaml's null for `.inf` and `.nan` (not for `1e999`): a design choice for this commit's author, presented, not adopted. **(3) D4 — new evidence, still pending.** The report's §5 is the research D4's recommendation asked for: serde-saphyr 2.0.0 (2026-10-13T15:11:53Z, rust-version 1.89, with granit-parser 2.0.0) is eligible at the cut and moves those two lock entries only; as a drop-in it compiles and passes with one rustc `deprecated` warning — `Options::reject_non_finite_typeless_float`, deprecated for `non_finite_float_policy` — which G-lint's 0-`deprecated` rule refuses and `non_finite_float_policy: serde_saphyr::NonFiniteFloatPolicy::AsString` replaces exactly (`#[allow(deprecated)]` is not an option under the no-compromise rule); with it the core suite keeps 1.3's counts and the workspace 5,126 / 0 / 17; on the four repository skills every facet is identical, and on 42 synthetic frontmatters 1.3 and 2.0 accept and reject the same inputs with equal values, only error texts differing (2.0's cleaner). This is evidence, not the decision: D4 stays the owner's. Under D4 (a) this commit writes `serde-saphyr = "2.0"` with `AsString` in place of the deprecated field, and DU-4, §1.2's and §1.6's rows and E6's `yamlcmp` (`=2.0.0`: its `Cargo.toml:17`, `main.rs:2-3` and `:46`, its `Cargo.lock`, the E6 README's row) move to 2.0.0; under D4 (b) it keeps `"1.3"`, and the hold is named in §3.1 with 2.0.0 as its way out. (1) and (2) apply under either.

- **Contents.** *Superseded scope (rev 3.13, A2-10): the "+23 sites" list that follows was rev 3.9's, withdrawn by the 100 → 0 gate below. The contents are `modernization-plan.md`'s **M04 + M07** as prep `ac6db26c` built them: every default-warn clippy finding on the tree under 1.99.0 — the 100 unique triples of the 2026-10-11 inventory less `clone_on_copy`, which P11's AES hunk removed — each fixed by hand: the 65 mechanical items by clippy's own suggestion (the prep body's count; the plans said 66, E-18), the let chains and the judgement items, and M04's `fetch_update` → `Atomic::update` as written below.* The **+23** sites of §2.2 (`useless_borrows_in_formatting` ×6, `result_large_err` ×5, `drain_collect` ×2, `single_element_loop` ×2, `unnecessary_sort_by` ×2, `for_kv_map`, `question_mark`, `collapsible_match`, `unneeded_wildcard_pattern`, `useless_format`) and the `fetch_update` deprecation at `session_log/mod.rs:519` — replaced by the **infallible** `Atomic::update` (1.95): `let prev = self.next_spill.update(Ordering::Relaxed, Ordering::Relaxed, |current| current.max(floor) + 1); let number = prev.max(floor);` (rev 3.9; `try_update` keeps a fallible shape for an update that cannot fail) — nothing older. Keep `event_bridge.rs`'s explicit `created_by: _` (R79), under `#[expect(clippy::unneeded_wildcard_pattern, reason = "R79")]` once `modernization-plan.md` M39 lands; `result_large_err` at `openalpaca_mcp/src/client.rs:663` against rmcp 3.5.0's `ClientInitializeError` fixed or allowed. **Never a blanket `clippy --fix`** (it broke the build in September [M-sep]).

### 5.4 Commits from the ZCode audit (no version moves)

*New in rev 3.14.* The source is `tasks/research/zcode-audit-2026-10-17.md` (rev 1): ZCode (Apache-2.0, read-only reference) read at `aac47556`, OpenAlpaca at `3598a6fb`, whose code is HEAD `ff0e7740`'s — `git diff --stat 3598a6fb ff0e7740 -- crates apps config scripts` is empty, so the audit's code citations are cited here at HEAD, and the ones below were re-read there. Its §4.4 lists 30 fixes within settled rules (apply) and 27 owner decisions with a commit shape (present, B-01…B-27). This section schedules the 30 and records the 27 as pending, with no Contents adopted. Every entry is a design transfer written fresh in OpenAlpaca's own code: no ZCode code is copied or transliterated, since OpenAlpaca ships no LICENSE and no notices (audit §1, Z7-02, B-22). Where the audit cites ZCode, the location is the audit's, checked by its verifier; this revision re-read the ones for N-MCP-1, N-LEAD-1, B-02, DU-25 (m), P19's forking fixture and D6's credentialed form.

**What every entry means.**
- **No version moves.** No `(name, version)` enters or leaves the lock, so §5.2's freshness gate finds nothing, DU-23 permits the commit at any time, and the cut waits for none of them. A test-only dev-dependency on a crate the workspace already locks, or a feature on one (N-MCP-2's loopback HTTP stub needs `axum` and tokio's `net`, the lines P19's prep `88394660` adds for its own stubs), changes a manifest and the lock's dependency lists but no version; the body names it, and the later commit's rewrite absorbs it.
- **The gate's named test fails first.** It is written before the fix and shown to fail on the entry's parent (or with the fix reverted), and the `Verified:` line says so. Beside it: G-CI at the parent's count with 0 failed; G-lint with no fmt hunk added and no new clippy warning (0 from P36 on); and `python3 -I scripts/gen_api_docs.py --check` clean, regenerated in the same commit when a route, CLI verb or public type changes, with `API_MAP.md` and CLAUDE.md's tables (the rule rev 3.1 set for Q04–Q06). A GUI change runs the four bun gates.
- **The body** names the audit ids it applies, the ZCode location it learned from, and what a person or a model now meets.
- **Tests follow §5.2 and CLAUDE.md's *Gotchas*:** the crate's home-store guard, `GuardedProject` for files and commands, `KillOnFail` for any spawned process, and no test that reads the developer's `~/.openalpaca` or keychain (`DiscoveryHost`).

**Placement.** Each entry's *Place* line applies these rules.
- **(a) Before the commit that rewrites its code.** An entry that touches code a later commit of this plan or of Part III rewrites lands before that commit, which is then written over it (by §5.1 item 10's rewrite where the later commit is pre-written in prep). Most entries touch a file that P36's prep `ac6db26c` also edits: `runner/lead_agent/tools.rs`, `tools/registry/mod.rs`, `orchestrator/skill/catalog/mod.rs`, `providers/anthropic/request.rs`, `session_log/record.rs`, `query_handler/simple_query_handler.rs`, `openalpaca_plugins/src/manager.rs` and `openalpaca_mcp/src/client.rs`. The MCP client set touches what P19 rewrites. For those the place is **before the cut**, between now and P42; an entry not ready by then lands in the wave before the named commit, never as a reason to delay the cut.
- **(b) Not in G-base's way.** G-base's run of record replays the measured wave on the tip (§5.1 item 6b) by applying three patches from `tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/gbase/patches/`:
  - `step-4-secrets.patch`: `hot_reload.rs`, `managers/mcp.rs` near `:2432`, `key_encryption/{mod,tests}.rs`, `content_io.rs`, `update_persona/common.rs` and three test files;
  - `step-6-rmcp.patch`: `openalpaca_mcp/src/{client,error,lib}.rs`, `tools/mcp/bridge.rs`, `tools/builtins/mod.rs` `:660-704`, `tools/config/annotations.rs`, `tools/registry/capabilities.rs`, hunks in the middle of `tools/registry/tests.rs` and `security/sandbox/tests.rs`, `managers/mcp.rs` near `:2312`, `routes/tools/tests.rs`, `tests/mcp_integration.rs` and `filesystem_e2e.rs`;
  - `step-8-config.patch`: `middleware/skill/mod.rs` `:45-80`, `renderer.rs` `:9`, and the last three lines of `middleware/skill/tests.rs`.

  An entry landing before that run must not rewrite a region those patches edit. One that only shifts lines in a patched file — a test appended to `tools/registry/tests.rs` (3,004 lines, its last patched hunk near `:1500`) or to `security/sandbox/tests.rs` (1,870 lines, hunks near `:758` and `:780`) — re-runs the driver's `--dry-run` green on the tree carrying it before the run. No entry appends to `middleware/skill/tests.rs` before the run, since step 8's hunk anchors on that file's last lines. An entry that rewrites a patched region — N-MCP-1, N-MCP-3, N-MCP-4 — lands **after** the run of record and before P19.
- **(c) After what it needs.** An entry that needs an earlier commit follows it: N-MCP-5 and N-PLUG-5 need P19's rmcp 3.5, and N-GUI-1 needs Q07. An entry that touches nothing a later commit rewrites lands after P38, in `modernization-plan.md` §6.3's merged order (the order of record after P38, §7.7).
- **(d) The audit's ordering against Part III** (A-34, A-35, A-38): N-SKILL-1 before M07; N-SKILL-2 before P30 and M36; N-PLUG-1 before M58 (a); N-PLUG-6 before any plugin-surface refactor. N-LEAD-1's `JoinSet` is M40's sibling, and N-LEAD-2's permit wait takes M60's `run_until_cancelled` form, which tokio-util 0.7.19, in the lock, already has (`src/sync/cancellation_token.rs:300`). `modernization-plan.md` §6.3 carries those places.
