#### The MCP client

**N-MCP-2 — `fix(mcp): a streamed MCP body is bounded by idleness, never by a total deadline; a refused redirect is a typed, non-retriable failure`** · Required · new (A-13; Z5-03, Z5-08's apply half)
- **Contents.** `crates/openalpaca_mcp/src/transport/http.rs:96-98` builds the streamable-HTTP client with `.timeout(self.request_timeout)` (30 s, `:30`). That is a total deadline reqwest runs until the body ends (reqwest-0.13.5 `src/async_impl/client.rs:1444-1450`, audit Z5-03). `build_transport` (`client.rs:619-656`) never calls `with_timeout`, so a configured `request_timeout_secs` never reaches it. Every SSE tool answer, the legacy GET stream, P19b's `subscriptions/listen` and M15's progress window are therefore cut at 30 s. The client gets a `connect_timeout` and an idle `read_timeout` only, as the LLM clients from `providers::build_http_client` already do; the per-request deadline stays `McpClient`'s (and M15's).
  - Second, a redirect the client refuses becomes its own non-retriable `McpError` carrying the target origin. Today an rmcp HTTP error maps to the retriable `TransportClosed` unless it is a 401 or 403 (`error.rs:154-174`). Bring-up then reads `ConfigInvalid` ("server redirected to <origin>; set `url` to the canonical endpoint"), and a call never enters the reconnect ladder. Under D6's no-change answer the only refused redirect is a chain past reqwest's default limit of ten hops, which gets the same typed error. If D6 is answered before this lands, D6's policy rides in this commit, since both edit `http.rs` (the paragraph after P23).
  - `docs/Daemon_Manual.md`'s MCP paragraph says a streamed answer is no longer cut at 30 s.
  - The tests' loopback HTTP stub needs `axum` (a workspace dependency, already locked) as a dev-dependency of `openalpaca_mcp` and tokio's `net` feature there — the two lines P19's prep adds for its streamable-HTTP stubs, which P19's rewrite then finds in place. No version moves.
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: an SSE stub that streams one tool answer for 45 s completes; a stub that holds a GET stream open past 30 s keeps it open; a loopback redirect to a second port yields the typed error, the row reads `ConfigInvalid` naming the origin, and the reconnect counter does not move. Commands: `cargo test -p openalpaca_mcp --features test-utils`; `cargo test -p openalpacad -- managers::mcp`. The driver's `--dry-run` on the tree with this commit, before G-base's run of record: the `error.rs` edit sits below `step-6-rmcp.patch`'s two `error.rs` hunks (`@@ -117`, `@@ -132`), and a new variant above them shifts their lines.
- **Back-out.** Revert; the 30 s total deadline returns.
- **Place.** Before the cut. `http.rs` is in no G-base patch and no prep delta. P19's HTTP round trip (A-04) and P19b's listen stream (A-05) need this commit first, and the review placed D6's fix before the cut.

**N-MCP-1 — `fix(mcp): a call that may have reached the server is never sent again`** · Required · new (Z5-02; amends the extension design's §3.6 item 1; the mechanism half of T6(a)'s ZCode row)
- **Contents.** `McpClient::call_tool` (`crates/openalpaca_mcp/src/client.rs:530-584`) loops. On any `is_retriable()` error (TransportClosed, Transport, Timeout, ServerInternal; `error.rs:83-91`) it reconnects and sends again with a fresh `request_timeout`; `reconnect` kills the stdio child (`:483-484`); and a good handshake resets the attempt counter (`:325`, `:510`, `:564`). So a server that handshakes and then fails every call loops until the sandbox cut (60 s for agents, 300 s for the lead), and a non-idempotent tool runs twice after a timeout. That is §3.3's unbounded reconnect ladder. Now:
  - Before sending, if the service is absent or `is_transport_closed()` (rmcp-0.16.0 `src/service.rs:422`; rmcp-3.5.1 `:1053`), reconnect once and send. This keeps the transparent respawn of a dead child in the extension design's §3.6 item 1.
  - Once the request may have left, it is never sent again. Timeout, ServerInternal, Transport and a mid-call TransportClosed go back to the model, and the next call respawns.
  - One deadline spans the pre-send reconnect and the call.
  - Consecutive failed handshakes count toward `ReconnectExhausted`; a handshake alone does not reset the count.
  - `tasks/extension-enable-design.md` §3.6 item 1's text and its revision log are amended in the same commit.
  - Re-sending when `idempotent_hint` is true is left out: it trusts a server's own annotation, and would be presented with T35 if ever wanted.
  - ZCode keeps one deadline per call, reconnects a disconnected record once before the call, and retries only on a definite "not connected" (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/adapters/src/mcp/index.ts:778, :828-830, :916-928).
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent:
  - a stub that counts `tools/call` and lets the first time out sees one call, and the model receives the timeout;
  - a stub that dies after the call is sent sees one send, and the next call respawns it;
  - a server that handshakes but fails every call reaches `ReconnectExhausted` after `max_reconnect_attempts` handshakes, not at the sandbox cut;
  - a dead stdio child is respawned once before the send, and the call succeeds.

  Commands: `cargo test -p openalpaca_mcp --features test-utils`; `cargo test -p openalpacad -- managers::mcp routes::tools`.
- **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Place.** After G-base's run of record, before P19 (at Phase G's head, after P18). `step-6-rmcp.patch` rewrites `client.rs`'s call path (`@@ -534,19`), and the driver applies it to the tip, so landing this before the cut would break the run of record unless step 6 were re-cut and re-checked. P19's rewrite (§5.1 item 10) is then made over the fixed loop, and its real-server round trip and disable tests exercise it. The audit's alternative, folding this into M15 (which rewrites the call path after P38), is the fallback if P19's rewrite over it proves costly; it is never dropped.

**N-MCP-3 — `fix(mcp): a call does not hold the service lock across its RPC`** · Required · new (Z5-04; amends the extension design's §3.2 T4 text)
- **Contents.** `call_tool`, `list_tools`, `list_tools_once` and `ping` hold the `service` mutex across the whole RPC (`client.rs:367`, `:387`, `:503`, `:556`), and the lock is taken inside the timeout wrapper (`:553-560`). A queued call spends its timeout waiting for the lock and fails unsent, and parallel subagents gain nothing. (A queued call's timeout does not close the service under the in-flight call, because `reconnect` takes the same lock, `:483` — the audit's correction.) Now: clone the `Peer` under the guard and drop the guard before awaiting — rmcp 0.16 already has `RunningService::peer` (`src/service.rs:445`) and `Peer::call_tool` / `list_all_tools` (`src/service/client.rs:362`, `:375`). For calls, the lock is taken outside the timeout wrapper or not at all. Before landing: confirm that T3's registry-level drain bounds a call running on a cloned peer, and amend the extension design's §3.2 T4 and disable-latency text (`tasks/extension-enable-design.md:2195-2197`).
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: two concurrent calls to a stub that holds the first for 2 s both finish in about 2 s. The drain test: a disable during an in-flight call on a cloned peer waits for that call within the drain bound. Commands: `cargo test -p openalpaca_mcp --features test-utils`; `cargo test -p openalpacad -- managers::mcp routes::extensions`.
- **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Place.** With or after N-MCP-1, in the same slot and for the same reason.

**N-MCP-4 — `fix(core): remote MCP tool names mapped to a provider-safe alphabet; collisions within one server warned`** · Required · new (Z5-05)
- **Contents.** The remote half of a tool name is used raw, in `format!("{server_name}__{remote_name}")` (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/tools/mcp/bridge.rs:33-34`). The registry refuses only empty names, names over 256 bytes and names containing NUL (`tools/registry/mod.rs:548-566`), and both providers send the name verbatim. Now:
  - First, read each provider's documented tool-name rule (the audit did not, §7) and record the result in the body.
  - Map the remote half to `[A-Za-z0-9_-]` and cap the combined length; keep `remote_name` raw for the call.
  - Detect collisions within one server's mapped list **before** registration: keep the first and warn, naming both originals. The case-13 skip ignores incumbents of the same extension (`apps/openalpacad/src/managers/mcp.rs:1245`), so today the second tool would overwrite the first.
  - A template or skill that names a remapped tool by its raw name stops matching. The body names that, and `docs/Daemon_Manual.md` says the mapped name is the capability.
  - ZCode sanitises names (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/core/src/mcp/name.ts:14-18).
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: `read.file` registers under the mapped name and calls the server with `read.file`; `read.file` beside `read_file` keeps the first and warns naming both. Commands: `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib tools::mcp`; `cargo test -p openalpacad -- managers::mcp`.
- **Back-out.** Revert; a server's tools return to their raw names.
- **Place.** After G-base's run of record, before P19. `bridge.rs` is a `step-6-rmcp.patch` file (hunks `@@ -7`, `@@ -87`, `@@ -106`), and P19's `ContentBlock` hunks rewrite it.

**N-MCP-5 — `feat(mcp): a stdio server's stderr kept as a bounded, redacted tail on its failure`** · Required · new (Z5-09; builds the extension design's rule for an MCP child's stderr, `tasks/extension-enable-design.md:1855-1859`, designed and never built)
- **Contents.** `TokioChildProcess::new(cmd)` inherits stderr (`crates/openalpaca_mcp/src/transport/stdio.rs:79`; rmcp-3.5.1 `src/transport/child_process.rs:124-130`). A server's stderr therefore goes raw into `daemon.log`, unattributed, unbounded and unredacted, and a Failed row's detail is the rmcp error alone (`apps/openalpacad/src/managers/mcp.rs:1132-1136`). Now:
  - Spawn with `TokioChildProcess::builder(cmd).stderr(Stdio::piped())`, and a reader task that always drains the pipe, keeping the last 4 KB per connection.
  - Redact by the exact secret values this load resolved, plus generic patterns, in OpenAlpaca's own code (not a port of ZCode's regex table, audit §1).
  - Attach the tail to the Failed detail and to the crash warnings, wrapped with `wrap_untrusted_context` where it enters a tool result or a status rendering, and escaped with `escape_hidden` where a person sees it.
  - ZCode keeps a redacted 4,000-character tail, attached to failures only (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/adapters/src/mcp/index.ts:110).
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: a stub that writes a known secret value and 10 KB to stderr and exits leaves a Failed detail of at most 4 KB that ends with the stub's last line and shows the secret redacted; a stub writing 1 MB to stderr still answers `tools/list` (the pipe is drained); a planted U+202E in stderr is escaped on the row. Commands: `cargo test -p openalpaca_mcp --features test-utils`; `cargo test -p openalpacad -- managers::mcp routes::extensions`.
- **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Place.** After P19, which it needs (rmcp 3.5's builder; P19's prep rewrites `stdio.rs` with the process-group wrapper, which this composes with). In the merged order after P38.

#### The lead and its subagents

**N-LEAD-1 — `fix(core): a lead's run cancels the subagents it leaves running`** · Required · new (Z4-01's apply half; the completion guard on top is B-15, pending)
- **Contents.** Each subagent runs in a detached `tokio::task::spawn` on `cancel_token.child_token()` (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/runner/lead_agent/tools.rs:651`, `:661`). `run_lead_agent` returns when its loop exits and cancels nothing (`runner/lead_agent/mod.rs:725-764`). The dispatcher then calls `remove_cancellation_token` (`orchestrator/dispatcher/lead_agent.rs:1068`), which deletes the token without cancelling it (`context/shared/mod.rs:299-305`); its comment (`lead_agent.rs:1066-1067`) assumes nothing is left to stop. A lead that exits on MaxRounds, CostExceeded or the 600 s wait cap (`tools.rs:1206`) therefore leaves subagents calling the model outside every cap, writing project files and raising approval cards, out of `/cancel`'s reach, while the timeline shows them as interrupted (`lead_agent.rs:1012-1015`). Now:
  - When `run_lead_agent`'s loop returns, the run's subagents are cancelled (`ctx.cancel_task(&task_id)`, or the tracker-held child tokens) before `lead_agent.rs:1068`. The token goes first, because R45's process-group kill rides on it.
  - A bounded wait follows for their spans to close as Cancelled, with the detail "the run ended before this subagent finished", and the completion record lists those subagents.
  - The handles are held in a `JoinSet`, so a dropped run future aborts them too (M40's reason).
  - `docs/agent-loop.md`'s lead contract says so.
  - ZCode aborts every in-flight ask before it writes a run's terminal status (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/dynamic-workflow/src/engine/engine-settlement.ts:26).
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent:
  - a lead that exits on MaxRounds while a subagent's stub model call is pending: the subagent's span closes as Cancelled within the bound, its stub sees no further call, and the completion record lists it;
  - a subagent running a `sleep` command under `KillOnFail` is killed with its group when the lead exits;
  - dropping the run future aborts the subagents.

  Command: `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib runner::lead_agent orchestrator::dispatcher`.
- **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Place.** Before the cut: it touches `lead_agent/tools.rs`, which P36's prep edits. If not ready, before P36. It is first in the lead set, because N-LEAD-2 bounds a queued subagent by this cancel.

**N-LEAD-2 — `fix(core): a subagent spawn queues under the lead's bound`** · Required · new (Z4-02)
- **Contents.** The lead is told "Spawning is always immediate … queued if capacity is limited — this is handled automatically" (`runner/lead_agent/prompt.rs:130-132`). In fact `spawn_subagent` waits up to 30 s for a semaphore permit before it registers, then fails (`tools.rs:235-249`; register at `:649`). The stock limit is 6 (`config/daemon.toml:10`), while `spawn_subagents_batch` accepts 8 and spawns them one after another (`tools.rs:1001`, `:1026`), so entries 7 and 8 each block the lead's call for 30 s and then fail. Now: the run is registered as Queued and its run id returned at once; the permit is acquired inside the spawned task with `child_token.run_until_cancelled(sem.acquire_owned())` (M60's form), and the run is set Running when it arrives. The cap does not change, and the prompt's sentence becomes true.
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: a batch of 8 under a limit of 6 returns 8 run ids at once, two of them Queued, each Running when a slot frees, with no 30 s wait in the call; a queued subagent cancelled by N-LEAD-1's run end never starts. Command: `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib runner::lead_agent`.
- **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Place.** Before the cut, after N-LEAD-1; if not ready, before P36. M60 later moves the remaining waits to the same form.

**N-LEAD-3 — `fix(core): each subagent result reaches the lead once`** · Required · new (Z4-04; refines Stage 0 F2's sizing)
- **Contents.** `summary()` lists every subagent the run ever spawned, on every call, and sizes each answer by `answer_budget(inline_bytes, map.len())` (`runner/lead_agent/tracker.rs:47-52`, `:191-233`). A second wait therefore re-delivers batch 1, and each answer's budget shrinks: about 1,216 bytes each for 16 entries, against 3,200 for 8. Now: a reported flag per entry; `wait_for_subagents` returns head and tail only for entries not yet reported, shares the byte budget among those, and adds one line naming the run ids already reported, so a compacted lead knows to ask `check_subagent_status`; `check_subagent_status` keeps whole re-reads. The tool's description changes in the same commit, and the body names the Stage 0 F2 sizing it refines.
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: after two batches of 8, the second wait returns only batch 2's answers, at the 8-entry budget, and one line naming batch 1's ids; a later `check_subagent_status` on a batch-1 id returns its whole answer. Command: `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib runner::lead_agent`.
- **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Place.** Before the cut (`tracker.rs`, and `tools.rs`, which P36 edits); if not ready, before P36. Any order within the lead set after N-LEAD-1.

**N-LEAD-4 — `fix(core): a resumed run rebuilds its subagents from the session log`** · Required · new (Z4-05)
- **Contents.** Resume replays the lead's own rounds (`session_log/replay.rs:152-158`), but the tracker is built fresh, so `check_subagent_status` answers "Unknown subagent_run_id" (`runner/lead_agent/tools.rs:1155`) and `wait_for_subagents` answers "No subagents have been spawned yet." (`tracker.rs:193-195`). Span rows keep only 200 characters of a subagent's answer (`runner/span.rs:22`, `:100-102`). Now: each subagent's whole final answer is spilled under the session's `sessions/<id>/results/`; on resume the tracker is rebuilt from `SubagentOpen` and `SubagentClose` — a closed span becomes Completed or Failed with its kept answer, an open span Failed, "interrupted by the daemon's stop"; and the resume note names those subagents.
- **Gate.** A test that fails on the parent: a run with one finished and one running subagent is stopped and resumed; then `check_subagent_status` returns the finished one's whole answer and the running one as Failed (interrupted), `wait_for_subagents` reports both, and the resume note names them. Command: `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib runner::lead_agent session_log`.
- **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Place.** Before the cut (P36 edits `lead_agent/tools.rs` and `session_log/record.rs`); if not ready, before P36.

**N-LEAD-5 — `fix(core): a subagent's failure names its cause and whether a retry can help`** · Required · new (Z4-14)
- **Contents.** A failed subagent reads `Agent '<id>' failed: <LoopFinishReason Debug>.` (`runner/lead_agent/tools.rs:952-959`): a CostExceeded with no amount, or a debug-escaped `Error("…")`. Meanwhile the prompt tells the lead to retry failures (`prompt.rs:135-136`). Now the failure is rendered by a lead-facing reason function that shares `LoopResult::no_answer_line`'s wording (`runner/agentic_loop/config.rs:366`), and it says when a retry cannot help: the cost cap, an auth failure, no routable model. ZCode's subagent design says the same (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/docs/design/v2/tool/07-subagent.md:215-216).
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: a CostExceeded subagent's failure line names the cap and its amount and says a retry will not help; an `Error` exit names the error without debug escaping. Command: `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib runner::lead_agent`.
- **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Place.** Before the cut (P36 edits `tools.rs`); if not ready, before P36.

#### Tools

**N-TOOL-1 — `fix(core): the shell's stated timeout and hint come from the enforced one; chaining wording fixed; the main loop reads max_tool_runtime_secs`** · Required · new (Z1-05's apply half; the per-tool policy, a model-settable timeout and the cap's value are B-11, pending)
- **Contents.** Today:
  - The main loop's tool timeout is `LoopConfig::default()`'s 60 s (`runner/agentic_loop/config.rs:179`; set at `apps/openalpacad/src/main.rs:478-483`; read at `orchestrator/query_handler/simple_query_handler.rs:340`; enforced at `security/sandbox/mod.rs:543`), and the daemon key `agent_defaults.max_tool_runtime_secs` (`daemon_config/execution.rs:36`) is not read on this path.
  - `shell_execute` tells the model "Timeout: 300 seconds" (`tools/builtins/shell_execute.rs:18`, `:126`).
  - Its description says chaining and pipes are allowed (`:124-125`) while its parameter says "single command only, no chaining" (`:135`).
  - The loop's recovery hint says "increase timeout", and no such parameter exists (`runner/agentic_loop/tool_helpers.rs:83-85`).

  Now: the stated timeout and the hint are generated from the timeout the sandbox enforces for the calling loop; the description and the parameter say the same thing about chaining, namely what the sanitizer actually admits (read before writing); and the main loop reads `agent_defaults.max_tool_runtime_secs`, or the body says why it does not. The cap's value (60 s or 300 s) is B-11's. R45's kill at the timeout is unchanged.
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: the main loop's `shell_execute` definition states the timeout the sandbox will enforce (60 s by default), and a changed `max_tool_runtime_secs` changes both the enforced cut and the stated figure; a timed-out command's hint names no `timeout` parameter; the description and the parameter agree on chaining. Command: `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib tools::builtins runner::agentic_loop orchestrator::query_handler`.
- **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Place.** Before the cut (`simple_query_handler.rs` is in P36's prep); if not ready, before P36.

**N-TOOL-2 — `fix(core): validate and gate a call before its confirmation card`** · Required · new (Z1-04)
- **Contents.** The sandbox raises the card after `confirmation_preview` (`security/sandbox/mod.rs:353-372`), which returns `Ok(None)` for any tool without a preview hook (`tools/registry/mod.rs:713-723`; default `:337-343`). The extension gate and `validate_tool_arguments` run only at dispatch (`:792`, `:797`, `:1523-1583`), so a person can approve a call that is refused after the click. Now: a `ToolRegistry` precheck — `validate_tool_arguments` and a non-holding extension-ledger check — is called in the sandbox before the card. Dispatch keeps its guarded check, and the drain guard is still taken at dispatch. The refusals are the same, only earlier: today's path fails closed, so this saves a wasted click and adds no refusal. ZCode validates and resolves a call before hooks and permission (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/core/src/tool/executor/call-runner.ts:196-260).
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: a call with an argument the schema refuses raises no card and returns the validation error; a call to a disabled extension's tool raises no card and returns the withheld-capability refusal; a valid call still raises its card. Command: `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib security::sandbox tools::registry`. A test appended to `tools/registry/tests.rs` or `security/sandbox/tests.rs` before G-base's run of record re-runs the driver's `--dry-run` (rule (b)).
- **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Place.** Before the cut (`tools/registry/mod.rs` is in P36's prep); if not ready, before P36.

**N-TOOL-3 — `fix(core): serialise non-read-only tool calls in a round`** · Recommended — a fix, presented because it changes a round's latency; it lands unless the owner declines it · new (Z1-01)
- **Contents.** Every executable call in a round runs under `join_all` (`runner/agentic_loop/mod.rs:1133`, `:1157`, `:1171`). Neither `file_ops.rs` nor `pre_edit.rs` takes a lock, and `file_edit`'s body has await points (`file_ops.rs:933-1012`). Bodies interleave once each call has passed its gate: on an approval-cache hit after Always allow, under `auto_approve`, in a sandbox with no broker, or when two answers arrive within a body's run time. Normally the second edit plans again after the first write lands (`file_ops.rs:974` against `:1068`). Now:
  - Each round is partitioned in model order. Consecutive calls whose tool declares `read_only_hint = true` (for MCP, also `idempotent_hint = true`) share a group; every other call is a group of one. Groups run in order; calls within a group run concurrently.
  - `spawn_subagents_batch`, `wait_for_subagents` and `check_subagent_status` stay concurrent-safe. Plugin tools, which carry no annotations, run alone.
  - Results keep model order, and cancellation is unchanged. (`RegisteredTool.annotations` lives in `tools/registry/mod.rs`.)
  - ZCode runs calls in parallel only when they are concurrent-safe, read-only or free of side effects (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/core/src/tool/scheduler.ts:85-104, :154-194).
- **Gate.** A test that fails on the parent: two `file_edit` calls on one file in one round, under a pre-approved (EntireTool-cached) or `auto_approve` policy, both land. A test with cards answered would likely pass today (audit Z1-01). Two read-only calls still overlap in time. Command: `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib runner::agentic_loop tools::builtins`.
- **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Place.** After P38, in the merged order: it touches `runner/agentic_loop/mod.rs`, which no commit of this plan rewrites. If it adds an accessor to `tools/registry/mod.rs`, it keeps clippy at 0, like every commit after P36.

**N-TOOL-4 — `fix(llm,core): tool errors carry is_error; hints move into the tools`** · Required · new (Z1-13)
- **Contents.** The Anthropic `tool_result` is sent without `is_error` (`crates/openalpaca_llm/src/providers/anthropic/request.rs:153-161`). Success is inferred from a `[tool_error]` text prefix (`runner/agentic_loop/mod.rs:1183`), and recovery hints are chosen by tool name and English substrings (`tool_helpers.rs:67-90`), one of them wrong (N-TOOL-1). Now: `is_error` travels on the tool-result message and maps to Anthropic's field; OpenAI-compatible providers keep the text prefix, because their wire has no field; the loop's success test reads the flag; and each hint moves into its tool's own error text. `docs/api/crates/openalpaca_llm.md` is regenerated if the message type is public.
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: a failed tool's Anthropic request body carries `"is_error": true` (a request-body pin); the loop counts a failure from the flag when the text does not start with `[tool_error]`; the OpenAI-compatible body is unchanged. Commands: `cargo test -p openalpaca_llm --features anthropic,openai`; `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib runner::agentic_loop`.
- **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Place.** Before the cut (`anthropic/request.rs` is in P36's prep), after N-TOOL-1, whose corrected hint it moves; if not ready, before P36.

**N-TOOL-5 — `fix(llm,core): malformed tool arguments are logged and every issue reported`** · Required · new (Z1-14)
- **Contents.** Two sites silently replace unparsable arguments with `{}`: `crates/openalpaca_llm/src/streaming.rs:93-94` and `crates/openalpaca_llm/src/providers/openai/response.rs:63`. `validate_tool_arguments` reports only the first problem and rejects a stringified object (`tools/registry/mod.rs:1523-1583`). Now, at both sites: a warning once, with the tool name and the byte length, never the content; a top-level string that parses to an object is accepted; and every issue is reported in one result, in OpenAlpaca's own wording. The contract follows ZCode:docs/model-tool-call-validation.md:89-172; its wording is not ported (audit §1).
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: a streamed tool call whose arguments are a JSON string holding `{"path":"a"}` is accepted with `path = "a"`; arguments that do not parse produce one warning naming the tool and the length, and no call with `{}`; a call with two schema violations reports both. The stream cases follow CLAUDE.md's *Gotchas* rule for OpenAI-compatible streams (the single-frame tool call among them). Commands: `cargo test -p openalpaca_llm --features openai`; `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib tools::registry`.
- **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Place.** Before the cut (`tools/registry/mod.rs` is in P36's prep); if not ready, before P36.

**N-TOOL-6 — `fix(core): an empty successful result says so`** · Required · new (Z1-15)
- **Contents.** A successful shell command with no output returns `""` (`tools/builtins/shell_execute.rs:47-60`), and so does an MCP result with no content (`tools/mcp/bridge.rs:84-107`). In one place in the loop, an empty `Ok` result becomes `(<tool> completed with no output)`; the tools themselves, `bridge.rs` among them, are untouched. ZCode does the same in one place (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/core/src/tool/executor/result-serialization.ts:57-68).
- **Gate.** A test that fails on the parent: a stub tool returning `Ok("")` reaches the model as the placeholder naming the tool. Command: `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib runner::agentic_loop`.
- **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Place.** After P38, in the merged order: it touches only `runner/agentic_loop/mod.rs`. Doing it in the loop keeps it off `bridge.rs`, a `step-6-rmcp.patch` file.

**N-TOOL-7 — `fix(core): a command's spill stub previews head and tail`** · Required · new (Z1-16; amends `tasks/api-fix-plan.md` §5.4's stub text)
- **Contents.** `spill_stub` carries the first 2 KB and a `result_ref` (`session_log/record.rs:185-190`); only the Err path keeps head and tail (`utils/text.rs:77`). For `shell_execute` and command tools, the stub now previews head and tail within the Err path's budget, so a long build's failing tail is in the stub. `spill_stub` is `tasks/api-fix-plan.md` §5.4's stub verbatim (*Spill, don't truncate*), so that text and its pinned tests change in the same commit, with that plan's rev line and log, and so does CLAUDE.md's *Gotchas* bullet on oversized tool results ("the first 2 KB plus `result_ref`").
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: a spilled `shell_execute` result whose last line is unique has that line in its stub; a spilled result of a tool that is not a command keeps today's head-only stub; the pinned stub-text test is updated to the new form. Command: `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib session_log runner::agentic_loop`.
- **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Place.** Before the cut (`session_log/record.rs` is in P36's prep); if not ready, before P36.

#### Skills

**N-SKILL-1 — `fix(skills): every available skill reaches the catalog, and the lead gets it`** · Required · new (Z3-02)
- **Contents.** Today:
  - `<available_skills>` has a 500-character budget and `break`s at the first line that does not fit (`orchestrator/query_handler/mod.rs:255-278`), so later skills drop without a word.
  - Its header says the *user* can invoke the skills with slash commands (`:262`), although the main loop holds `invoke_skill`.
  - A skill with no slash is listed by its display name (`:269`), which `invoke_skill` cannot resolve (`tools/builtins/invoke_skill.rs:159-162`; `orchestrator/skill/catalog/mod.rs:527-551`).
  - The lead holds `invoke_skill` and is told to use catalog skills (`runner/lead_agent/prompt.rs:16-22`), but its prompt passes `skills_catalog: None` (`:248`) in `SubagentMinimal` mode, which renders no catalog (`compose/static_prompt.rs:195-214`).

  Now one renderer serves the main loop and the lead, built from the availability-filtered `catalog_summary` (`skill/catalog/mod.rs:576`): each line gives the id, the `/slash` and the description cut with an ellipsis; over the budget it falls back to names only; the budget lives in `[orchestrator.prompt_budgets]`; the wording names `invoke_skill`; and for the lead the same block is a raw block beside `extension_tools_suffix`. The lead's prompt goldens move in this commit. ZCode's catalog falls back to names only so that no skill silently disappears (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/core/src/context/sections/skills.ts:9-10, :36-78).
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: with 20 skills the block lists every one (names only past the budget) and drops none; a skill without a slash is listed by its id, which `invoke_skill` resolves; the lead's prompt contains the catalog block (golden). Command: `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib orchestrator::query_handler runner::lead_agent compose`.
- **Back-out.** Revert (with the goldens).
- **Place.** Before the cut: `skill/catalog/mod.rs` is in P36's prep, and M07 edits it (A-38: land before M07, or rebase M07). If not ready, before P36.

**N-SKILL-2 — `feat(skills): skill diagnostics on the API, CLI and Settings; the level-1 scan bounded`** · Required · new (Z3-03, Z3-10; it carries the `unknown_key` code that P30's warning uses, A-07)
- **Contents.** A SKILL.md that cannot be read or parsed, or that conflicts on its slash, is skipped with a log warning only (`orchestrator/skill/catalog/mod.rs:272-281`, `:296-300`, `:336-345`, `:433-442`). `GET /v1/skills` lists only loaded skills (`apps/openalpacad/src/routes/skills.rs:110-172`), and the CLI has no skills command. Now:
  - The catalog keeps the last scan's diagnostics `{code, severity, path, skill_id?, message}`, with the codes `read_failed`, `missing_frontmatter`, `invalid_yaml`, `missing_field`, `id_mismatch`, `deprecated_field`, `slash_conflict`, `unknown_key` and `too_large`.
  - They are served on a new `GET /v1/skills/diagnostics`, so `GET /v1/skills` stays a bare array (api-fix-plan §7).
  - `openalpaca skills list|info` and a count in Settings → Tools show them; `docs/api`, `API_MAP.md` and CLAUDE.md's route and code-navigation rows change in the same commit.
  - The boot scan (`skill/catalog/mod.rs:286-292`, `:339-340`; `apps/openalpacad/src/services/mod.rs:195-209`) requires a regular file (an owner's own link is still followed) and caps the level-1 read with a `too_large` diagnostic.
  - The messages are `SkillParseError`'s Display strings, which `modernization-plan.md` M36 golden-tests.
  - It is a complement to E6's `yamlcmp`, not the gate P30 lacks.
  - ZCode turns every scan error but ENOENT into a diagnostic (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/adapters/src/skills/scan.ts:11-15).
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: a malformed SKILL.md, a slash conflict and a FIFO named SKILL.md each yield their diagnostic on `GET /v1/skills/diagnostics` and in `openalpaca skills list`, the FIFO without blocking the scan; an oversized file yields `too_large`; `GET /v1/skills` is unchanged. Commands: `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib orchestrator::skill`; `cargo test -p openalpacad -- routes::skills`; `cargo test -p openalpaca`; the four bun gates; `gen_api_docs.py --check` after the regeneration.
- **Back-out.** Revert (with the regenerated docs).
- **Place.** Before the cut: `skill/catalog/mod.rs` is in P36's prep, and the audit wants this before P30 and M36 (A-38). If not ready, in the wave before P30. Its tests do not append to `middleware/skill/tests.rs` before G-base's run (rule (b)). Rule (b) binds `middleware/skill/mod.rs` too, a `step-8-config.patch` file: `SkillParseError` and its `Display` (`:21-42`), whose strings are this entry's messages, sit directly above the patch's first hunk (`@@ -45,13`, `tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/gbase/patches/step-8-config.patch:4`; the second, `@@ -59,8`, at `:41`), and the serde_yaml parse calls (`mod.rs:51-66`) lie inside those hunks. Six of the nine codes (`read_failed`, `id_mismatch`, `deprecated_field`, `slash_conflict`, `unknown_key`, `too_large`) have no variant today. Before G-base's run of record this commit edits nothing inside `mod.rs:45-80`, and if it shifts `mod.rs`'s lines, a variant added within `:21-42`, the driver's `--dry-run` is re-run green on the tree carrying it, as N-TOOL-2 and N-MCP-2 do for their files.

**N-SKILL-3 — `fix(skills): create-skill writes only where the daemon reads`** · Required · new (Z3-08)
- **Contents.** create-skill's `scope=user` writes to `$HOME/.config/openalpaca/skills` (`config/skills/create-skill/scripts/write_skill_md.sh:84-86`; `scaffold.sh:59`; `check_exists.sh:31`; `list_skills.sh:58`). The daemon never scans that directory — it reads `<config>/skills` only (`apps/openalpacad/src/services/mod.rs:195-209`) — the directory lies outside `~/.openalpaca` (D1), and the skill still reports success. Now: the `user` scope is dropped, or refused naming the one root; `SkillScope::User`'s comment is fixed (`middleware/skill/types.rs:13`); and because the seeding gate never overwrites an existing skill directory (`apps/openalpacad/src/bootstrap/content.rs:61-71`), the switch-over note in `docs/Installation_Manual.md` says existing installs keep the old scripts. `list_skills.sh`'s frontmatter grep (`:39-42`) is P30's (A-07).
- **Gate.** A test that fails on the parent: `write_skill_md.sh` run with `scope=user` under a scratch `HOME` writes nothing outside the config root and exits non-zero naming it (a scripted run in the crate's tests, or one recorded in the body if no harness runs the scripts).
- **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Place.** After P38, in the merged order: scripts and a comment are in no commit's way.

**N-SKILL-4 — `fix(skills): say when a skill's instructions were cut`** · Required · new (Z3-09)
- **Contents.** `skill_to_prompt_block` keeps `budget_tokens*4` characters (16,000 by default) with no marker (`middleware/skill/renderer.rs:41-57`). Now one line names the cut: "instructions cut at N of M characters".
- **Gate.** A test that fails on the parent: a 20,000-character body renders with the line naming 16,000 of 20,000; a short body renders unchanged. The test sits outside `middleware/skill/tests.rs`'s last lines (rule (b)). Command: `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib middleware::skill`.
- **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Place.** Before the cut. `renderer.rs` is a `step-8-config.patch` file, but its hunk `@@ -9` lies above `:41`, so an edit there shifts nothing the patch anchors on; P30 rewrites the file. If not ready, before P30.

#### Plugins

**N-PLUG-1 — `fix(plugins): an isError result is an error`** · Required · new (Z2-02)
- **Contents.** `PluginToolProxy::execute` joins `content[].text` and returns `Ok` whatever `isError` says (`crates/openalpaca_plugins/src/bridge/tool_bridge.rs:46-66`), while the MCP bridge maps `is_error` to `Err` (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/tools/mcp/bridge.rs:102-104`). Now `isError: true` maps to `Err(joined text)`, and the bridge's comment states that a plugin's `tools/call` result follows MCP's `CallToolResult`, which no other document says.
- **Gate.** A test that fails on the parent: a plugin stub answering a single-frame `{content: [{type: "text", text: "boom"}], isError: true}` yields `Err("boom")`. Command: `cargo test -p openalpaca_plugins`.
- **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Place.** After P38, before M58 (a) (A-34): `tool_bridge.rs` is in no commit of this plan.

**N-PLUG-2 — `feat(plugins): warn on unknown manifest keys and unwired types`** · Required · new (Z2-04; the warning half of the owner's taken D-I (revised), extended to connectors and unknown keys)
- **Contents.** `PluginManifest` has no `deny_unknown_fields` and no warning for an unknown key (`crates/openalpaca_plugins/src/manifest.rs:7-72`). A typo such as `tool = true` — the key is `types.tools`, and tool discovery runs only under it (`manager.rs:1237`) — loads a plugin with zero tools and no message. `types.connector` and `types.provider` are probed (`:1247`, `:1268`) and listed in the preview with no caveat (`install.rs:196-202`), though neither bridge has a production caller. Now a warning — never a refusal — for an unknown key, and for `types.connector` or `types.provider` while their bridges are unwired, in the validate dry run, the install and approve preview, the row's `warnings` and the log. If the owner deletes the connector bridge (Z2-12's connector half, a new pending row), the connector warning goes with it. ZCode reports recognised-but-unsupported components (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/adapters/src/plugins/index.ts:112, :378-390).
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: a manifest with `tool = true` validates with a warning naming `tool`; a manifest with `types.provider = true` warns that the provider bridge is not wired; both still install. Commands: `cargo test -p openalpaca_plugins`; `cargo test -p openalpacad -- routes::extensions`.
- **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Place.** Before the cut (`openalpaca_plugins/src/manager.rs` is in P36's prep); if not ready, before P36.

**N-PLUG-3 — `docs(plugins): the approve surface says what approving grants`** · Required · new (Z2-05)
- **Contents.** The GUI manual says "`Approve` grants exactly those strings" (`docs/GUI_Manual.md:1332`; also `apps/openalpaca/src/commands/ext.rs:602`, `:1144`). In context the sentence describes what the consent records, not a sandbox: the child is spawned as the owner, with the daemon's environment and its own directory as working directory (`crates/openalpaca_plugins/src/process_pool.rs:36-48`). Now one sentence sits under the approve preview in the CLI and the GUI, and in both manuals, in substance: approving starts the plugin's program as you, with the daemon's environment (less the master-key variable, Stage 0's F3) and full file and network access; its capabilities name what it contributes to agents, not what it can do. It cross-references `docs/Daemon_Manual.md:185`. If B-04 is taken, the environment clause changes with it. ZCode states the same boundary plainly (ZCode:docs/plugin-hooks-compat.md:23-29).
- **Gate.** The CLI's approve-preview test and the GUI's preview test pin the sentence; `cargo test -p openalpaca`; the four bun gates.
- **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Place.** After P38, in the merged order: the docs, `ext.rs` and the GUI preview are in no commit's way.

**N-PLUG-4 — `fix(plugins): an update rolls back a failed swap and recovers at boot`** · Required · new (Z2-09)
- **Contents.** `update_from_path` trashes the old tree and then commits the staged one, with no rollback between the two steps (`crates/openalpaca_plugins/src/manager.rs:2096-2099`). If the commit fails, `Staged`'s Drop sweeps the staged copy too (`install.rs:471-486`): the plugin is then absent, and its last good tree sits only in `.trash/`. Now: the trashed tree is restored when the commit fails, before the error returns; a one-line marker, `.staging/<id>.swap`, is written before the trash move and removed after the commit; and a boot reconcile pass that finds the marker restores the trashed tree and sweeps `.staging/` copies older than the process. D1 and the extension design's §1.3 rule 3 (nothing the owner dropped in is deleted) are kept. ZCode swaps a plugin tree atomically, with a transaction marker, a backup and recovery on read (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/adapters/src/plugins/atomic-directory.ts:19-120).
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: a commit step forced to fail leaves the old tree in place and returns the error; a marker left as by a crash between the trash move and the commit is recovered at the next manager start. Command: `cargo test -p openalpaca_plugins`.
- **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Place.** Before the cut (`manager.rs` is in P36's prep); if not ready, before P36.

**N-PLUG-5 — `fix(plugins): mcp_compatible plugins get the real MCP lifecycle`** · Required · new (Z2-08's option (a); option (b), deleting the flag, is the owner's alternative if Z2-07 is declined)
- **Contents.** When `mcp_compatible` is true, the manager skips `initialize` and sends `tools/list` first (`crates/openalpaca_plugins/src/manager.rs:1165-1190`, `:1518-1521`), and an rmcp server refuses any first message other than `initialize` (rmcp-0.16.0 `src/service/server.rs:195-201`). The fixture `tests/fixtures/echo-plugin` is a tolerant shell script, which is why the tests pass. Now such a plugin runs the lifecycle `openalpaca_mcp` runs after P19; the manuals state that an `mcp_compatible` plugin receives no `[config]`, and the validate step warns if one declares config keys (N-PLUG-2's warning path).
- **Gate.** A test that fails on the parent: a plugin fixture that refuses any first message but the lifecycle's, as an rmcp server does (scripted, so that no rmcp server feature enters the lock), loads and lists its tools; the echo fixture still loads. Command: `cargo test -p openalpaca_plugins`.
- **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Place.** After P19, whose lifecycle it reuses; in the merged order after P38.

**N-PLUG-6 — `fix(plugins,core): honour register_template's answer; unload removes only what the plugin owns`** · Required · new (Z2-01's apply half, = Z3-07; the collision skip rule itself is B-05, pending)
- **Contents.** `register_template` refuses a duplicate agent id by returning `false` (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/agent/registry/mod.rs:83-92`), and the plugin manager ignores the answer and records the id as its own (`crates/openalpaca_plugins/src/manager.rs:1432-1434`). At T2, entries are removed by id whoever owns them, and a tombstone naming the plugin is written (`manager.rs:953` → `orchestrator/skill/catalog/mod.rs:199-224`; `manager.rs:962` → `agent/registry/mod.rs:118-126`), and `remove` falls back to a scan by display name (`catalog/mod.rs:672-707`). So a plugin that answers `agent/info` with `writing_agent` deletes the shipped template at its next disable. Now: the return value is honoured, and at T2 an entry is removed only when its source is this plugin (`SkillSource::Plugin { plugin_id }`, or the template's source), never through the name-scan fallback. What a plugin may claim at E4 when a live incumbent holds the name (the skip rule, on both insert paths) is B-05; canonical `<plugin>:<id>` naming is a further owner option.
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: a plugin answering `agent/info` with a shipped template's id leaves that template in place after the plugin's disable; a plugin's unload never removes a file skill through the display-name scan. Commands: `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib agent orchestrator::skill`; `cargo test -p openalpaca_plugins`.
- **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Place.** Before the cut (`manager.rs` and `skill/catalog/mod.rs` are in P36's prep); the audit wants it before any plugin-surface refactor (A-34). If not ready, before P36.

#### The daemon, the store and the GUI

**N-DAEMON-1 — `fix(daemon): each shutdown stage within the remaining window; discovery removed on forced exit; no follow-up autostart during shutdown`** · Required · new (Z4-15; Z4-03's apply half — what boot does with a follow-up a crash left `running` is B-14, pending)
- **Contents.** Today:
  - The extension sweep is bounded by what remains of the shutdown window (`apps/openalpacad/src/main.rs:891-895`); the connectors and the wake manager are not (`:899-904`; `apps/openalpacad/src/managers/connector.rs:254-259`), and they run before `remove_discovery` (`main.rs:908`).
  - The force-exit watchdog calls `process::exit(1)` (`:805-813`), leaving `discovery.json` behind.
  - Autostart at a run's end ignores shutdown (`orchestrator/dispatcher/lead_agent.rs:1073-1080`), and `claim_next` flips a follow-up to `running` (`crates/openalpaca_storage/src/repository/followup/mod.rs:341-347`) that only a returning gateway turn marks done (`apps/openalpacad/src/followup.rs:28-71`).

  Now: the connector and wake stages are bounded by what remains of `FORCE_EXIT_GRACE`; the watchdog's exit path removes `discovery.json` first; and autostart is skipped once the daemon's cancel token is set, `AppState.cancel_token` being threaded into the dispatcher, which holds only the run's token today (`lead_agent.rs:531`). CLAUDE.md's daemon-lifecycle residue line ("a forced exit … still skips `remove_discovery()`") is updated. ZCode bounds each shutdown stage (ZCode:docs/cli-bounded-shutdown.md:30-39, :56-70).
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: a connector whose shutdown never returns does not hold the shutdown past the window (the `graceful_shutdown_tests` pattern, with no store); the watchdog's exit path removes the discovery file it is given; a run that ends after the cancel token is set starts no follow-up. Commands: `cargo test -p openalpacad --bin openalpacad -- graceful_shutdown followup`; `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib orchestrator::dispatcher`.
- **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Place.** Before the cut (`main.rs` is in P41's prep `96a1dc30`); if not ready, before P41.

**N-STORE-1 — `fix(store,daemon,cli): Open copies and editor buffers live under state/, not $TMPDIR`** · Required · new (Z8-03; applies D1)
- **Contents.** Every Open of an upload copies it to `$TMPDIR/openalpaca-open/<id>-<name>`, and nothing removes the copy (`apps/openalpacad/src/routes/files_types.rs:66-75`, `:128-143`). `openalpaca memory edit`, `memory add` and `persona edit user` use `$TMPDIR` buffers (`apps/openalpaca/src/commands/memory.rs:1277`, `:1526`; `commands/persona.rs:171`), and a refused edit keeps its file there (`memory.rs:1292-1296`). Neither a project's Delete data nor the factory reset can reach them. Now: Open copies are staged under `state/cache/open/<file_id>/` (0700, the original filename kept, which is the reason for staging), swept at boot and removed in the project purge by file id; the CLI's editor buffers sit in a 0700 directory under `state/`. CLAUDE.md's data-directory paragraph names both, and the residue recorded in `tasks/storage-design/01-current-layout.md:105` is closed there. ZCode moved its product's temp writes into its data root (ZCode:docs/tmpdir-storage-paths-spec.md:9-35).
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: an Open of an upload under a guarded home store stages the copy under `<store>/state/cache/open/<file_id>/` and leaves a scratch `TMPDIR` empty; the project purge removes the staged copy; a boot sweep removes a stale one; the CLI's edit buffer is created under `state/` at mode 0700. Commands: `cargo test -p openalpacad -- routes::files`; `cargo test -p openalpaca_storage project_life`; `cargo test -p openalpaca`.
- **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Place.** Before the cut (`files_types.rs` is in P17's prep `75358018`); if not ready, before P17.

**N-GUI-1 — `fix(gui): the oa-* storage keys are namespaced by the served store`** · Required · new (Z6-10's apply half; the D1 record of the webview's own storage is B-27, pending)
- **Contents.** The project selection and the pane widths live in WKWebView's localStorage (`apps/openalpaca-gui/src/stores/project.ts:13`, `:100-108`; `stores/pane-widths.ts:82-93`). That storage sits under the bundle identifier in `~/Library`, outside `~/.openalpaca`, and neither `OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE` nor the factory reset touches it; Tauri's `data_store_identifier` needs macOS 14, and `data_directory` is unsupported on macOS (tauri-2.12.3 `src/webview/webview_window.rs:1194-1205`). Now the `oa-*` keys are namespaced by the served store's install id or root from `GET /v1/status`, so a different store starts empty. ZCode namespaces its renderer's storage by runtime environment (ZCode:packages/desktop/src/main/desktopRuntimeEnv.ts:60-80).
- **Gate.** A vitest that fails on the parent: two stores' status answers give two key sets, and a project chosen under one is absent under the other. The four bun gates; G-bundle leg A, with Q07's checks.
- **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Place.** After Q07, which rewires the GUI's host calls (§7.3), in Part II's order.

#### Owner decisions with a commit shape — pending, nothing adopted

The audit presents these as decisions (§4.4's second table, *Owner decisions with a commit shape*); the split between that table and its first, *Fixes within settled rules*, is the audit's, and this plan follows it without re-deciding it. Most of these add an enforcement point, widen a component or reverse a settled ruling. New surface alone does not make an entry a decision here: additive diagnostic or tuning surface within settled rules stays in the audit's first table and is scheduled above — N-SKILL-2's `GET /v1/skills/diagnostics` and `openalpaca skills` serve the catalog's own scan diagnostics, and N-SKILL-1's key moves a hard-coded budget (`orchestrator/query_handler/mod.rs:255-278`) into the existing `[orchestrator.prompt_budgets]` section (`config/daemon.toml:90`). Each is recorded in `tasks/pending-decisions.md` (rev 5) — beside an existing row where one exists, as a new row otherwise (A-42) — with ZCode's evidence and the audit's recommendation; where the audit offers only an option (B-11, B-13, B-14, B-15, B-17, B-23, B-24, B-25, B-26), that file gives the records' own recommendation and says so on the row, while the column below keeps the audit's text. The **no-change** answer is the shipped code. "Would sit" says where its commit goes if the owner takes it; none is scheduled, and none appears in a count.

| id | decision (from) | ZCode's evidence | recommendation (the audit's) | would sit | pending row |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B-01 | Always allow scoped per tool on the card, with a dangerous-command catalog as data (Z1-02) | per-tool "always allow" options (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/contracts/src/tools/contract.ts:106-114); a 13-rule catalog forcing a single-use ask (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/core/src/permission/service.ts:136-147) | the card declares what Always allow may mean — `shell_execute`: these exact arguments or none; `file_write`/`file_edit`: none or these arguments; extension tools: the whole tool — with the catalog; within R43; changes Round G's Always-allow contract and toast | after P38 | new, beside T35 and R43 |
| B-02 | T35: read plugin annotations; extension tools ask unless they declare themselves read-only (Z1-03, Z2-03) | every MCP tool needs approval whatever its hints (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/core/src/mcp/index.ts:148) | option (iv): ask unless `read_only_hint == Some(true)` and not `destructive_hint == Some(true)`, with B-01 keeping a whole-tool allow for extension tools; unattended turns then refuse such tools | after P19 | T35 |
| B-03 | A skill's confirmation list adds to the defaults; `skill_script:*` asks like the shell (Z3-01) | skill scripts run through its shell tool, under that tool's approval | union on all three skill paths, agent templates keeping R30 and R-T35; the shell's annotation for `skill_script:*`, with the narrower form for cron skills (attended turns ask, unattended follow T35); reverses R30 for skills | after P38 | new, beside T35 |
| B-04 | Extension children spawned without the daemon's secrets (Z1-06, Z5-11) | its own runtime and credential keys stripped by a denylist (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/adapters/src/mcp/network.ts:10-21) | the minimal form first: `env_remove` every `secret_env` name, every `mcp.toml` `*_env`/`*_from` name and `OPENALPACA_*`; a full allowlist as the stricter option; the shell keeps inheriting (ST-14) | after P19 (`stdio.rs`), and `process_pool.rs` | §1.2, new |
| B-05 | The plugin collision skip rule, amending the extension design's §10 case 13; canonical `<plugin>:<id>` naming as a further option (Z2-01, Z3-07) | canonical `<plugin>:<agent>` names and an ambiguity diagnostic (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/bootstrap/src/subagents.ts:135-148, :207-213) | skip, and record beside `skipped_tools`, a plugin skill id, slash, alias or agent id a live incumbent holds, on both insert paths | after N-PLUG-6 | new |
| B-06 | Consent bound to a digest of the declaration, `DeclarationChanged` (Z2-10) | hook trust keyed by a digest of the declaration (ZCode:packages/shared/src/workspace-hook-digest.ts:192-248) | a `consent_digest` over `entry`, `args`, `env`, `types` and capabilities; a mismatch at E1 treated like capability growth; no escape hatch (§13 Q4) | after P38 | new; informs the extension design's §13 Q4 |
| B-07 | SIGTERM grace and a detached reaper before the group kill, reversing R-T23 (Z1-07) | SIGTERM to the group and its descendants, SIGKILL after 1.5 s (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/adapters/src/exec/process-tree.ts:3-90) | take it **without** descendant signals, which would break R45's pid-reuse guarantee | after P38 | storage design §18 residue |
| B-08 | Compare-and-swap between the edit card and the write, S4b (Z1-08) | Edit refuses a file changed since it was read — related, not the same mechanism (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/core/src/tool/handlers/edit.ts:421-458) | record the target's size, mtime and SHA-256 when the card is planned; refuse a mismatch at the write | after P38 | storage design §18 S4b |
| B-09 | Connector approvals and work only from the bound person, code audit H1 and H2 (Z1-09, Z8-01) | one bound actor per bot; group commands owner-only (ZCode:packages/services/src/bots/groupSessions.ts:26-37) | take both, decided with MD-4 | after P38 | §3.3, §3.6 |
| B-10 | Deny with a reason, `feedback` (Z1-10) | offered on its V4 desktop and web surfaces (ZCode:docs/permission-project-approval.md:30-50) | an optional `feedback` of at most 4,096 characters on `POST /v1/chat/confirmations/{id}`, escaped wherever shown; the GUI, `--reason` in the CLI, `/no <text>` in the connectors; `docs/api` regenerated | after P38 | new |
| B-11 | A per-tool timeout policy, a model-settable `timeout_secs`, and the main loop's cap value (Z1-05 (2)) | a timeout policy per tool (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/contracts/src/tools/contract.ts:141-153) | `{default, max, call_override}` on `RegisteredTool`, taking the lower of it and the agent's cap; the cap 60 s or 300 s | after N-TOOL-1 | new |

| B-13 | Lead-only `cancel_subagent` and `message_subagent` (Z4-07) | a per-subagent inbox drained at its round boundary (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/docs/design/v2/tool/07-subagent.md:401, :433-450) | an owner option; the tracker keeps each child token, which N-LEAD-1 needs anyway | after N-LEAD-1 | new |
| B-14 | Boot settles a follow-up a crash left `running` (at most once) or re-queues it (Z4-03) | at-most-once settlement with a notice (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/core/src/runtime/methods/background-notifications.ts:58-82) | settle to `cancelled` with a visible notice, or re-queue as `recover_unprocessed_steering` does | after N-DAEMON-1 | new |
| B-15 | The completion guard on top of N-LEAD-1 (Z4-01) | none cited (ZCode aborts a run's in-flight asks at settlement, N-LEAD-1's evidence) | one more round for the lead to wait for, or explicitly abandon, its running subagents (precedent: the steering completion guard, `runner/agentic_loop/mod.rs:1318`) | after N-LEAD-1 | new |

## 6. Verification for Part I

**What CI covers** (first time for some of it): the Linux build and tests (the first Linux compile of zbus/keyring 4, sysinfo 0.39's Linux zombie path, the `managers::mcp` direct-child kill with process-wrap 10); clippy and `--locked` (DU-22); the four bun gates under the pinned Bun; Pester on Windows; the temporary Windows `cargo check` (P01a) or, once Q08 lands, the permanent three-OS shell-check; the advisories jobs (P34). *Rev 3.13 (SF-2, SF-17): CI covers nothing on this branch until §5.1 item 9's draft PR exists — `ci.yml:3-6` runs on `pull_request` and on pushes to `main` only, and PR #34 is merged — and the Linux compile of keyring-core's zbus store (P12), the libdbus removal (P13) and sysinfo's Linux path (P08) are read at item 9's second CI read, before P15; the Windows `cargo check` and Q08's Windows and Linux legs are deferred under DU-33, while the existing windows-latest Pester job runs on every PR and every push to `main`; Dependabot and the weekly `schedule:` run read the default branch, so P35's gate and the advisories' weekly run are judged after the merge.*

The sequence, **with Q02/Q03 landed**: `bun run prepare:sidecar:release` (cargo's own artifact, the `.sha256` and `.provenance.json` recorded), `bunx tauri build --bundles app --config '{"identifier":"com.tangyunpei.openalpaca-gui.wavecheck"}'` with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target/gui`, then compare the bundled `Contents/MacOS/openalpacad*` and the staged copy to the recorded `.sha256`. *Corrected, rev 3.13 (A2-11): with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target/gui` exported for `tauri build`, its `beforeBuildCommand` hook rebuilds and re-hashes the daemon into that target dir, so G-ident can end up comparing a build with itself. The sequence is `package-macos.sh`'s, replicated by hand — `bun run prepare:sidecar:release` (the hash and provenance recorded), then `bunx tauri build --bundles app --config '{"identifier":"com.tangyunpei.openalpaca-gui.wavecheck"}'` with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` **not** exported (`src-tauri/.cargo/config.toml` already sends the shell to `target/gui`), then the comparison — or `package-macos.sh` itself once it passes a `--config` through. Every `bunx tauri build` here carries the cache confinement of G-bundle's G-09 note (§5.2): `BUN_INSTALL_CACHE_DIR`, `BUN_RUNTIME_TRANSPILER_CACHE_PATH`, `npm_config_cache` and `npm_config_logs_dir` under the scratch root, and a closing `find -newermt` check over those directories only. The "Without them" branch that follows is dead since Q02/Q03 landed (rev 3.5) and is kept as the record of rev 3.1's hand sequence only.* **Without them**, by hand in a worktree or export of the wave tip (never the main checkout's staged binary): 1. `rm -f apps/openalpaca-gui/src-tauri/bin/openalpacad-*`; `cargo build --release --locked -p openalpacad` (**alone**, `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` unset because the hook reads `./target/release`); `shasum -a 256 target/release/openalpacad` **recorded now**; 2. `cd apps/openalpaca-gui && bun install --frozen-lockfile && bunx tauri build --bundles app --config '{"identifier":"…wavecheck"}'` — the hook finds its source newer than the absent copy, rebuilds the daemon alone (cargo: up to date) and stages it; 3. G-ident: the bundled daemon's hash == the recorded hash; markers from the chosen lock (`rmcp-3.5.0` ≥ 1, `rmcp-0.16.0` = 0, `3.53.2` ≥ 1 — *rev 3.13 (SF-17): derived from the wave tip's lock, not literals — `rmcp-<the 3.5.x the lock holds>` ≥ 1, `rmcp-0.16.0` = 0, the bundled SQLite version of the libsqlite3-sys the lock holds ≥ 1 — as G-ident says*); if the owner's `package-macos.sh` is used instead, its `libexec/openalpacad` must carry the same hash — which it cannot while the joint build stands (Q03). 4. Launch from the isolated shell (§5.2: the seeded `llm.toml` with discovery off; **rev 3.8: launcher (iii) leaves the webview unable to reach its daemon (Q00) — start the daemon first under launcher (i), then the app unsandboxed with the same store variables; the paragraph's original wording follows** — **the app under `sandbox-exec -f scripts/eval/memory/loopback-only.sb …/Contents/MacOS/openalpaca_gui`, directly, so the sidecar it spawns inherits the profile** — rev 3 wrote "the daemon under `loopback-only.sb`" with no mechanism that reaches a sidecar); run §5.2's two-request proof (localhost succeeds, a public host fails, from the sandboxed shell and from a `setsid`-detached child of it) **before the first chat turn**; before any write confirm the store root in Settings → Connection and `GET /v1/status` equals `$T/store` with `$T` canonical (§5.2), the `Config base dir:` line in `daemon.log`, and that no project is selected. **4a. Onboarding** (rev 3.1, §5.2): one attended turn asking for `update_persona` on both documents, then confirm `BOOTSTRAP.md` is gone — before 5; a refusal during onboarding is not an attempt. 5. **The checks** — rev 2.1's (the first-run card; Models & keys add a key → restart → it loads; Persona ETag / memory If-Match; Stop daemon then Start daemon — *rev 3.13 (MF-4): the restart and the Start go through §5.2's confined in-app Start: G-ident first on the untouched bundle, then, only in a copy used for these legs, `Contents/MacOS/openalpacad` replaced by the wrapper beside `openalpacad.real` (whose hash is recorded), or the Start done by hand under launcher (i); after each Start, Q00's confinement proof — `sandbox_check(network-outbound)` on the new daemon's pid, a refused public request in its log, and a `ps` check that the running pid is the confined one — before any further check; a bare in-app Start spawns the daemon with full outbound network (`src-tauri/src/lib.rs:275-305`), and every model turn after it would run unconfined*; Chat, Memory and Settings in WKWebView against a pre-wave bundle built the same way with a second throwaway identifier and G-ident's pre-wave markers) **plus the current contracts, on synthetic registered projects** (R28): project registration, re-point (`Re-point…`) and `Delete data…`; approval re-entry (`approval-card-returns.test.tsx` is the unit twin); the working state card; an upload and an artifact's Open and Reveal; the memory flows; **Stage 2** — a project `file_write`/`file_edit` with its confirmation card and the pre-edit snapshot under `sessions/<id>/snapshots/` (`pre_edit.rs:47` `check_pre_edit`, `:86` `keep_pre_edit_copy`), a projectless scratch write (`scratch/<conversation id>/`), a pointer row beside a by-product at the same address (`Results in the project` filter, no History/Diff), a missing/re-pointed project's `410 ARTIFACT_PROJECT_MISSING` on Preview/Open/Export and Reveal's refusal, and a project delete that keeps the real project file. 6. **Cleanup**: stop the recorded temporary daemon, wait for exit and lock release (the shell detached it, `lib.rs:362`), then `rm -rf "$T"` and the `~/Library` entries named after the throwaway identifier. *Rev 3.15 (D1 (a); - **E7 — a rollback rehearsal against E5's isolated store with a pre-wave build whose identity is checked, from the same application source.** `<pre-wave>` is the **wave's base commit** (a post-Stage-2 commit: `7295f91b` today or later; never `d484a56a`, which registers only schemas 1–3 and refuses a schema-4 file as "newer than this build" [M-today]). 1. In an export of `<pre-wave>` (`git archive … | tar -x -C $T/prewave`), `cargo build --release --locked -p openalpacad` **alone** (the daemon-only invocation, the same feature graph as the sidecar's; the CLI built separately) with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=$T/prewave-target` (*and `CARGO_BUILD_BUILD_DIR=$T/prewave-build` beside it — MF-5; harmless while the pre-wave export carries no `build-dir`*). 2. G-ident pre-wave markers on `$T/prewave-target/release/openalpacad`: `rmcp-0.16.0` ≥ 1, `3.51.1` ≥ 1 [M-sep: the markers exist in a pre-bump release build]. 3. Quit E5's app, stop its daemon (wait for exit + lock), start the pre-wave daemon in the isolated shell (`OPENALPACA_DAEMON_BIN` naming §5.2's **wrapper script** around `$T/prewave-target/release/openalpacad` — `exec sandbox-exec -f scripts/eval/memory/loopback-only.sb $T/prewave-target/release/openalpacad "$@"` — the pre-wave CLI's `openalpaca daemon start`, from a scratch directory; the two-request proof before any turn; *rev 3.13 (SF-13): §5.2's pre-seed is already in E5's store, and the log check is repeated for this daemon*) and check: the database opens (schema 4 both sides; files portable [M-sep on the old schemas, E8 on this one]); the key added in E5 decrypts (the provider lists its key as loaded) [M-sep]; the config reads; the synthetic project registered in E5 is listed and its pointer row resolves.
- **E8 — a rehearsal on a copy of the owner's real store, read-only, no daemon, before the first start of a bumped build on the real store.** It is the only check of the owner's existing partitions, snapshots and database under SQLite 3.53.2 and sha2 0.11 on schema 4 / cache 6. 1. Stop the real daemon (wait for process exit **and** lock release, `daemon_lifecycle`); `cp -Rp ~/.openalpaca "$T/realcopy"` (database with `-wal`/`-shm`, `state/memory-control/`, `memory/`, `projects/`, `scratch/`, `config/`); **the real daemon stays stopped until step 6 is done** (rev 3 said "restart if wanted" here; withdrawn in rev 3.1 — step 2 says why; step 9 is where it restarts). The copy holds `.master_key` and encrypted keys: keep it under `$T`, delete it afterwards. 2. In the isolated shell: `OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE=$T/realcopy`, `OPENALPACA_CONFIG_DIR=$T/realcopy/config`, `HOME=$T/home`, a scratch working directory outside any repository. **Then delete `$T/realcopy/state/discovery.json` and `$T/realcopy/state/openalpacad.lock` from the copy, or assert both are absent** (rev 3.1): `openalpaca memory doctor` branches on `crate::manager::is_daemon_running()` → `daemon_lifecycle::running_daemon_pid()` → `read_discovery()` → `pid_is_daemon(pid)` [C: `apps/openalpaca/src/commands/memory.rs:693`, `apps/openalpaca/src/manager.rs:120-122`, `crates/openalpaca_storage/src/daemon_lifecycle.rs:122-125`], and under `OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE=$T/realcopy` that reads the **copy's** `discovery.json`, which `cp -Rp` carried over; a graceful stop removes the file (`apps/openalpacad/src/main.rs:908`) but a forced exit does not (CLAUDE.md), so a copy carrying a pid that is alive again would make steps 5–6 call `Api::connect(&at)?.get("/v1/memories/doctor")` on the **real** daemon with its real token and compare the real store with itself. **Never start a daemon on the copy**: the learner's boot catch-up would call the model with the copy's real keys and write into each turn's real project root, and — Stage 2 — the copy's `in_project = 1` rows point at **real project files** which no copy isolated (§5.2). 3. **A raw read-only connection first, before any application opens the copy** (R08): `sqlite3 'file:$T/realcopy/state/openalpaca.db?mode=ro'` (or the rebuilt probe in `mode=ro`) — *rev 3.13 (A2-17, SF-15): in double quotes, `sqlite3 "file:$T/realcopy/state/openalpaca.db?mode=ro"` — the single quotes above keep the shell from expanding `$T` — and never with `immutable=1`, which skips the copied WAL and would undercount rows; the distinct `project_root` values are counted, not printed (F-04, the output rule below)* — record `schema_version` (expect 4), every `sqlite_master` entry (tables, index definitions — `idx_file_assets_addr` on `(COALESCE(project_root, ''), in_project, rel_path)`), per-table row counts, the count of `file_assets` rows with `in_project = 1` and their distinct `project_root` values (**inventory only — never stat or read those paths**), and the retryable-`Error` extraction rows P15 will re-extract. Both CLIs' `memory doctor` open the **migrating** database (`memory.rs:697` → `:1737`), so this capture is the baseline, not the doctor's. 4. **An independent inventory** (R10): `projects/*/project.json` (id, root, version), every owner directory under `memory/owners/` and `projects/*/memory/owners/`, every `state/memory-control/*.json` (partition key, bound root or project id, note count, tombstones) — the expected partition set, against which both doctors' reports are checked; a doctor that omits a snapshot whose id no folder records (`doctor.rs:168-172`; pinned by `memory_files/tests.rs:4194`) or continues after a listing error (`:154-160`) is **explained** by this inventory or the rehearsal fails. 5. **The pre-wave CLI** (E7's binaries): `openalpaca memory doctor --global`, no `--repair`; save the report. **Pass condition for steps 5 and 6** (rev 3.1): the saved output's first line is `No daemon is running; scanning locally.` (`memory.rs:696`) — a report without it came over HTTP from a daemon, and the rehearsal is void. **Home partitions will read as quarantined in both builds**: a home snapshot binds its absolute root (`snapshot.rs:283`) and the copy is at `$T/realcopy` — equal reports are relocation, not healthy reads (R09). Project partitions bind by id and live under the copied `projects/<name>/memory/`, so they are readable in place in the copy. 6. **The bumped CLI**, same command: the same report, partition for partition. 7. **The rebuilt current-source probe** (old/new variants from `<pre-wave>`, differing only in rusqlite/sha2): in read-only mode, `PRAGMA integrity_check`, `PRAGMA foreign_key_check`, FTS5 `integrity-check` on **all three** tables of the cache copy (*corrected, rev 3.13 (SF-15): FTS5's `integrity-check` is an `INSERT` and fails on a `mode=ro` connection with "attempt to write a readonly database" (measured by the review), while `PRAGMA integrity_check` already runs FTS5's integrity check read-only since SQLite 3.44 — so the read-only pass is the two pragmas, and the FTS5 `INSERT` form runs only on a second scratch copy of the cache opened read-write*), the same row counts as step 3 (equal), the same search over the index copy by both variants (same ids); and — the part that stands in for the home partitions' healthy read — for every control snapshot, re-derive each note file's revision hash and the partition key with both variants' `sha256_hex` and compare with the snapshot's recorded revisions, printing mismatch **counts** only, writing nothing (no `MemoryIndex::open` that would recreate a cache; no snapshot write; never `doctor --repair` on unknown state). 8. Reopen the copy with the pre-wave variant: equal integrity results and counts. 9. `rm -rf "$T"`; only now restart the real daemon, if wanted. The archived `sqlite-probe` is **not** this probe (R12: no `src/database/`, registries 43/44/45, cache 3, two FTS tables, failures printed not returned). *Rev 3.13:* **(SF-15)** the probe does not exist yet — `tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/` holds only `e6/` and `gbase/` — so it is a scheduled deliverable that moves no version, built and reviewed like the E6 tools (old and new variants from the branch point, differing only in rusqlite and sha2), with a Chunk 25 item of its own, before E8 can run. **(F-04) Output rule:** E8 prints what E6 prints — counts, health words and partition keys only; the doctor's full report (partition roots, note file names) and step 3's per-table output are saved under `$T`, outside the repository, and never pasted into a transcript; the evidence tarballs being git-ignored keeps them out of history, not out of a session. **(SF-1)** Instead of deleting the copy at this step the owner may keep it as §5.1 item 4's backup — moved to a 0700 directory outside any repository, its checksum list recorded — provided no daemon has started on the real store since step 1; otherwise item 4's backup is taken immediately before the first bumped start. **(MF-5)** The probe's builds set `CARGO_BUILD_BUILD_DIR` beside `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`.

**Load and timing.** A full suite is ≈ 5.5 min here [M-today]; measure on a quiet machine or rerun a flaky module before calling a failure a regression.

**Runner images.** The PR runs in the 2026-10-19 … 2026-11-19 window: P01b pins `ubuntu-24.04`, P39 reverts it last and its own run is the deliberate move to 26.04. *Rev 3.15 (the compatibility report §2.1, §7):* jsdom 30.1.2 (P31) declares `engines.node` `^22.22.2 || ^24.15.0 || >=26`, met by the 24.04 image's default 22.23.3, by 26.04's 24.21.0 (§9) and, locally, exactly by 24.15.0; Bun does not enforce `engines` at install, so a runner below the floor would show only at test time — P39's own run is where 26.04's Node is read.

### 7.1 Goal and assumption

The owner asked for apps that are "more flexible". Rev 3 reads that as the 10-10 audit's G1–G8 — a thin Tauri 2 shell over the independently running daemon (one installer, React + Vite, a small native shell, the daemon owning migrations, agents, tools and the API; the GUI and the CLI sharing it; closing a window never stops background work), a browser-first React dev loop, and low memory / fast startup / fast compilation **measured before promised**. One sentence from the owner replaces that reading. No framework migration, no Tauri 3 (alpha only), no CEF: the current boundary is improved first (§7.8; rev 3–3.12 cited a §7.9, which does not exist — rev 3.13, A1-15).

Everything quantitative here is a crate count from `cargo tree --locked` [M-today] or a line count from `fn_ranges.py` [M-today]; **no compile time, binary size, RAM or startup figure exists** — nothing was built or launched for Part II [NE]. *(Dated, rev 3.13: true when written. Q00 has since measured the build times, the packaged app's daemon-ready time and the daemon's idle memory on `a91f4b23` (rev 3.8, `tasks/deps-upgrade/baselines/2026-10-12/README.md`). The GUI rows a person owes are §5.1 item 11's, under owner decision D13, pending.)* The frontend-asset numbers (740,645 B JS, a 1.15 s `vite build` [A10]) are not startup or memory evidence.

### 7.2 The architecture (the architect's blueprint, `SP/reports/architect.md`)

**G1 — the shell leaves `openalpaca_storage`.** The shell (`apps/openalpaca-gui/src-tauri/src/lib.rs`; `main.rs` uses nothing) uses **17** storage items, all file-system or process-table reads [C]: `discovery::read_discovery` (`:27`, `:45`, `:58`), `ensure_not_expired` (`:31`), `ConnectionInfo` (`:26-61`), `Discovery` (`:257`), `daemon_lifecycle::wait_for_daemon_exit` + `STOP_TIMEOUT` (`:170`), `StopOutcome` (`:124-149`), `store::daemon_log_path` (`:71`, `:103`, `:325`), `read_log_tail` (`:72`, `:104`), `logs_dir` (`:324`), `rotate_daemon_log` (`:326`), `MANAGED_LOG_ENV` (`:350`), `ensure_store(&StoreScope::Home)` + `StoreScope` (`:312`), `ensure_runtime_config_dir` (`:313`), `revealable_path` + `RevealRefusal` (`:198`). Its manifest also declares `directories` and `serde_json`, which `lib.rs` never names [M-today]. What those pull: `daemon_lifecycle` → `sysinfo` (`:76-78`), `read_discovery`, the singleton lock (`file-lock`); `reveal` → `home_root` and **`projects::list`** (`reveal.rs:92`, `:115`) → `list_in` → `read_project_dir` → `fsx::read_if_exists`/`read_regular_nofollow`, `serde_json`, `canonicalize`, `root_is_missing`, the duplicate-id filter, `missing_project_sentence` — and through `lookup`, `folder_name`/`hashed_folder_name` (`unicode-normalization`, `sha256_hex` → `sha2`), `canonical_root` (`fsx::canonical_path_lenient`), `ProjectId` (`uuid`); `ensure_store(Home)` → `directories::BaseDirs`, the README const, `ensure_layout` (`uuid::new_v4`, `write_atomic`); the discovery reader → `serde`, `serde_json`, `chrono` [C]. **So Reveal needs the one canonical file-backed project reader, not only `home_root`** (A10's correction of A09) — and that reader needs no SQLite.

The graph [M-today: `cargo +1.99.0 tree -p openalpaca_gui -e normal --locked`, dedupe and `--no-dedupe`]: **245** distinct `(name, version)` crates; **60** under storage; **226** reachable without it; **18** reachable only through storage. After the extraction **7 leave** — rusqlite 0.38.0, libsqlite3-sys 0.36.0, hashlink 0.11.1, hashbrown 0.16.1, fallible-iterator 0.3.0, fallible-streaming-iterator 0.1.9, similar 3.2.0 — and 9 under W2 (+ rand 0.10.2, chacha20 0.10.2, the discovery writer's); sysinfo, objc2-io-kit, unicode-normalization, tinyvec, chrono, iana-time-zone, num-traits and file-lock **stay** through support; sha2 (also tauri-codegen), uuid (also tauri-utils, tauri-codegen, schemars, cfb), base64 (also plist, swift-rs, tauri-codegen), serde_json, tracing, anyhow, libc stay whatever happens. **245 → 238 (236)** — a small drop; the gain is that **no edit under `crates/openalpaca_storage/` rebuilds the shell**, which is G1's purpose. The shell has no HTTP client in its graph (reqwest/hyper/ureq: 0) [M-today]. For comparison: the daemon's normal graph 364, storage's own 58 [M-today].

**The `openalpaca_app_support` crate** (name a proposal; `openalpaca_host` the alternative).
