**Risk — the discovery wire format.** The daemon writes `discovery.json` with `serde_json::to_vec_pretty` (`discovery/mod.rs:95`; field order = struct order; chrono's RFC 3339 with a variable fraction) and the CLI and the shell read it; **no test pins the bytes** [M-today]. Moving the types changes no derive and should be byte-identical — "should" is the gap. **Q01** lands first: `discovery_json_bytes_are_pinned` (a `Discovery` with fixed `DateTime` literals and a fixed token, `to_vec_pretty` equal to a literal), the `ConnectionInfo` serde names exactly `baseUrl`/`token`/`instanceId`, a captured real file round-tripped; kept in storage as a cross-crate check and copied to support.

**G3-a — HostBridge** (DU-28). Every `@tauri-apps/*` reach under `apps/openalpaca-gui/src` [M-today: grep, non-test]: `lib/connection.ts:26` (`invoke` import), `:79` (`get_connection_info` / `ensure_daemon_running`, validated by `isConnectionInfo` `:59-67`), `:106` (`read_daemon_log_tail`), `:241` (`await_daemon_stopped` → `DaemonStopReport` `:220-231`); `lib/reveal.ts:13`, `:43` (`reveal_path`), `:25` (`canReveal` on `globalThis.isTauri`); `components/shell/TrafficLights.tsx:18`, `:24-27`, `:40-42` (`getCurrentWindow` → `isDecorated`/`close`/`minimize`/`toggleMaximize`; browser fallback `settle(false)`); `views/settings/AddExtensionForm.tsx:44-45` (dynamic `@tauri-apps/plugin-dialog` `open({ directory: true })`). Consumers: `lib/events.ts:187-188`, `lib/http.ts:159`, `lib/chat-stream.ts:526`, `lib/api/telemetry.ts:73`, `hooks/useConnection.ts`, `lib/daemon-control.ts:50`, `views/settings/ConnectionSection.tsx:60`, `:332`, `:372`, `hooks/useFiles.ts:78`, `canReveal()` at `components/chat/FilePanel.tsx:331`, `components/chat/WrittenArtifactCard.tsx:102`, `views/library/LibraryDetailHeader.tsx:174`, `<TrafficLights/>` at `components/shell/NavRail.tsx:59`; **23 test files** `vi.mock("@tauri-apps/api/core")`. The interface (`lib/host/types.ts`): `HostBridge { kind: "tauri" | "dev-server" | "fixture"; ensureDaemonRunning(); getConnectionInfo(); readDaemonLogTail(lines); awaitDaemonStopped(); reveal: null | { label(); inFolder(path) }; dialog: null | { pickDirectory() }; window: null | { isDecorated(); close(); minimize(); toggleMaximize() } }`, with `ConnectionInfo` gaining `version: string | null; protocol: number | null` (§ G3-b). `lib/host/index.ts` chooses once at module load: `isTauri()` from `@tauri-apps/api/core` → `TauriHost` (rev 3.9: the official check, not the hand-read global; `TauriHost` tested through `mockIPC`, Q07) (≈120 LOC: the four `invoke`s with today's validation, the window and dialog wrappers); `import.meta.env.DEV && VITE_OPENALPACA_HOST === "dev-server"` → `DevServerHost` (≈40 LOC + a ≈80-LOC Vite plugin, `apply: "serve"`, `configureServer`: `/__openalpaca/connection` and `/__openalpaca/log-tail?lines=` read `$OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE/state/discovery.json` server-side at request time with the shell's rules — expiry, a TCP probe — answer only `127.0.0.1`/`localhost` origins, spawn nothing, never run under `vite build` — *corrected, rev 3.13 (SF-21, DU-28): that origin rule pins no port. Vite's default `server.cors` admits localhost, `*.localhost`, 127.0.0.1 and `[::1]` on any port, and Vite installs it before `configureServer` runs. So the plugin is registered only when `VITE_OPENALPACA_HOST=dev-server`; it sets `server.cors: false`; it answers only an `Origin` that is exactly `http://localhost:1420` or `http://127.0.0.1:1420`; and it refuses unless `OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE` is set and absolute. There is no fallback to `home_root()`, which would serve the owner's real token. Both endpoints follow these rules*); `… === "fixture"` → `FixtureHost` (≈60 LOC; `{ connection, logTail, stopReport }`; `vitest.setup.ts` and `?host=fixture`); otherwise `NoHost`, every method rejecting with today's `ConnectionError`. `setHost(h)` for tests. `connection.ts` (369 lines) keeps the cache, listeners, stop intent and URL helpers; `reveal.ts` becomes a 15-line facade. Net ≈ +350 / −250 LOC in `src/`, ≈ −230 lines of mocks [NE: estimates]. **Why no token in the bundle**: the daemon mints a 32-byte token at every boot (`discovery/mod.rs:236-240`), writes it 0600 (`:120-124`), expires it after 24 h (`:239`), and every `/v1/*` call carries it (`http.ts:146-148`; SSE/WS `?token=`, `connection.ts:355`, `:363`); CORS is already `permissive()` (`router.rs:480`; `CorsLayer::permissive()` itself is at `:487` at `3598a6fb`. Whether this and the CSP's loopback wildcard stay as accepted residue or are narrowed once Q04 lands is owner decision D9, pending — C-24), so the only gap is how a page gets the current token — a dev-server-side read of the 0600 file at request time, never a build-time constant [C].

**G3-b — the compatibility contract** (DU-29). Today [C]: `discovery.json` carries `schema: 1`, `instance_id`, `pid`, `started_at`, `listen {host, port}`, `auth {token, issued_at, expires_at}`, `build {version: env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION") = "0.1.0", protocol: 1}` — the `1`s are literals at `discovery/mod.rs:226`, `:241`; `ConnectionInfo` (`:253-267`) keeps `base_url`, `token`, `instance_id` and drops the rest (TS mirrors it, `connection.ts:28-32`); `GET /v1/health` (`router.rs:16`, `:485-492`; `:492-505` at `3598a6fb`, where `health_json` is Q01's pinned body) returns `status`, `version`, `pid`, `instance_id`; `useHealth` polls it every 30 s and clears the client on an `instance_id` change (`hooks/useConnection.ts:64-73`, `:199`); the shell reuses any discovery whose `listen` accepts a TCP connect within 300 ms (`lib.rs:257-268`), even past the token's expiry (`:40-49`; T21) while `get_connection_info` refuses an expired one (`:31`); a spawn over a live daemon meets the singleton lock (`discovery/mod.rs:180-210`; `main.rs:118`) and exits. Proposed: `support::protocol::{PROTOCOL, OLDEST_SUPPORTED}` read by `make_discovery`, `/v1/health` (new `protocol` field) and the shell; `version`/`protocol` additive on `ConnectionInfo` (Rust + TS); liveness in two steps (TCP, then `GET /v1/health` with a 300 ms budget parsed for `instance_id` and `protocol`); `decide_reuse`, a pure function with the world injected (like `daemon_lifecycle::wait_for_exit_with`), with the five-step rule of DU-29 (a). *Rev 3.13 (C-09) adds a sixth case, **unresponsive**: the discovery pid is a live `openalpacad` (`daemon_lifecycle::running_daemon_pid()`, `crates/openalpaca_storage/src/daemon_lifecycle.rs:122`, which has no side effect) but health does not answer. The shell parks and retries, never spawns, and rotates no log; today a spawn there rotates the log under the live daemon's open fd and is then refused by the lock. `singleton_lock_is_free()` (`:135`) is never a first step, since it takes the lock and can make a booting daemon exit. An unparseable discovery file over a live pid is a mismatch candidate, not a spawn.* *Rev 3.14 (A-18, A-20; Z6-01, Z6-06): the sixth case is shown as "daemon is starting" and is bounded — the shell retries within a budget derived from a measured boot (Appendix B #51) and on timeout reports with the log tail — because the daemon writes `discovery.json` before its database, its MCP reconcile, its plugins and connectors are up, and 563 lines of `main.rs` before it serves (`apps/openalpacad/src/main.rs:222`, `:785`; DU-29's rev 3.14 bullet). A seventh row, the same protocol with a different `build.version`, reuses with a note naming both versions and Stop then Start, and never restarts the daemon.* `decide_reuse` returns a `Mismatch { daemon_version, daemon_protocol, shell_protocol, remedy }` rejection the TS side reads as `ConnectionError.kind = "mismatch"`, parked like `stopped_elsewhere` (`connection.ts:143`), **never** retry-spawned by `events.ts`'s ladder. Tests: table-driven over `decide_reuse` (compatible; older-supported; newer; missing `protocol` in health and in discovery; malformed discovery; stale pid with a dead port; a wrong server at the old address — health returns 404 HTML; an instance change between discovery and health; an expired token with a live daemon — **T21's current answer, pinned**; *rev 3.13, C-09:* a live daemon pid whose health does not answer — unresponsive, no spawn, no rotation; an unparseable discovery file over a live pid); the `/v1/health` JSON pinned in `apps/openalpacad`; TS: `connection.test.ts` (the `mismatch` kind, the optional fields), `events.test.ts` (no reconnect-spawn on mismatch), `useConnection.test.tsx` (the note); native: a shell against a daemon built with `PROTOCOL + 1` (a test-only env override — *rev 3.13, C-20: compiled only under `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]` or a test-only feature that every release build leaves off, so a release daemon cannot be told to report another protocol. The check runs on a debug daemon build, and the override's variable name appears in no release binary: `LC_ALL=C grep -c -a -F <name>` gives 0, G-ident's method*) shows the mismatch state and leaves the daemon running. `docs/api` regenerated.

**G2 — build provenance** (DU-30): §3.3's two defects and the fix of DU-30 (a): cargo decides freshness and names the artifact (`--message-format=json-render-diagnostics`, the `compiler-artifact` message for `openalpacad`'s bin target — right for `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, `[build] target-dir`, `--target`; never tauri-build's copy, because cargo writes the artifact before any GUI build script runs); `--reuse <path>` for dev only (*built without a path, rev 3.5's ruling 1: `--reuse` keeps what the script staged, checked against its `.sha256`, and `OPENALPACA_SIDECAR_REUSE=1` is the dev switch — rev 3.13, A1-15*); `.sha256` + `.provenance.json` before `tauri build`; `target/gui` by default; one canonical daemon-only build staged into both `libexec/` and the sidecar; `--frozen-lockfile` and `--locked` in the three scripts; the triple from `--target` when given, else `rustc -vV`, asserted equal to `tauri build`'s. Whether Bun's `execSync` buffers a 364-crate build's JSON without limits is untested — use `spawnSync` with a large `maxBuffer` or stream lines [NE]. **Gate** (in a temporary worktree, never over the main checkout's staged binary): a one-line source edit in `apps/openalpacad/src/args.rs` changes the staged hash; `touch` of the staged file does not skip cargo and the staged hash equals cargo's artifact; `--release` and debug stage different hashes; `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=/tmp/x` stages `/tmp/x/<profile>/openalpacad`; `--target <host>` stages `…/<triple>/<profile>/openalpacad`; after `cargo check -p openalpaca_gui` in the default target dir the staged file is unchanged and the next prepare still resolves cargo's artifact; bundled == staged `.sha256` == `libexec/`.

**The four dev loops** (G2's other half, from the 10-10 audit): React/CSS — Vite HMR with the shell and daemon running, no Rust compile (already true; `vite.config.ts:26-27` excludes `src-tauri` from the watch); browser UI study — the fixture host, or the dev-server host against an isolated development daemon; daemon logic — build and restart the development daemon alone, the GUI's `instance_id` invalidation doing the rest; native integration — build shell + support with `--reuse` of a compatible development sidecar; release — frozen JS install, locked Rust build with explicit target/profile, the verified daemon, the production assets, the bundle. `tauri.conf.json:7-9`'s `beforeBuildCommand`/`beforeDevCommand` couple the sidecar build to dev and build today [C]; `--reuse` is the dev-side decoupling.

### 7.3 The commit sequence (Q)

**Q00 — B0 baselines** (a measurement, not a commit): on the frozen application commit, before P01: the §7.5 protocol's scenarios on a release package built by E5's hand sequence and launched as §5.2 says (the app under `sandbox-exec -f scripts/eval/memory/loopback-only.sb`, the two-request proof, onboarding first); the shell's dependency graph (`cargo tree -p openalpaca_gui …`, today 245); the Rust and frontend baselines of G-base; the build-time scenarios. Recorded under `tasks/deps-upgrade/baselines/<date>/` (local, like this plan) and cited by every later Q commit's body. *Taken (rev 3.8):* `baselines/2026-10-12/README.md` — the GUI rows a person owes are listed there, under the launcher rev 3.8 recommends. *Rev 3.13 (MF-4, SF-5, C-25):* the launcher this entry first named (the app under `sandbox-exec`, §5.2's launcher (iii)) cannot carry a GUI row; Q00 found that itself. Under rev 3.8's launcher an in-app Start spawns an unsandboxed daemon. So the owed rows are either taken on `a91f4b23` under rev 3.8's launcher with §5.2's confined in-app Start, or taken later on a rebuilt `a91f4b23` bundle, or recorded as given up (§5.1 item 11; owner decision D13, pending). Scenario (7) owes no row: it is quit/relaunch, which Q00 measured (§7.5).

**Q04 — `feat(daemon,gui): the daemon compatibility contract — protocol constants, /v1/health.protocol, additive ConnectionInfo fields, decide_reuse`** · Recommended (DU-29 a)
- Rust: `support::protocol` (in storage until Q05 moves discovery; then in support), `make_discovery` reads it, `/v1/health` gains `protocol`, `ConnectionInfo` gains `version`/`protocol`, the shell's `ensure_daemon_running` goes through `decide_reuse` with the two-step probe (the hand-written GET under (a-i)), the `Mismatch` rejection. TS: `ConnectionInfo` fields, `ConnectionError.kind = "mismatch"`, the parked ladder, the Connection-panel note. **Docs, same commit** (rev 3.1): `docs/api` regenerated (`python3 scripts/gen_api_docs.py`; the health route); `apps/openalpaca-gui/API_MAP.md` — the `discovery.json` shape (`:39-57`, gains nothing but `build.protocol` reads from the constant), the two Tauri-command rows (`:63-64`, `decide_reuse` and the two-step probe), the `ConnectionInfo` interface (`:69-76`, the two additive fields) and the `/v1/health` line (`:100-101`, the `protocol` field) [C]. Gate: the table-driven tests of §7.2; `cargo test -p openalpacad routes::health`; the three TS suites; G-GUI; `gen_api_docs.py --check` clean; the native mismatch check against a `PROTOCOL + 1` daemon (E5-style, isolated). Back-out: revert; the fields are additive, so an older shell ignores them.
- **Rev 3.9.** The sidecar is located with `tauri::utils::platform::current_exe()` (Tauri's launch-time cached form) and refused in every profile when `<exe dir>/openalpacad` is absent — the debug-only `PATH` fallback (`src-tauri/src/lib.rs:287-307`) goes, for binary identity (G-ident: an installed release daemon instead of the staged one), not for the store, which the inherited environment decides either way; `current_exe()` errors on macOS when the path holds a symlink [D: `tauri-utils-2.10.1/src/platform.rs:108-121`], so the refusal sentence names that case. The health probe runs on `tokio::net::TcpStream` + `tokio::time::timeout` (Q12), and `Mismatch { daemon_version, daemon_protocol, shell_protocol, remedy }` is a variant of Q13's typed error enum, its `message` the sentence Q04 writes. Q11's suite carries `decide_reuse`'s table. Gate addition: the refusal sentence pinned; G-bundle leg B (the spawn, and the refusal with the sidecar removed).
- **Rev 3.13.** **(C-14, D7)** Which `current_exe()` locates the sidecar is owner decision **D7**, pending. Tauri's form refuses on macOS when **any ancestor directory** of the executable is a symlink, not only the executable itself [D: `tauri-utils-2.10.1/src/platform/starting_binary.rs:22-42`, `:66-83`; the doc comment at `platform.rs:116-118`]. That is a new refusal for the sidecar locator, and rev 3.9's citation (`platform.rs:108-121`) did not show it. Under D7's recommendation, the locator is `std::env::current_exe()` canonicalized, which meets G-ident's aim without the ancestor rule. Under the other answer, it is Tauri's form as rev 3.9 wrote: the refusal is recorded in the body and named in the refusal sentence, and Tauri's `process-relaunch-dangerous-allow-symlink-macos` feature (`tauri-2.12.3/Cargo.toml:116`; Tauri's own doc calls it highly discouraged) is named as its way out. Under either answer the debug `PATH` fallback goes, the absent-sidecar refusal holds in every profile (that is G-ident's, not D7's), and G-bundle leg B's symlinked-ancestor case asserts the answer taken. Until D7 is answered, rev 3.9's sentence above is a record, not an instruction. **(C-20)** The `PROTOCOL + 1` override is compiled only under `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]` or a test-only feature that every release build leaves off (§7.2 G3-b). `support::protocol` is written in storage here and moved by Q05, which re-points its callers (`make_discovery`, the health route, the shell) in the same commit; Q04 precedes Q05 in §7.7 and in `modernization-plan.md` §6.3. **(C-09)** `decide_reuse`'s table carries the sixth case, unresponsive (§7.2 G3-b). **(MF-4)** The native mismatch check runs under rev 3.8's launcher: the debug daemon with the override is started by hand under launcher (i), then the app runs unsandboxed. It runs in the bundle copy whose `Contents/MacOS/openalpacad` is §5.2's wrapper, so a shell that wrongly spawns instead of parking spawns a confined daemon. The pass condition includes `ps` showing no second `openalpacad`.
- **Rev 3.14 (the ZCode audit, A-18, A-19, A-20; Z6-01, Z6-06).** **(A-18, merged into C-09)** The unresponsive case is the GUI's **starting** state: a parked state the TS side names `starting`, the Connection panel reading "daemon is starting"; the shell retries `/v1/health` within the budget its body states (from a measured boot, Appendix B #51) and never spawns, rotates `daemon.log` or calls `singleton_lock_is_free()`; on timeout the error carries the log tail (Q13's `SpawnFailed { log_tail }` shape). Gate additions: `decide_reuse`'s table gains "live pid, health silent, then answering → reuse" and "live pid, health silent past the budget → the timeout report, no spawn"; natively, in the mismatch check's form (the rev 3.13 bullet above, MF-4): a debug daemon started by hand under launcher (i) with an `mcp.toml` naming a stdio stub that sleeps before it answers `initialize`, then the app run unsandboxed from the bundle copy whose `Contents/MacOS/openalpacad` is §5.2's wrapper, so that a shell that wrongly spawns instead of parking spawns a confined daemon: the window reads "daemon is starting" and then connects, `ps` shows one `openalpacad`, the hand-started pid, and `daemon.log` was not rotated. **(A-20)** The seventh row: same protocol, different `build.version` → reuse with a note naming both versions and Stop then Start, never a restart (`CLAUDE.md:76`); the row in the table and the note in `useConnection.test.tsx`. **(A-19, pending: *Discovery timing*)** If it is taken before this commit, its form rides here — `discovery.json` written just before `axum::serve`, a phase field with Q01's pinned literal moved in the same commit, or an early health route; until then Q04 is written under C-09's row. The installer's prompt (A-20's other half) is not this commit's.

**Q09 — `ci: native-smoke nightly and on release tags`** · Recommended (DU-31 a)
- Three jobs as §7.6 on `schedule` and `push: tags`; each OS's gaps labelled in the workflow's comments. **The daemon the job launches is a live daemon** (§5.2, rev 3.1): on `macos-latest` the app is launched under `sandbox-exec -f scripts/eval/memory/loopback-only.sb`; on Windows and Linux no equivalent is named [NE] — those jobs seed `llm.toml` with every provider off and credential discovery off, make no model turn (the smoke is launch → health → reuse → stop → log → Reveal), and the workflow's comment labels the gap. Gate: one green scheduled run per OS, or the failure recorded as the image's/platform's. Back-out: revert.
- **Rev 3.13 (C-17, C-03, C-25).** Under DU-33 (a) only the `macos-latest` job is written; the Windows and Linux jobs are deferred. That job's launcher is §5.2's launcher (iii), under which the webview never reaches its daemon (Q00). So the job proves: the spawn; `/v1/health` from outside; reuse after a quit and relaunch (closing the last window quits the app, C-25); the explicit stop with the lifecycle wait; and the failing start's log tail. A check that needs the webview connected, Reveal through a test page among them, runs under rev 3.8's launcher with §5.2's confined in-app Start, or is labelled a gap. Whether `sandbox-exec` runs on `macos-latest` at all is [NE] (Appendix B #48). If it does not, the job falls back to the Windows and Linux form above (every provider off, §5.2's pre-seed, no model turn), and its comment labels the gap.

So an `ensure_daemon_running` round trip with no live discovery file would reach `spawn_daemon()`, which could exec a real `setsid` daemon from `PATH` against whatever store the test's environment names. **Q12 — `fix(gui-shell): every command async; blocking work on spawn_blocking; the liveness probe on tokio`** · Recommended (rev 3.9) · **new** · before Q04
- `get_connection_info` and `read_daemon_log_tail` (a 64 KiB read) run on the UI thread today, and `ensure_daemon_running` blocks a runtime worker with file reads, a 300 ms connect per address and the spawn [Docs: v2.tauri.app/develop/calling-rust/ — "Commands without the async keyword are executed on the main thread"; C: `src-tauri/src/lib.rs:25-34`, `:101-106`, `:38-85`, `:254-268`]; every command becomes `async`, file and process work moves to `tauri::async_runtime::spawn_blocking`, the probe to `tokio::net::TcpStream` + `tokio::time::timeout`. Gate: Q11; G-GUI; G-bundle leg B (start) and leg A (stop). Back-out: revert.
- **Rev 3.13 (C-15, MF-4).** The shell takes `tokio.workspace = true` (`apps/openalpaca-gui/src-tauri/Cargo.toml:17`). The workspace's tokio features (`Cargo.toml:52-60`) include `time` but not `net`, so `tokio::net::TcpStream` does not compile in the shell. The shell's line becomes `tokio = { workspace = true, features = ["net"] }`, and the body records the `cargo tree -p openalpaca_gui -e features -i tokio` delta. This enables a feature of a crate already in the graph; it adds no dependency. If this commit exercises leg A's start, it goes through §5.2's confined in-app Start.

As built: a run is Tauri's hook only when `TAURI_ENV_TARGET_TRIPLE` and `TAURI_ENV_PLATFORM_VERSION` are both set (a partial set is refused as exported in the shell); the triple is Tauri's, and `rustc -vV` runs only to compare it with the host — on the host's triple cargo runs exactly as by hand, and **a non-host triple is passed to cargo as `--target`** (a cross build is built, not refused — expected to fail in rustc when the target is not installed, not shown); `TAURI_ENV_DEBUG` sets the profile, and a value other than `true` is refused; a `TAURI_ENV_DEBUG` leaked into a real `tauri build` cannot be told apart from the environment — the release scripts' by-hand first call, the bundle-vs-recorded-sha check and the provenance's `profile_from` are the protections; **`--reuse` refuses a stage whose provenance profile or triple differs from this run's**; `package.json`'s `prepare:sidecar:dev` is `prepare:sidecar` (one hook script for dev and build; `:release` kept for by-hand runs); the Linux and Windows scripts' comment on the hook corrected (comments only, DU-33). Gate run: the G2 items (a string edit changes the staged hash and its revert restores it; a `touch` skips nothing; `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` followed; a root check with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target/gui` leaves the stage alone), a real `tauri build --debug` (bundled == staged `.sha256` == cargo's debug artifact), a release hook build on the previous tree, the dry-run matrix (cross, partial sets, mismatches); not run: a real cross build (no target installed), `tauri dev` (it launches the app), the owner's packaging run.

### 7.4 G4–G6 — measure-first items (no commits scheduled)

- **G4 — streaming render.** Path [C]: each SSE `delta`/`reasoning` frame dispatches (`lib/chat-stream.ts:433-444`); the reducer appends (`:292-298`, `:281-287`); `useChatStream` dispatches into React state (`hooks/useChat.ts:195-214`, `:311-317`); `buildTranscript` is memoized on the whole `stream.state` among eleven inputs (`views/chat/useChatSession.ts:1098-1125`), so every delta rebuilds every item; `Transcript` maps all items (`Transcript.tsx:36-43`); `MessageBody` calls `parseProse(text)` on every render (`components/chat/MessageBody.tsx:117-124`); `UserMessage`/`AssistantMessage` are plain functions (`MessageRow.tsx:84`, `:144`). Change, when justified (rev 3.9: measured on the compiled build, `modernization-plan.md` M69): split the live row out of `buildTranscript`'s inputs; manual memoization only where the compiled build's profile shows work the compiler leaves; only then coalesce deltas to one flush per animation frame with a **mandatory flush** before `done`/`error`/`confirmation_requested` and on unmount, never delaying approval or control events. **Gate first**: a vitest harness replaying a recorded 2,000-delta stream into `useChatSession`, counting `parseProse` calls per history row (expect 1 after; ≈ deltas today) and `MessageRow` renders per row; a React Profiler trace of a 100-message conversation while streaming (commit count, render ms) — *rev 3.13 (C-16, SF-22): a release bundle cannot produce a Profiler trace without react-dom's profiling build, so the trace is taken on §7.5's instrumented build at baseline and after; the 100-message conversation is §7.5's seeded fixture*. Worth it = history-row renders per delta ≈ 0 **and** `done.content` and the event order byte-identical in the harness; a lost delta rejects the change.
- **G5 — the latest history page.** Path [C]: `useChatSession.ts:379-406` asks `GET /v1/chat/history?limit=100`, reads `total`, then asks `offset = total − 100`; the route (`routes/chat.rs:657-706`) → `ChatService::get_history` (`chat/service.rs:303-313`) → `list_by_session(limit, offset)` + `count_by_session` (`repository/conversation/mod.rs:197-202`). **The repository already has the tail**: `list_recent_by_session` (`:276-299`, DESC-then-ASC subselect) and `list_recent_by_lane` (`:246-271`), used by the context builder (`orchestrator/context_builder.rs:82-83`, `:248`). So G5 = `tail=true` (or `offset=-1`) on `GET /v1/chat/history` answered by `list_recent_by_session(limit)` with the same `total`, one request for the GUI and for the CLI's `chat --resume` (`apps/openalpaca/src/commands/chat.rs:270-324`, the same two calls); `(created_at, id)` is the sort key in both queries, so an append between two requests moves the tail and never duplicates within a page. **Gate first**: the probe→tail latency on a 500-message fixture session (*seeded by §7.5's deterministic seeder — rev 3.13, SF-22*) (two sequential local requests; the saving is one RTT plus one query, expected small); worth it if §7.5's "first useful content" on a long session improves measurably or the CLI shares the simplification. Tests: route tests for short (< limit), exact (= limit) and long sessions; a concurrent-append test (no missing or duplicate ids across the tail + one older page); `gen_api_docs.py --check`.
- **G6 — lazy previews, then maybe virtualization.** Path [C]: `App.tsx:51-55` lazy-loads the five views; `ChatView.tsx:57` imports `FilePanelSlot` statically, `:64` `WorkPaneSlot`; `FilePanelSlot.tsx:17-35` imports `ArtifactPreview`, `ArtifactDiffTab`, `HistoryTab`, the library preview helpers; `WorkPaneSlot.tsx:19` imports `WorkPane`. The build [A10: `gui-baseline-dist.json`]: `ChatView-*.js` 78,600 B importing `DiffView-*.js` 88,997 B (≈29 KB gzip) and `preview-*.js` 4,831 B; entry 395,009 B. Change: `lazy(() => import("./FilePanelSlot"))` behind the aside's open state with loading/error boundaries, same for `WorkPaneSlot`. **Gate first**: in the packaged webview, the bytes evaluated before first paint of the chat route and the time-to-interactive of the empty chat (*rev 3.13, C-16: Web Inspector figures need Tauri's `devtools` feature, which a release bundle does not enable, so they come from §7.5's instrumented build, at baseline and after*); worth it if the deferred chunk is not needed for the default screen (the aside is closed by default) **and** first open of a preview stays under a budget the baseline sets (e.g. ≤ 150 ms on the reference machine). **Virtualization only after** a React Profiler + DOM-node count on a 100-message fixture of long code/tables (*§7.5's instrumented build and seeded fixture, rev 3.13*) shows layout cost dominating; the acceptance list stands: scroll anchoring while streaming, selection/copy, find, keyboard navigation, approval focus. The 100-message tail and the 500-event ring bound two dimensions, not bytes, DOM nodes or cached sessions [A10].

### 7.5 G7 — the measurement protocol

**Fixture store**: a fresh isolated `OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE` under `$T` (never the owner's); `OPENALPACA_CONFIG_DIR=<store>/config` with `llm.toml` from `scripts/release/templates/config/llm.toml` plus credential discovery off; the daemon under `scripts/eval/memory/loopback-only.sb` from its first launch; onboarding finished first (one attended `update_persona` turn; `tasks/lessons.md`); then seeded through the HTTP API: one synthetic registered project (a scratch git repository), one 100-message conversation of long text/code/tables, one 20-note memory partition, five artifacts (markdown, image, a diff pair, …). Snapshot the store once and copy it for every trial. *Rev 3.13 (SF-22, C-06): the HTTP API cannot seed the conversations. No route writes a message without a model turn (`apps/openalpacad/src/router.rs:197-235`, read by the review), the deterministic commands write only short fixed rows, and Q00 built no such fixture; G4's 100-message and G5's 500-message fixtures rest on it. The seeder is deterministic, and it is one of two forms. **(s-1)** A canned OpenAI-compatible stub on loopback, the only enabled provider, answers each turn with recorded long text, code and tables; this also makes scenario (4)'s 2,000-token stream reproducible. An OpenAI-compatible provider needs a key, so the store's `llm.toml` carries a test value for it, never a real key, beside §5.2's pre-seed; a stub that answers Ollama's discovery routes needs none. **(s-2)** A test-only storage seeder runs before the daemon starts and writes the rows through `openalpaca_storage`'s repositories. Q10's record and the baseline it is compared with name the form used. The fixture is built once, archived beside the baseline (the repository keeps counts and hashes only), and copied for each trial.*

**Processes to count** [Docs/NE — nothing was launched]: the shell (`openalpaca_gui` on macOS) and its WebKit XPC helpers (`com.apple.WebKit.WebContent`, `.Networking`, `.GPU`, attributed as "responsible process"); `openalpacad` and its children (MCP servers, plugins — none in the fixture). Windows: `openalpaca-gui.exe` + the `msedgewebview2.exe` tree; Linux: the app + `WebKitWebProcess`/`WebKitNetworkProcess` (*the Windows and Linux rows are deferred under DU-33 (a) — rev 3.13, C-17*). Per process **and** a combined figure using the OS's non-double-counting measure — macOS `footprint`/`vmmap -summary` (phys_footprint), Linux PSS from `/proc/<pid>/smaps_rollup`, Windows private working set — never a sum of RSS across processes sharing pages. Local-model memory (Ollama) reported separately and excluded.

**Scenarios** (5 cold trials each; median and p95; the driver's OS clock): (1) cold launch, no daemon → first shell paint, first useful content (the transcript rendered), daemon-ready (`/v1/health` 200); (2) launch with the daemon running; (3) idle foreground 60 s and hidden/minimized 60 s → memory and CPU %; (4) one streamed 2,000-token answer → peak memory, dropped frames; (5) the 100-message conversation opened and scrolled; (6) three previews opened; (7) ten window close/reopen cycles with the daemon surviving (same `instance_id`; closing never stops it) (*rev 3.13, C-25: ten quit/relaunch cycles — the next paragraph*); (8) build-side: clean full build, warm full build, shell-only incremental (`touch src-tauri/src/lib.rs`), support-only incremental, daemon-only incremental, React HMR turnaround — each with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, profile, toolchain, hardware and cache state recorded.

**Launchers and the instrumented build** (rev 3.13; SF-22, C-04, C-16, C-25, MF-4). Each scenario names its launcher (§5.2). **(1)** Cold launch with no daemon: the app spawns its daemon, so the run uses the bundle copy whose `Contents/MacOS/openalpacad` is §5.2's wrapper, and confinement is proved after the spawn. The wrapper's own cost (one `sandbox-exec` exec before the daemon starts) is measured apart and reported beside the figure. Launcher (iii) gives the daemon-ready figure only, since its webview never connects (Q00). First paint and first useful content need the instrumented build below, or a person timing a screen recording (rev 3.14: once Q14 lands, Q14's startup marks, the Rules paragraph's rule (2)). **(2)–(6)** use rev 3.8's launcher: the daemon first under launcher (i), then the app unsandboxed with the same store variables. **(7)** is ten quit/relaunch cycles. Closing the last window quits the app, which installs no `RunEvent` handler (`apps/openalpaca-gui/src-tauri/src/lib.rs:416-427` at `3598a6fb`; [D: `tauri-runtime-wry-2.11.4/src/lib.rs:4310-4324`, read by the review]). Q00 already measured that (10/10 cycles, the daemon surviving), so no row is owed for it. **(8)** launches nothing. **The instrumented build.** Profiler traces and Web Inspector figures (G4, G6) cannot be taken on a release package. They come from a named build: a release bundle with Tauri's `devtools` feature and react-dom's profiling build aliased in. That build is used identically at baseline and after, its provenance (commit, features, the alias) is recorded in Q10, and its figures are never compared with a release package's. Q00's rows are release-package figures, so an instrumented row needs its own baseline on `a91f4b23`, taken with the rows §5.1 item 11 still owes or given up with them (owner decision D13, pending).

**Rules**: baseline on the same application commit before each architecture step; compare like with like (release package vs release package; instrumented build vs instrumented build — rev 3.13); **set budgets from the baseline** — this plan writes no MB/ms target.

*Rev 3.14 (the ZCode audit, A-26, A-25; Z6-04).* **(1)** Baseline and candidate alternate trial by trial — baseline, candidate, baseline, … — each trial on its own fresh copy of the one archived fixture, so a drift in the machine's state falls on both sides alike (ZCode's startup measurement ran five alternating trials per side on clones of one storage fixture, ZCode:docs/desktop/startup-critical-path.md:183-199); this refines SF-22's "copied for each trial". **(2)** Scenario (1)'s first paint and first useful content are read from the startup marks Q14 writes into `gui.log` — T0, the page-load marks, and the webview's own marks through the instrumented build — not from a person timing a screen recording. `a91f4b23`'s shell has no marks, so for Q00's owed rows the baseline side needs the same instrument: a measurement build of `a91f4b23` carrying Q14's marks, in the instrumented build's class (its provenance recorded, never compared with a release package's figures); otherwise those rows stay owner decision D13's, pending (§5.1 item 11). The audit's D13 recommendation is unchanged: do not hold the cut.

### 7.6 G8 — the native CI matrix

Today `ci.yml` excludes `openalpaca_gui` from Rust (`:27-31`), the `gui` job runs the four bun gates on ubuntu (`:37-58`), the only Windows job is Pester (`:64-78`) [C] (at `7295f91b`; the exclusion is at `:42-46`, the `gui` job at `:48-74` and the Pester job at `:82-98` at `3598a6fb`).

| Job | Runner | Steps | Sidecar | Wall-clock [NE, reasoned] |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `shell-check` ×3 (Q08) | `macos-latest`, `windows-latest`, `ubuntu-latest` (+ `libwebkit2gtk-4.1-dev libayatana-appindicator3-dev librsvg2-dev` [Docs: v2.tauri.app/start/prerequisites]) | `bun run build` (codegen needs `dist/`), `cargo check -p openalpaca_gui --locked`, `cargo clippy -p openalpaca_gui --all-targets --locked`, `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target/gui`; after Q06 `cargo test -p openalpaca_app_support` | an **empty placeholder** at `src-tauri/bin/openalpacad-<triple>` created by the job in the fresh checkout and deleted at the end (tauri-build errors when it is absent [D]); never where a real sidecar exists | cold 10–20 min (≈245 crates); warm 2–5 min |
| `native-smoke` ×3 (Q09) | the same three | `cargo build --release -p openalpacad --locked`, `prepare-sidecar.ts --release`, `tauri build` (`--bundles app` / `msi` / `appimage`), then the packaged app against an isolated store: launch → `/v1/health` → `instance_id` stable across close/reopen → explicit stop + the lifecycle wait → `daemon.log` tail after a failing start (`OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE` unwritable) → Reveal on a store file and a project file, refusals on a missing project and a symlink escape (through `reveal_path`, by a test page or `tauri-driver`; WebDriver on macOS is limited, so that job may assert launch/reuse/stop only [Docs]) | the real daemon | cold 25–45 min per OS (the 364-crate release build); warm 8–15 min |

Cost: macOS minutes bill 10× and Windows 2× on a private repository, free on a public one [Docs: GitHub Actions billing]; the repository's visibility is [NE]. Reasoned: `shell-check` ≈ 33 billed-minute-equivalents warm per PR on a private repo; `native-smoke` ≈ 490 cold / ≈ 150 warm — too much per PR, hence nightly and on tags. Gaps labelled per OS: WKWebView rendering is asserted by none of it; dialogs are not driveable headless.

*Rev 3.13 (C-17, C-25).* Under DU-33 (a) only the `macos-latest` row of each job is scheduled. The Windows and Linux runners, their packages and their gap labels are deferred with DU-33. The cost is therefore one macOS runner [NE, reasoned from the rows above]: `shell-check` warm 2–5 min, about 20–50 billed-minute equivalents per PR on a private repository at the 10× macOS rate; `native-smoke` cold 25–45 min (about 250–450) and warm 8–15 min (about 80–150), nightly and on tags. Both are free on a public repository. In `native-smoke`, close/reopen means quit/relaunch, since closing the last window quits the app (§7.5), and its launcher is Q09's.

*Rev 3.14 (the ZCode audit, A-22, A-23).* From Q06 on, `shell-check`'s macOS row runs Q08's G1 guard script (applied). A third row — `rust-macos`, the CI set's build, test and clippy on a pinned macOS label — is the pending *macOS CI job* (DU-31's rev 3.14 bullet) and is not scheduled; its cost would be the CI set's build and suite at the 10× macOS rate [NE].

### 7.7 Ordering with Part I

| Part II step | Depends on Part I? | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Q00 baselines | **no — and must precede both parts** | every comparison needs a same-architecture, same-dependency baseline; taken at the frozen application commit before P01 |
| Q01 pins, Q02/Q03 provenance | no | script and test work; Q02/Q03 make G-ident/E5/E7 mechanical, so land them **before P04** (DU-30 w-i) |
| Q04 contract | no | additive fields, independent of any bump |
| Q05/Q06 extraction | no for correctness; **yes for comparisons**: after Q06 the shell's dependency graph changes (storage leaves; sysinfo/chrono/file-lock arrive through support), so G-GUI for P04/P08/P31a (and P09/P10, rev 3.13, A1-10) is run against the architecture they land on and the shell baseline is **recaptured** after Q06 | P06's rusqlite stops reaching the shell after Q06; P08's sysinfo still does |
| Q07 HostBridge | no | TS only; one fixture host instead of 23 mocks for P31–P33's re-runs |
| Q08 shell-check | depends on P01 (SHA pins, `rust-toolchain.toml`), DU-22 | supersedes P01a/P40 (both deferred under DU-33; Q08 is its macOS job alone — rev 3.13, C-17) |
| Q09 native-smoke | depends on Q02/Q03 and P01 | — |
| Q19 size ratchet (rev 3.14, A-28) | no | needs Q10's figures for its budgets, so it follows Q10 |
| G4–G6 | no | need Q00's frontend profile first |

**Recommended order** (rev 3.9) — *superseded as the order of record by the next paragraph (rev 3.13, SF-16) and kept as rev 3.9's record; it lacks DU-M4's clean, M63 and Part III's places*: Q00 (taken) → P02 → Q01 → Q02 → Q03 (landed) → Q15 → Q16 → Q17 → the G-base driver amended (§5.1 item 6b) → **Part I in its phase order: P01, P01b (landed) → P42 → P03 → P31a (the Tauri pair) → Q11 → P04 … P38, with P28 → P41 in Phase H; the security-bearing P04, P15, P16 as early as the order allows** → Q12 → Q13 → Q14 → Q04 → Q05 → Q06 → recapture the shell baseline (G-GUI, `cargo tree`) → Q07 → Q08 (dropping P01a/P40 if they are still open) → Q09 → Q18 (once the owner states the floor) → Q10 → G4–G6 as Q10's profiles justify → E5/E7/E8 with the §5 identity check. `modernization-plan.md`'s Part III follows P38 (its rev 2.0 §6.3); its shell commits M72–M75 may land after P31a and Q11. Dependency groups are always compared against a baseline of the same architecture. **The alternative** (the architect's): Q04–Q06 before Part I, so the GUI's dependency bumps never reach the shell; it costs a re-baseline of the shell mid-wave and delays the five advisories by the extraction's review time — not recommended while lopdf 0.34.0 and rustls 0.23.43 are in the lock. *(Rev 3.13, B-09: the review's match against advisory-db `64f92cd` finds 14 RustSec vulnerabilities in HEAD's lock, not five, and the cut-day run reads them again, §2.11. The reason stands, with more advisories behind it.)*

**The one order, rev 3.13 (SF-16, E-08).** Rev 3.9's order above and `modernization-plan.md`'s rev 2.0 placed Part II and Part III against Part I separately and gave their interlocks pairwise. The one merged order for Parts I, II and III is published in `modernization-plan.md` §6.3, rev 2.2 (that plan's revision of 2026-10-17), and `tasks/todo.md` Chunk 25 copies it. This plan keeps no second copy that could drift; where this paragraph and §6.3 differ, §6.3 governs and the difference is a defect to fix in both. Part II's places in that order: Q01–Q03 and Q15–Q17 landed; **Q11** in its one place, right after P31a — P42 → P03, DU-M4's one `cargo clean` → P31a → Q11 → M63 → P04 … P38, Part I in §5.3's phase order, P19b pending owner decision D5 and P28/P41 waiting on the test-bot tokens (D10); after P38, M57 first, then **Q12 → Q13 → Q14 → Q04**, then M18 → **Q05 → Q06** → the shell baseline recaptured (G-GUI, `cargo tree`); then Part III's Rust series; then **Q07**, before Part III's GUI commits, so their tests run on `FixtureHost`; then the shell commits M72–M79, with E5's one Export click before M76; then **Q08** (its macOS job), **Q09** and **Q18a/Q18b** (once the floor is stated); then, after Part III's conditional M83/M84, M64 — after every commit written against `file:line` anchors, Part II's `lib.rs` ranges among them — M45–M53 and M65; then **Q10** → G4–G6 as Q10's profiles justify; then the E-gates E1–E8 at the very end (E6 at §5.1 item 3's consent point; E5 there but for its one Export click, taken before M76). Superseded by it: rev 3.9's "its shell commits M72–M75 may land after P31a and Q11" above. They follow P38, after Q12–Q14 and Q04, so that M74's command list and M76's typed errors are written on the final shell. §6.3 rev 2.2 names Q18 as one commit; this plan splits it into Q18a and Q18b (C-18), which take Q18's place together. *Rev 3.14:* §6.3 rev 2.3 names Q18a and Q18b (its step 13). §5.4's thirty take §6.3 rev 2.3's steps 1a, 2 (Phase G's head: N-MCP-1, N-MCP-3, N-MCP-4), 3a, 6b, 8 and 11; Part II's share is **N-GUI-1**, right after Q07 (step 11). **Q19** (A-28) lands right after Q10, before G4–G6: §6.3 rev 2.3's step 18 reads Q10 → Q19 → G4–G6, named there by the final consistency pass, which closed the difference this paragraph's rule would otherwise leave. *Rev 3.15 (D1 (a), D3 (a)):* P42 and every version-moving commit after it start at or after the cut instant, 2026-10-21T19:53Z. **P03b** (`build: Rust 1.99.1`) joins the order as the next commit from 2026-10-23T04:47:44Z, wherever the sequence then stands — after P03, among P31a…P41 — never before that instant and never folded into another commit, and always before P38, which records the toolchain (§5.3's P03b); a second `cargo clean` right after it is presented to the owner, not adopted — the owner's DU-M4 (a) is the one clean after P03 (`modernization-plan.md` §5.1's DU-M4, its rev 2.4). `modernization-plan.md` §6.3's step 2 leaves P03b's place to Part I and §6.4 counts it — the programme 42 + 91, 43 + 91 with P19b under D5 — in that plan's rev 2.4.

### 7.8 What Part II explicitly does not do

- No framework migration: not Electrobun, Wails, Electron; not Tauri 3 (alpha.4 only) and not CEF (a Chromium distribution against the packaging goal); the system webview stays, its rendering differences tested per OS.
- No RAM, startup or compile-time promise: every such claim is [NE] until Q00's baseline and Q10's recapture exist; crate counts are not runtime figures. *(Dated, rev 3.13: Q00's baseline exists since rev 3.8, so a claim waits for Q10's recapture.)*
- No new reader of `project.json` in the shell: Reveal keeps the one canonical reader (moved, not copied); webview-supplied roots are never authority.
- No token in a bundle, no loosening of CORS or path checks for browser development. *(Rev 3.13: DevServerHost's rules tighten rather than loosen, DU-28 (SF-21). Two things are pre-existing: the CSP's loopback wildcard in `connect-src` and `img-src` (`apps/openalpaca-gui/src-tauri/tauri.conf.json:24`), and the daemon's `CorsLayer::permissive()` (`apps/openalpacad/src/router.rs:487`), which lets any page read `/v1/health` cross-origin. Whether to accept them as residue or narrow them once Q04 lands is owner decision D9, pending (C-24); this rule does not decide it.)*
- No change to storage formats, migrations or the daemon's API beyond the additive `protocol` field; `test-home-guard` behaviour is not lost in the move. *(Rev 3.13: owner decision D8, pending — Reveal as a daemon route by file id, DU-27 (e) — would change this rule. It is not adopted here. Rev 3.14: so would the pending *Discovery timing* (A-19) — a phase field in `discovery.json` or an early health route — which is not adopted either.)*
- No G4–G6 commit before its gate.

## 9. Keeping current afterwards

Rev 2.1 §8 stands (monthly Dependabot with a 7-day cooldown; the weekly advisories jobs; semver-major bumps hand-planned one commit per crate with the format pin first; the toolchain moved deliberately every second or third stable, an exact x.y.z, `rust-version` with it; Bun bumped with `packageManager` and the local install together), with these changes:

- **Dependabot's bun updater is at Bun 1.4.2 and accepts `bun.lock` versions ≤ 3** (source level, since 2026-10-06) [Docs]. Under DU-13 (a) nothing watches the lock version any more; the first hosted run on the v2 lock is the one check (P35's gate). Under (b) the §8 checklist keeps watching `lockfileVersion`. *Rev 3.13 (SF-2, SF-29): that run happens only after the merge (Dependabot reads the default branch), and it can fail a second way — the updater's bundled Bun 1.4.2 against `packageManager` 1.4.3 (P42); a fallback to (b) needs a fresh Bun 1.3.x in its own `BUN_INSTALL` root (D12).*
- **The Tauri pairing rule, three guards** (rev 3.9): (1) the requirement strings in `[workspace.dependencies]` — `tauri = "~2.12"` and `tauri-build = "~2.7"` to the minor (tilde — rev 3.10 corrected rev 3.9's caret, which admits the next minor), `tauri-plugin-dialog = "=…"` exactly (P31a: the newest version both registries carry that is eligible, rev 3.13 — `"=2.8.3"` at the cut, rev 3.15) — stop a lockfile-only cargo PR or a plain `cargo update` from moving a Tauri minor or a plugin patch alone; (2) Dependabot's `ignore` for `tauri*`/`@tauri-apps/*` minors and for **every** update type of `tauri-plugin-*`/`@tauri-apps/plugin-*` (DU-15 (p-i), widened); (3) G-GUI and G-bundle on every pair commit. (p-ii) is withdrawn. A Tauri minor or a plugin patch is always a hand-made commit that moves both locks (`tauri` ↔ `@tauri-apps/api` same minor; each `tauri-plugin-*` = its `@tauri-apps/plugin-*` exactly; a crate-only patch, as 2.0.3–2.0.5 were, a Rust-only commit that says so), followed by an E5-style packaged check. The Tauri CLI's own check compares nothing under Bun [M-today] — an upstream tauri-cli issue is the way out (the owner's to file).
- **The per-PR checklist** (every dependency PR, Dependabot's included): (1) the data-path watch-list (P04) — read the changelog of every listed crate the PR moves; (2) G-GUI by hand (or Q08's `shell-check`) when the PR moves anything the shell links — `tauri*`, `wry`, `tao`, `windows*`, `sysinfo`, and until Q06 `openalpaca_storage`'s dependencies; (3) the format-pinning tests ran and passed: P05's AES fixture, the `sha256_hex("abc")` and the UUID partition-key literal, the owner-key and folder-suffix pins, the newer-than-build refusal (`database/tests.rs:68`), the serde-saphyr pin (P30), P14's characterisations, Q01's discovery byte pin — a failing pin is a format change, never a test to update in passing; (4) the Tauri pairing (above); (5) a packaged build gets G-ident — bundled == staged `.sha256` == `libexec/` (Q02/Q03), markers from the lock; (6) a `bun.lock` PR keeps the committed `lockfileVersion` (DU-13). *Rev 3.13:* (1)'s list carries the parsers the wave added (`cff-parser`, `typed-path`, `atoi_simd`, `fast-float2`; P04, G-07); (7) a hand-made lock move passes the freshness gate (§5.2) and its body names the new transitive majors it pulls in. *Rev 3.15:* (1)'s list gains aws-lc-sys (C9: 0.46.0 bundles AWS-LC 5.11.0, which rejects non-canonical Ed25519 public keys); and (7) holds for a JS lock too — `bunfig.toml`'s `minimumReleaseAge` counts from the install, so a `bun.lock` written for an instant other than its install's runs §5.2's `bun.lock` check against that instant's max date (C5).
- **Freshness.** A hand-planned bump takes the newest release ≥ 7 days old in both ecosystems. **Cargo 1.100 (2026-11-12) stabilises `registry.global-min-publish-age`** — the resolver form of this rule; adopt it in `.cargo/config.toml` once the pin is ≥ 1.100 (then P04-style `--precise` step-backs are no longer hand work). *Rev 3.13 (MF-2): until then `tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/gbase/lockdates.py --gate --max-date <UTC>` is the cargo half of the rule (§5.2); the Cargo 1.100 claims here were not re-read by the 2026-10-17 review (the Cargo Book changelog has moved) [NE].* Cargo 1.100 also changes the intermediate build-directory layout (Q02 reads cargo's JSON messages, so it is unaffected; anything else reading `target/<profile>` is) and makes `cargo install --locked` a no-op [Docs: the Cargo Book changelog].
- **Rev 3.9:** the freshness rule is applied at the cut by DU-23's minute table; a step-back is taken only for a release younger than 7 days, never to keep a measured older version (DU-25 (v4) took rmcp 3.5.1 on this rule). `build.build-dir` (stable since 1.91) is adopted by `modernization-plan.md` M63, with the shell's build-dir equal to its target-dir because tauri-build resolves its sidecar copy from `OUT_DIR`. *Rev 3.13:* **(MF-1)** the step-back rule binds Tauri's crates too — no hold except DU-23's freshness step-back, the override question being part of owner decision D1 (*rev 3.15: D1 (a) taken — moot at its instant, where the whole 2026-10-14 family is eligible; the rule stands as worded*); **(MF-5)** from M63 on every build that sets `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` sets `CARGO_BUILD_BUILD_DIR` beside it (§5.2), or the build-dir's tree in `~/.cargo` grows where deleting a scratch root never reaches.
- **The weekly advisories run** (P34): a red run on an unchanged lock is a new advisory — a lock refresh, a bump, or a reasoned `ignore` (`deny.toml`) / `--ignore=<CVE>` (`bun audit`). The one pre-recorded candidate: zip2's three `extract()`-path GHSAs against zip 8.6.0, lifted when calamine moves to zip 9. *Rev 3.13 (SF-7): live since RustSec's 2026-10-16 import — RUSTSEC-2026-0367, -0368, -0369 in `deny.toml` and cargo-audit's configuration (P34), lifted when calamine requires `zip ^9`; and rustls's RUSTSEC-2026-0346…-0349 only under D2's third branch, lifted by the follow-up that takes 0.23.46.* *Rev 3.15: that branch is void — D2 (a), P04 takes 0.23.46 — and the three zip ids are the only ignores (C6).* Once a year, read the transitive unmaintained list (4 warnings after the wave).
- **The Rust toolchain**: the procedure is P03's gate (clippy and fmt counts by the stated method against the previous toolchain, the full suite, G-GUI, the future-incompat list; the lock refresh after it). 1.100.0 is eligible from 2026-11-19. *Rev 3.13 (SF-8): 1.99.1 exists (2026-10-15/16); whether DU-2's "take an x.y.1" or DU-23's 7-day rule decides, and which publication time counts for a toolchain, is owner decision D3 (P03).* *Rev 3.15 — D3 (a), taken on 2026-10-17: DU-23 governs a toolchain as it governs a crate, and the clock rule for a toolchain is named: a Rust release is eligible 7 days after the later of its stable channel manifest (`static.rust-lang.org/dist/channel-rust-<x.y.z>.toml`) and its GitHub release — for 1.99.1, 2026-10-15T17:39:23Z and 2026-10-16T04:47:44Z, so from 2026-10-23T04:47:44Z, which is P03b. DU-2's "take an x.y.1" is read inside that rule, never ahead of it. The freshness gate reads crates.io only, so the check is made by hand and recorded in the commit's `Verified:` line (§5.2). 1.100.0's date above assumes both of its publication times fall on 2026-11-12; the same rule re-derives it from the two times once they exist.*
- **Bun and Node**: `packageManager` bumped with the local Bun (exactly that version); 1.4.3 is taken at the cut (P42, rev 3.9; its own one-line commit with the four gates re-run). Node stays with the runner image until a floor is threatened (today 22.23.3 default on 24.04, 24.21.0 on 26.04). *Rev 3.15:* the floor nearest to being threatened is jsdom 30.1.2's `engines.node`, `^22.22.2 || ^24.15.0 || >=26` (§6, *Runner images*).
- **Part II's own upkeep**: after any architecture step the shell's dependency baseline is recaptured before the next dependency comparison; `openalpaca_app_support` joins the guard audit; the `shell-check` jobs are the standing G-GUI; `native-smoke`'s labelled gaps are re-read when a platform tool (WebDriver on macOS) improves. *Rev 3.14 (the ZCode audit, A-22, A-28):* from Q06 on Q08's G1 guard script runs on every macOS job and by hand, and Q19's budgets are re-set from each recapture, never loosened in passing.
- **Owner settings, once**: Dependabot alerts on; local Bun at the pinned version.
